# Changelog

All notable changes to Objeck will be documented in this file.

## [v2026.10.2] - 2026-10-25

**Objeck gains a database in the standard library: `Query.Structured` is now a columnar engine with SQL joins, grouping, indexes and expressions over typed in-memory tables, files and URLs named in `FROM`, a crash-safe `.otab` file format, and sources that read CSV, JSON, XML, text logs, filesystems, zip and tar archives, Excel workbooks, ODBC databases, `System.ML` results and image detections as tables. The runtime collects in proportion to live data and allocates without searching, `Collection.Hash` is open-addressed with unboxed `IntVector`/`IntMap`/`BitSet` beside it, calls nest 16,384 deep, DTLS works for the first time, every socket failure says why, servers take IPv4 and IPv6 clients on every platform, and Windows handles non-ASCII arguments, environment variables, paths and console text. `System.ML` gains kernel and one-class SVMs, six outlier detectors and an autoencoder, a new `System.ML.Lab` for experiments, result tables and charts, and BERT sentence embeddings over ONNX; `Data.JSON` decodes escapes when it parses, so a document reads back as it was written; and the JIT compiles code it used to hand back to the interpreter and no longer loses a value at a loop's collection safepoint. Probing six libraries and the compiler fixed defects in CSV, JSON, regular expressions, NLP, ML, Ollama, OpenAI, Gemini, SDL2, ODBC, numeric literals, name resolution and array literals.**

> **Upgrading:** every `.obl` is a new format (magic `0xffb9`): rebuild any library of your own with this toolchain. The full list of behaviour changes is in the **Upgrading** section at the end of this entry; the ones most likely to touch a program are an `.obe` from an earlier release being refused (recompile), `Directory->List` no longer returning `.` and `..` on Windows, a leading-zero integer literal (`010`) being a compile error with `0o10` as octal, `Byte->CompressBr` renamed `CompressDeflate`, `Data.JSON` decoding escapes when it parses (stop calling `JsonElement->Decode`), `TF_IDF->New()` smoothing and normalizing by default, `Byte[]->ToHexString` returning a digest, `Hash` iteration order changing, and a call stack that defaults to 16,384 frames rather than 256.

### Query.Structured

- **A columnar engine, designed first and built in phases** ([#1295](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1295), [#1298](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1298), [#1301](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1301), [#1303](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1303), [#1325](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1325); design and as-built notes in `docs/QUERY_DESIGN.md`): every column is a typed array (INT, FLOAT, STRING, DATE, BOOL, OBJECT) with a null bitmap, text is dictionary-encoded, and each 65,536-row group carries min/max zone maps so a filter skips groups it cannot match. A query returns a `View`, a selection of row numbers with nothing copied; `Where`, `Select`, `OrderBy` and `Limit` chain on it. A `where` is compiled once into typed predicates (`Col("value")->Gt(0.5)->AndAlso(...)`), prepared statements take `?` placeholders, and a table caches 64 parsed plans. The SQL front end is a new recursive-descent parser with contextual keywords: `INNER`/`LEFT JOIN ... ON` through a `Catalog`, `GROUP BY` with `COUNT`/`SUM`/`AVG`/`MIN`/`MAX`/`MEDIAN`/`STDDEV` and `HAVING`, multi-key `ORDER BY ASC|DESC`, `LIMIT`/`OFFSET`, multi-column `DISTINCT`, `MATCHES` over `Query.RegEx`, and `INSERT`/`UPDATE`/`DELETE`/`CREATE TABLE` applied in full or not at all. The old API (`Table->New(name, String[])`, `Insert() ~ Row`, `Query(sql) ~ Table`, `FromCsv`, `FromFilesystem`, `Finder`) keeps its signatures and results. Measured at 100,000 rows on the Windows x64 development machine, against the v2026.10.1 library: `where value > 0.5` 0.558 s to 0.023 s, `order by` 3.16 s to 0.096 s, `Sum` 0.051 s to 0.004 s, 10,000 `Get(pk)` 0.032 s to 0.005 s; a join with a 5,000-row table 0.11 s, `GROUP BY` into 5,000 groups 0.11 s.
- **Files and URLs are tables** ([#1303](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1303), [#1366](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1366), [#1384](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1384), [#1391](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1391)): a quoted path in `FROM` or `JOIN` is read by its extension (`.csv`, `.tsv`, `.json`, `.jsonl`, `.xml`, `.otab`, each also `.gz`), `csv('data.txt', delimiter => ';', header => false)` and the other function forms set options, a glob reads several files as one table with a `_file` column, and `Catalog->Run(sql)` is the stateless one-off form. An `http://` or `https://` path is fetched through a download cache revalidated with `ETag` and `Last-Modified`. The cache is correct across processes that share the temporary folder: each URL has one folder of immutable versions, each written once under a name no other writer uses, and one small note naming the current version with its validators, published by a single atomic rename, so the validators a reader sends always belong to the copy it reads and a 304 means that copy is current. Before, a Windows writer could not replace a copy another process had open (the query failed with `cannot write its copy`), and two processes that downloaded different versions could leave one's copy beside the other's note, so an older file was served as current; superseded versions are deleted after a five-minute grace period with no lock file, and the old layout is not read, so the first fetch of each URL after upgrading downloads it once more. An archive's member or a workbook's sheet follows the path after a dot, as `schema.table` does: `select * from 'results.zip'.'2026/runs.csv'`, `from 'budget.xlsx'.'Q1 2026'`, `from 'runs/*.tar.gz'.'metrics.csv'`. Each catalog caches files by size and modification time, and a file read within a second of its last change is also held with a digest of its bytes, so one rewritten in the same second to the same size is read again (`File->ModifiedTime` has whole-second resolution, and a finer clock does not help because file systems stamp with a coarse tick); `Catalog->SetFileAccess(false)` refuses them all, which is what `TableServer` runs with.
- **Sources** ([#1298](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1298), [#1301](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1301), [#1303](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1303), [#1329](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1329), [#1331](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1331), [#1334](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1334), [#1350](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1350), [#1352](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1352)): a `Source` declares its tables and fills a catalog, with a `ScanHint` for the columns, filter and row limit a query needs. In `query.obl`: `CsvSource` (whole-column typing, RFC 4180 through `Data.CSV`), `FilesystemSource` (flat or recursive, with `_id`/`_parent_id`/`depth`), `ObjectSource<T>` (a `Vector` of the program's objects), `TextSource` (regular-expression capture groups become typed columns; multi-line records; unmatched lines counted), `JsonLinesSource`, `JsonSource` and `XmlSource` (nested repeated parts become child tables joined on `_parent_id = _id`), `HttpJsonSource` (records at a dotted path, paged by a next link or an offset), `ArchiveSource` (zip, tar and gzipped tar read from a file, URL or bytes, with every member in a readable format as a table; nothing is extracted, and `..`, absolute paths, duplicate or overlapping zip members, zip bombs and CRC mismatches are refused), and `XlsxSource` (each worksheet as a typed table: shared and inline strings, booleans, error values, dates in both the 1900 and 1904 systems with the Lotus leap-year serial undone, merged cells kept as Excel stores them or filled with `SetFillMerged`). In their own libraries, so a program that only queries files loads no native module: `query_ml.obl`'s `MlResultSource` (folds, out-of-fold predictions, threshold sweeps, confusion matrices, bootstrap intervals and per-group recall from `System.ML`, with `MlColumns` as the bridge back to a matrix), `query_odbc.obl`'s `OdbcSource` (a table or query from any ODBC database, typed from the driver's description, with columns, a row limit and a `SetWhere` condition pushed into the SQL it sends, names always quoted and values always bound), and `query_vision.obl`'s `VisionSource` (a folder of images and a detector's boxes as `images` and `detections` tables, with image headers read in pure Objeck and `YoloDetector` over `API.Onnx`).
- **The `.otab` file** ([#1315](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1315), [#1321](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1321), [#1365](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1365); byte-exact specification in `docs/OTAB_FORMAT.md`, an independent reader in `tools/otab/otab_dump.py`): `table->Save(path[, compression])`, `Table->Load`, `ToBytes`/`FromBytes`, and `Append`, which adds row groups without rewriting the existing ones. Every block and the footer carry a CRC32, a save writes `path.part` and renames it into place, and `OtabFile` reads only the footer and then only the columns a `Read` names, over HTTP range requests as well as from disk. `Compression->FAST`/`SMALL` are zlib levels 1 and 9 per block, stored raw where compressing would not shrink. A table opened with `TableStore->New(path)` or `Table->Open(path)` is durable: every statement is appended to a change log before the call returns, a torn last record is dropped while a damaged one refuses the open with its offset, `Checkpoint()` folds the log into a save, and a writer lock holding the process id refuses a second writer and clears a dead one's. Index definitions are kept in format 2, written only for a table that has indexes, so a table without them is byte for byte what v2026.10.1 wrote.
- **Hash and sorted indexes, and a planner that shows its work** ([#1351](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1351), [#1364](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1364)): `table->CreateIndex(column, IndexKind->HASH | SORTED)`, `DropIndex`, and in SQL `CREATE INDEX [name] ON t (col) [USING HASH | SORTED]` and `DROP INDEX`. A hash index serves `=` and `IN`; a sorted index serves ranges, `BETWEEN`, `ORDER BY`, `GROUP BY` on one table and `Min`/`Max`. An index lookup returns candidate slots and the whole condition is then tested on them in slot order, so results and their order are a scan's by construction, which a differential test holds it to. `View->GetPlan()`, `Statement->Explain()`, `Table->Explain(sql)` and `Catalog->Explain(sql)` report steps such as `where runs: HASH index runs_grp on grp (=)`. At 1,000,000 rows (Windows x64, `-opt s3`), equality on an Int 73 ms to 0.02 ms, a 0.1% range 109 ms to 0.7 ms, `ORDER BY value LIMIT 10` 2.8 s to 0.08 s; building an index takes 0.2 to 0.6 s. Several threads may query one table at once, including right after a write: a stale index, zone map or plan cache is rebuilt under one process-wide lock taken only on that slow path.
- **SQL expressions in every clause** ([#1367](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1367), [#1398](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1398), [#1416](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1416), [#1417](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1417); design in `docs/QUERY_DESIGN.md`, [#1395](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1395)): `+ - * / %`, unary minus and parentheses over columns, numbers, text, dates, `TRUE`/`FALSE`, `NULL` and `?` placeholders work in the select list, in `WHERE` (on either side of every test, in `IN` lists and `BETWEEN` bounds, and in `UPDATE` and `DELETE` conditions), `ORDER BY`, `GROUP BY` and `HAVING`, inside aggregates (`sum(price * qty)`), over aggregates (`sum(a) / count(*)`, `max(x) - min(x)`) and in `JOIN ... ON` (`on t.n + 1 = u.w`, a range join with `between`, a key plus another test, `OR`, a join over two earlier tables). A parsed expression is never changed, so cached plans stay shareable between threads; it is bound to a table once per run, constants are folded, and it is evaluated 1,024 rows at a time into typed arrays with no per-row allocation. A column tested against a constant is still reduced to the ordinary filter, so zone maps and hash and sorted indexes serve it as before, and a group or join key that is an expression is computed into a column. `/` always gives a Float; an empty cell, a non-numeric cell, a zero divisor or an Int overflow gives an empty cell rather than stopping the program, and `x % -1` is 0. Refused with a message of their own: an aggregate inside an aggregate, an aggregate in `GROUP BY` or `ON`, a `GROUP BY` entry that reads no column (`group by 1`), and an `ON` that names a table joined later. At 1,000,000 rows (Windows x64, `-opt s3`, best of three), `select price * qty` went from 585 ms to 146 ms and `where price * qty > 100` takes 29 ms (it was a syntax error); a join of 200,000 rows to 1,000 takes 29 ms on `t.n = u.w` and 62 ms on `t.n + 1 = u.w + 1`. A differential test holds every form to a row-at-a-time evaluator written from the design's rules, over random tables with NaN, infinities, extreme Ints, empty cells and deleted rows, each also on an indexed twin (9,672 queries over 60 seeds).
- **Bridges and serving** ([#1325](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1325)): `ToCsv`, `WriteCsv`, `ToJson`, `WriteJson` and `ToMatrix(names[, fill])` on both `View` and `Table`, with `Table->FromCsvText`, `FromJson` and `FromMatrix`; numbers round-trip exactly through `Float->ToShortString`. `TableServer`, in `query_server.obl` so `query.obl` does not pull in `Web.HTTP.Server`, answers `GET /tables`, `GET /tables/NAME` and `GET`/`POST /query` as CSV, JSON or `.otab`, refuses anything but a `SELECT` (403), any query naming a file or URL (403) and any result over its row limit (413). `RemoteSource` queries a published table as a local one, sending its columns, limit and a `SetWhere` condition so only matching rows cross the network.
- **Safe for threads** ([#1378](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1378), [#1389](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1389)): `Table->GetError` and `Catalog->GetError` answer the calling thread's own last error, through two new runtime functions, `Thread->GetCurrentId()` and `Thread->IsAlive(id)`. A `Catalog` can be shared: its table map, plan cache and file cache are locked only for a map operation, sixteen file locks give single-flight loading so threads that miss together read a file once, and `CREATE TABLE` by several threads at once lets exactly one succeed. Writes to a table are still the program's to order. `TableServer` answers requests at once: it held one mutex for each request from parsing to the last byte sent, which only serialized reads and never guarded the program's own writes. Each request now holds a read share of a writer-preferring lock, `TableServer->BeginWrite()` and `EndWrite()` bracket the program's changes to published tables (DML, indexes, columns, `Compact`) and keep requests away from them, and `Publish` and `Unpublish` no longer lock. Eight clients sending one `GROUP BY` over 20,000 rows went from 48 to 112 requests a second (Windows, 24 logical processors); with the program's writes inside that pair, no request reads a table half changed (in the new test, 18 to 136 reads a run saw a change half done before). Writes over the wire were and are refused with 403.
- **Correctness first** ([#1291](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1291), before the redesign): `Average` and `Sum` counted string and Nil cells, `order by` through a `Map` keyed on the value lost rows with equal values, `where` comparing an int column with a float literal aborted the VM, `and` did not bind tighter than `or`, `order by` after `where` was ignored, `LIKE` compiled a regex per row, and `Delete` of the only row crashed. All fixed, with `Min`, `Max` and `Count(name)` added.

### Runtime

- **Allocation no longer searches a free list** ([#1308](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1308)): the old generation's free-block cache kept one list per power of two holding blocks of any size in that range, so after a major collection freed thousands of small blocks every allocation of a slightly larger size walked the whole list and then allocated fresh anyway. Each list now holds one size class, four per power of two, so a cache hit is one pop. The first read of a 4,000-row text column went from 0.96-1.03 s to 0.023 s, and building 16,000 rows from 2.06 s to 0.20 s (Windows x64). A stack sampler had put every sample in `MemoryManager::GetMemory`.
- **The major-collection threshold follows the live heap** ([#1311](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1311)): it started at 8 MB and grew only after eight majors in a row freed nothing, so a program keeping a table or a model past 8 MB ran a full collection on nearly every array allocation. After each major the next threshold is twice the live heap, never below 8 MB. Medians of three runs on Windows x64: binarytrees 18 from 13.9 s and 36 majors to 10.0 s and 10, peak memory 353 MB to 273 MB; a 70,000-row `.otab` probe from 2.97 s to 2.09 s. A given `--gc-threshold` is a trigger that scales with reality ([#1383](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1383)): it holds exactly as given while a major collection leaves at least a quarter of it free, so `--gc-threshold=64k` still collects about every 64 KB of allocation for a program with little live data, which the nightly stress run relies on; while more than three quarters of it survive, the next major waits until the heap reaches twice the live size; and the given value returns once the live heap fits under it again. Before that, a threshold below the live heap collected on every allocation: a program keeping 4,000 objects under `--gc-threshold=64k` took 23,539 major collections and 11-18 s, and now takes 8 and 0.1 s (Windows x64). `OBJECK_GC_TRACE` reports, once per run, the first time a program outgrows its threshold; `docs/cli_options.md` states the semantics.
- **Collections** ([#1341](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1341)): `Collection.Hash` was a chained table whose buckets were `CompareList`s with built-in cursors, about four objects per entry, resizing in fixed steps that stopped at 196,613 buckets, and keys of different classes that collided aborted the VM with an invalid cast. It is now linear probing over parallel key, value and hash arrays, doubling at 70% load with no limit, deleting by backward shift, and comparing keys only when they share a class. `Cache` found and reordered its LRU list with a linear search on every call and evicted a different entry when a key it already held was updated; it now keeps a `Hash` of nodes in a doubly linked list. New unboxed classes in `Collection`: `IntVector`, `FloatVector`, `IntMap`, `IntSet` and `BitSet`. Measured on Windows x64 (`--stat min`, five runs, about one million operations a row): `hash_int_insert` at 1M 2.506 s to 0.424 s, `hash_str_find` at 1M 2.056 s to 0.401 s; appending 1M to an `IntVector` 0.058 s against 0.933 s for a `Vector<IntRef>`. On macOS the collector's old-generation pointer set clustered under the nano allocator's power-of-two stride (one major collection took 29.8 s on the hosted runner, every sample in `PtrSet::insert`); each 64 KB address window now gets a pseudo-random offset, mean probe distance 613,504 to 0.50 in the model, `collect_hash_open` about 6 s to 3.7 s on Windows at a cost of about 2% on binarytrees. `perf-results/libs/run_lib_bench.py` runs the library benchmarks against a baseline.
- **Runtime batch** ([#1304](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1304)): `Int`, `Float`, `Byte` and `Char` get bulk `Copy(dest, dest_offset, src, src_offset, count)` and `Fill`, native traps 4-14x faster than an element loop that raise `IndexError` on a bad range; `Float->ToShortString` gives the shortest text that reads back as the same value and `JsonElement->New(Float)` writes it, so JSON carries `0.5` rather than `0.500000`; `"1e400"->ToFloat()` gives infinity instead of stopping the VM, and the interpreter and JIT share one parser (the JIT's ignored a signed hex prefix); `Serializer->Write(Bool[])` over-read 8 bytes per 1-byte element and `ReadBoolArray` returned an Int array posing as `Bool[]`; `Int`/`Float->ToBytes` and `Byte->ToInts`/`ToFloats` convert whole arrays little-endian; `Serializer->Serialize` compressed at zlib level 9 (5 s for 32 MB) and now uses level 1 (0.23 s, 14% larger), with `CompressZlib(input, level)` for a choice.
- **Compression** ([#1296](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1296), [#1314](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1314), [#1350](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1350)): `"hello"->ToByteArray()->CompressGzip()` ended the program, because the buffer was sized for zlib's 6-byte wrapper rather than gzip's 18; the decompressors returned partial output as success at their 512 MB limit and leaked stream state on every error path; the six compression traps now report failure through a recoverable error. `Byte->CompressBr`/`UncompressBr` were documented as Brotli and were raw deflate (zlib with `windowBits -15`); they are now `CompressDeflate`/`UncompressDeflate`, in the same trap positions so compiled programs keep running, and `Web.HTTP.Server`'s `Compression->BR`, which sent that raw deflate as `Content-Encoding: br` to clients that could not read it, is removed. `UncompressDeflate(input, limit)` and `UncompressGzip(input, limit)` never grow past `limit + 1` bytes, which is what lets the archive reader refuse a zip bomb after producing at most the size the member claims.
- **Files and processes** ([#1297](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1297), [#1307](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1307)): `File->Rename` replaces an existing target atomically on Windows (`MoveFileExW`), as POSIX `rename` always did, so write-beside-then-rename saves work there; `File->CreateNew` (exclusive create), `Runtime->GetProcessId` and `Runtime->IsProcessAlive` are new, for the `.otab` writer lock.
- **Number parsing refuses bad text instead of throwing** ([#1310](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1310)): every throwing `std::sto*` in the VM, compiler, debugger and REPL was audited. `Runtime->SetProperty("float:string:precision", "x")` ended the program at the next `Float->ToString` with `internal error: invalid stoll argument`; it is now a recoverable error and the property keeps its value. A non-numeric HTTP/2 `:status` threw through C frames; `"{$f:.99999999999}"` was an internal compiler error with no position and is now a compile error; a bad DAP `Content-Length` ended obd with an exception; `/g 99999999999` exited the REPL; `--jit=4294967297` ran as `--jit=1` and is now a usage error.
- **Interpolating a Nil prints `Nil`** ([#1306](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1306)): `"x={$s}"` with a Nil `String` or object stopped the program inside `String->Append`; a missing value is often what a message is about, so it now reads `x=Nil`. `String->Append(Nil)` and `+` with a Nil operand still raise.
- **Memory and thread hazards found by the heap verifier and CI** ([#1388](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1388), [#1390](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1390), [#1392](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1392), [#1393](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1393)): on POSIX every broken-down time was read from the one static buffer `localtime()` and `gmtime()` share, so two threads calling `Date->New()`, `AddDays` or `File->ModifiedTime` at once read each other's fields (a catalog stamping files from six threads read some twice), and on Windows the function returned the address of a local; each caller now owns its buffer. Objects the traps build (a reflection call's `Class`, `Method` and `DataType`, a `CommandOutput`, the socket an accept returns, a recovered `Error`) were stored into with a plain C++ store, so when the nursery was full and the holder landed in the old generation nothing recorded its young references and the next minor collection left it pointing at the old copy, an intermittent wrong method name, socket address or error message; the stores now run the write barrier. A `Float[]` field read back by `System.Deserializer` was typed `Int[]` to the VM. On Linux with transparent huge pages set to `always` (the default in WSL and on many distributions) an idle VM thread held up to 2 MB resident, because the kernel backed its stack with a huge page at the first touch; thread stacks and call stacks are now mapped with `MADV_NOHUGEPAGE` and a guard page, and 100 idle threads cost 7.9 MB rather than 37.0 MB (WSL2, medians of 3).
- **An `.obe` from another toolchain version says what to do** ([#1387](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1387)): the loader refused it with 'appears to be invalid or compiled with an incompatible version of the tool chain', which named neither version, and a VS Code user whose extension shipped an older `objeck_lsp.obe` than the `obr` it started saw exactly that. It now names the file and both toolchain versions, says which side is older and to recompile with this `obc` (or to upgrade the runtime when the program is the newer); `obd` shares the loader, and running an `.obl` as a program says the file is a library and how to use it. `check_obe_version_mismatch.py` patches a probe's version one down and one up on every CI leg.

### Concurrency and the call stack

- **Calls nest 16,384 frames deep, and a full stack is recoverable** ([#1377](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1377)): `CALL_STACK_SIZE 256`, unchanged since commit c2208e3a0d, ended the shipped Rosetta example `man_or_boy.obs` with `call stack bounds have been exceeded` and any recursive-descent parser past about 300 levels. Raising the constant alone would have broken what it quietly protected: an operand stack of 768 words that was never bounds-checked, compiled frames on a 1 MB Windows thread stack, and a `CallCompiled` path that printed and exited so compiled recursion could not be recovered by `try`/`on` or `?->`. A per-thread `CallStack` now reserves the frame array and operand stack for the maximum and commits on demand, so the array never moves and the JIT's cached frame slots stay valid; `--call-depth=<frames>` and `OBJECK_CALL_DEPTH` take 256 to 1,048,576 with a default of 16,384; `PushFrame` checks the committed depth and that 1,024 operand words remain; and the compiled call site compares the native stack pointer against a per-thread floor, so running out of native stack is the same `RuntimeError`, recoverable compiled or interpreted. `obr` and `obi` reserve 32 MB for the main thread and VM threads 16 MB on Windows (8 MB on POSIX, scaled only above the default depth, since transparent huge pages made larger thread stacks cost resident memory on Linux). An unhandled error's listing shows the innermost 40 and outermost 10 frames. CLBG benchmarks moved within run-to-run noise (plus or minus 2%); per extra thread at 1,000 idle threads, 93.7 KB to 94.4 KB on Windows.
- **Blocking I/O parks for the collector** ([#1300](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1300), [#1302](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1302), [#1317](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1317), [#1319](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1319), [#1326](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1326)): the collector is stop-the-world with cooperative safepoints, and a thread blocked in TLS, HTTP/2, HTTP/3, DTLS, UDP, a named pipe, console input, `Runtime->Command` or a DNS lookup held up every collection in the process; when the thing it waited for was another thread of the same VM that needed a collection first, neither moved again. Every such trap now parks, re-rooting what it still needs on the operand stack and copying through C buffers, because the collector can move a parked thread's young objects. Native libraries could not park at all: `VMContext` gains `begin_blocking`/`end_blocking` and `lib_api.h` an `APITools_Parked` scope guard, with the ODBC (`SQLConnect`, `SQLExecute`, `SQLFetch`, ...), SDL (`SDL_WaitEvent`, `SDL_Delay`, PCM loops) and OpenCV (`VideoCapture`, `waitKey`) waits bracketed by it; a library built against the old header keeps working unparked, and one built against this header runs on an older VM. A TLS reconnect whose new connect failed left a freed context in the instance and was fixed on the way.
- **TCP and TLS accepted sockets shared one class-id cache** ([#1320](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1320)): whichever kind of server accepted first fixed the class of every socket accepted after it, so a process serving HTTP and HTTPS side by side handed a `TCPSecureSocket` to the plain server's callers, and a TLS accept that got the four-field `TCPSocket` class wrote its address and port past the object's end, where the collector never traced the string. An audit of every trap that stores into an instance after allocating found no further hazard: no allocation inside a VM trap can start a collection, and only an explicit park can.
- **Calls back into Objeck from C++ run on the thread's checked call stack** ([#1386](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1386)): `APITools_MethodCall`, which a native library's `APITools_CallMethod` and `Class->Instance` reach, built a fresh interpreter and call stack for every call, so none of the bounds of [#1377](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1377) applied, and a recursion through a callback (native code calling an Objeck method that calls it again, or a constructor making another of its class by name) ended in 0xC00000FD with no output on Windows, even inside `try`. A callback now shares the thread's call stack and operand stack, checks the native stack at each level (about 7.5 KB a level, compiled), and under a `Try()` beneath the native call records the error and raises it when the native function returns; a thread the VM did not start has no call stack to share and keeps its own.

### Networking

- **DTLS works for the first time** ([#1318](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1318), [#1333](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1333), [#1376](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1376)): the parser accepted all 18 `SOCK_DTLS_*` directives and the emitter had a case for none of them and no default, so every `DTLSSocket`/`DTLSSocketServer` method compiled to nothing and returned whatever was on the operand stack; on Windows `Listen` crashed the VM. With the cases emitted (and a `default` that stops the build on any directive the emitter forgets), running the handlers found that accept never completed a handshake (the cookie's transport id was never set), `ReadBuffer` waited to fill a buffer a datagram never fills, a second client was never served, and the server listened on one address family chosen by the platform. `DTLSSocketServer->New(cert, key, passwd, address, port)` binds one address and keeps to it across the re-bind each accept performs, and a write after the peer has gone fails with the peer's `close_notify` or the OS reason rather than returning true forever.
- **Servers take IPv4 and IPv6 clients on every platform, or one address** ([#1322](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1322), [#1326](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1326), [#1373](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1373)): on Windows an HTTPS server refused every connection to 127.0.0.1 while its HTTP sibling refused every connection to ::1, and on Linux both were IPv4 only. Every wildcard server now binds `[::]` with `IPV6_V6ONLY` off, falling back to `0.0.0.0` on a machine with no IPv6 stack, and reports an IPv4 client as `a.b.c.d`. `TCPSocketServer->New(address, port)`, `TCPSecureSocketServer->New(cert, key, passwd, address, port)`, `UDPSocketServer->New(address, port)` and `WebServer->Serve(callback, address, port)` bind one address, with `"localhost"` always 127.0.0.1; a bad address leaves `Listen()` false with the reason in `GetLastError()`. `UDPSocket->New` resolves names and IPv6 through `getaddrinfo`, tries each address its name resolves to and moves on when one is refused, and a Windows UDP server no longer fails its next read because a reply to a departed client drew an ICMP port-unreachable.
- **A server bound to port 0 reports its port** ([#1372](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1372), [#1380](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1380)): `TCPSocketServer`, `TCPSecureSocketServer`, `DTLSSocketServer` and `UDPSocketServer` gain `GetPort()`, `WebServer->GetBoundPort()` is the real port once `Serve` is listening, and `McpServer->Serve` accepts an `"http_port"` of 0 with `McpServer->GetBoundPort()` reporting it. Thirty-two regression tests that listened on fixed or randomly picked ports, wrote fixture files of fixed names or shared the system temporary folder now use port 0, per-process names and private folders; before, two suites on one machine collided in 14 of 31 server tests on both Windows and Linux.
- **Every socket failure says why** ([#1348](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1348), [#1349](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1349), [#1371](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1371), [#1373](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1373)): a `TCPSocketServer` on a taken port reported `GetLastError()` as "The operation completed successfully." on Windows, because the error was read when `GetLastError` ran rather than when the bind failed and a successful `closesocket()` had reset it; worse, `SO_REUSEADDR` let a second Windows server bind and listen on a port a server already held, so a `WebServer` on a taken port came up and served nothing. `TCPSecureSocket->GetLastError()` always returned Nil, so a refused certificate, an absent server and a missing key file looked the same. Every plain, TLS and DTLS call now records its own outcome per thread as it happens, `GetLastError()` is Nil when the thread's latest call worked, a connect that fails at every address reports the most informative reason (an answer from the network beats a route that never left the host), a verification failure lists mbedTLS's reasons ("The certificate is not correctly signed by the trusted CA"), and `TCPSecureSocket->WriteByte`/`DTLSSocket->WriteByte` return whether the byte was written rather than always true. A write to a peer that hung up fails with "Broken pipe" instead of killing the process, because `SIGPIPE` is ignored from VM start on POSIX; `Winsock` is started once for every socket, so in-process VMs (obi, embedding hosts) can open plain TCP sockets on Windows. A Windows connect timeout waited only on the write set while Winsock reports a failed connect in the except set, so a refused connect sat out the whole timeout.
- **TLS verification, said once and done the same everywhere** ([#1323](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1323), [#1335](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1335), [#1349](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1349)): on Linux only, a client connecting by IP address refused a certificate that named that address in an iPAddress SAN, because the distribution's mbedTLS 2.28 compares an address only against DNS names and the CN; a verify callback now reads the iPAddress entries from the raw extensions and clears only the name-mismatch flag, only when the address is listed, and does nothing with mbedTLS 3.5 or later. `TCPSecureSocketServer->GetIssuer`/`GetSubject` read the server's configuration as if it were a session and returned Nil; a server now reports its own certificate and a socket its peer's. `DTLSSocket->New(..., verify := false)` was documented as allowing self-signed certificates and never did; the doc now says what it does (verification unless the process is in insecure test mode, which `verify=true` overrides), and `TCPSecureSocket->SetVerifyPeer` no longer changes the mode of a connection that was already verified.
- **HTTP** ([#1300](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1300), [#1328](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1328), [#1340](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1340)): `Web.HTTP.Server` wrote a handler's headers and body only for 200, a few codes went out bare, and every other code was sent as 400, so a handler could not send 201, 204, 301, 304, 401 or 403 and `SetCodeContent(404, "...")` lost its body; both `ProcessResponse` copies now share one formatter for any code from 100 to 599, with the RFC 9110 reason phrase and no body on 1xx, 204 and 304, and the clients read no body on those codes rather than waiting out a 30 s timeout. `Url->Encode` passed non-ASCII text through raw (an invalid URL) and `Decode` turned `caf%C3%A9` into `cafÃ©`; both are now UTF-8, with `EncodePath`/`DecodePath` keeping `/` and a literal `+`. Query parameters split at the first `=`, so `eq=x=y` and a bare `flag` are kept, through a public `Request->ParseQuery`.

### Windows and Unicode

- **Non-ASCII text everywhere on Windows** ([#1313](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1313), [#1327](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1327), [#1357](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1357), [#1362](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1362)): `données.txt` was created on disk as `donnÃ©es.txt`, and a file another program had named `données.txt` could not be found, because every file and directory trap narrowed the name to UTF-8 and passed it to an ANSI API that read it in the active code page; the narrow calls agreed with each other, so a program could read back the names it had garbled itself. Every file, directory, link and pipe trap now goes through the W APIs (also fixing `File->Size` truncating above 2 GB, a 256-character full-path buffer and four Nil dereferences). Command-line arguments reached obr, obc, obd and obi through the ANSI code page and were decoded as UTF-8, so a source, `.obe` or library path with a character outside the code page arrived as `?`; each tool now enters at `wmain`, on MSVC and MinGW builds alike, and the files those names reach are opened by their wide names. Environment variables, `OBJECK_LIB_PATH`, `--lib-path`, the native-library loader (`LoadLibraryA`, which loaded nothing from an install directory with a non-ASCII name), the CA bundle and certificate loads, and `CommandOutput`'s temp file (unquoted, so a temp directory with a space sent the output elsewhere) are all UTF-16 now. On POSIX too, an argument was widened byte by byte through a signed char, so a program never received a non-ASCII argument intact. `obu`, `obb` and the `obn` launcher take UTF-16 arguments (`obu update` aborted in such an install directory because `fs::path::string()` throws), and `obn` also gained argument quoting, a PATH buffer longer than 1 KB, and a change to its own directory. `obd`'s interactive mode puts its streams in `_O_U8TEXT` so the first non-ASCII character no longer leaves `wcout` failed, its command scanner accepts non-ASCII letters in a file name, the launcher's `SetObjEnv.cmd` switches code page around the lines that hold paths, and `obu` writes console text through `WriteConsoleW`.
- **Found on the way**: `lang.obs` is compiled by the `_SYSTEM` bootstrap compiler, which skipped the all-paths-return check for the whole library, and three methods shipped with a return type and no return: `Runtime->CommandOutput(command)` crashed its caller on Windows on every call, and `Alarm->GetInterval()` and `UDPSocketServer->Bind()` returned whatever the trap left. The bootstrap compiler now checks every method except one whose body is a trap ([#1327](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1327)). `CommandOutput(command, variables)` kept a variable's old value when it was already set and left an unset one set to `""` afterwards, on every platform.

### Compiler and language

- **Numeric literals** ([#1332](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1332), [#1345](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1345), [#1355](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1355)): a float literal outside the double range compiled silently (`1.0e999` as infinity, `2.4e-324` as `0.0`) and is now `Float literal out of range`, judged by the value rather than `errno` because glibc and MSVC disagree on subnormals; `-9223372036854775808` was rejected and is accepted as the operand of a unary minus, Java's rule; a negated out-of-range integer reported "Expected expression"; one bad number produced a run of follow-on errors (eight inside an array literal) and now produces one. A decimal-looking literal with a leading zero was read as octal with no diagnostic (`010` was 8, `0_10` was 8, `0b` was 0): `0o17` is octal, with `_` separators and the `u` suffix, and `010` or `09` is one error that suggests `0o10` or `10`. The debugger's expression scanner follows the same rules (it saturated integers, printed Int's minimum off by one, and scanned no exponent).
- **Name resolution** ([#1312](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1312), [#1324](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1324), [#1336](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1336), [#1343](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1343)): with two `use`d bundles that both declare `class Foo`, an unqualified `Foo` silently resolved to whichever bundle the search reached first, and a program's class silently beat a used library's of the same name; it is now `Ambiguous name 'Shape': 'A.Shape' (x.obs:13) and 'B.Shape' (x.obs:21) are each in scope through 'use'`, while a qualified name, the current bundle's own name and the class's own enum still win. The check found that a callee's parameter and return types were resolved in the *caller's* bundle. Bundle-level enums and aliases are named with their bundle, as classes are (`Query.Structured.ColumnType` is the enum's real name), so two bundles can each declare `enum Kind` and a program's `enum ColumnType` no longer collides with `query.obl`'s; this is the library-format change. Constructors were inherited by accident: `Kid->New()` with no `New` of its own built the parent, and a class with no constructor built a `System.Base` that failed at the first cast. A class's enum is in scope in its own class, its children and the other classes of its bundle, and must be qualified from elsewhere. `x->As(Kid)->Only() <> "only"` failed with an invalid-cast error, `Defs.ColumnType->INT` did not resolve, and two classes each declaring `enum Type` resolved to the first.
- **Arrays** ([#1299](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1299), [#1309](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1309), [#1339](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1339)): `["a", "b", v]` crashed the program at every `-opt` level (a string-pair counter never reset, so the second literal emitted a `SWAP_INT` with one value on the stack); a call returning an interface made on an array element failed a cast at run time because the result's cast was emitted before the call; a literal `Nil` did not bind to an array parameter; `Vector<String[]>` is a clear error; `[["a","b","c"],["d","e","f"]]` had its dimensions swapped (3 x 2 holding a 2 x 3 layout), a 2-D `Char` literal reported a first extent of 420913348705 because the VM's copy assumed a 1-D header, and 3-D literals were refused. A call on a whole array (`a->Fill(7)`, `block->CompressZlib(1)`) pushed the array after its arguments, so every multi-argument call on an array got its arguments in the wrong slots and `CompressZlib(1)` passed `1` as the array; `names->ToString()` on an object array bound `System.Base`'s instance method to a static call and the three `*ArrayRef` classes recursed until the stack overflowed.
- **Calls and loops** ([#1299](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1299), [#1346](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1346), [#1347](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1347), [#1350](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1350)): `obc` segfaulted on any `for` whose condition was not a `<` comparison (`for(i := 1001; true; i += 1;)`), because range detection cast the condition to a binary expression unconditionally; an instance method could not call, with no receiver, an instance method inherited from a library class (`GetName()` in a `Thread` subclass's `Run()`); named arguments on a library method with several defaults were "ambiguous"; a chained call on a library generic class lost its type arguments; a ternary of an enum constant and a call chain failed to type-check; `Int->F(..);` on its own line was "Invalid statement". The label merging at `-opt s2`/`s3` could give a merged label the id of another label in a linked library method, so adding classes to `query.obl` made every query loop forever; fixed with a shared maximum-label scan that also covers `TRY_START`.
- **Narrowing is checked** ([#1299](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1299)): a `String[]` passed for `Compare[]` compiled and then failed at run time, but only for array literals; one rule now applies to literals, variables and call results. Widening to a parent or interface array needs no cast, and implicit narrowing (`Base[]` for `Kid[]`) is a compile error, since nothing could check it at run time; an explicit `As(Kid[])` still works.
- **A Float or function-reference conditional meets at the join as what it is** ([#1397](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1397)): `c ? a : b` leaves its value in a local at the join and stored it with the Int opcodes whatever its type. A Float conditional was right in the interpreter, but the JIT will not store a float register that way, so the method ran interpreted without saying so (a loop over a million elements with a `?:` on floats: about 113 ms a pass, 2.7 ms once compiled); a function reference is two words and the store took one, so a compiled method called through the closure of the branch it compiled last, giving wrong answers or an access violation. Found measuring SQL expression evaluation. No new opcodes.
- **A subscript on a later link of a call chain** (`v->Get(r)->Get()[c]`) no longer crashes the compiler ([#1426](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1426)).

### JIT

- **A compiled Float division by NaN no longer stops the program** ([#1399](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1399)): the x64 guard for a zero divisor compared with `ucomisd` and jumped on the zero flag, which an unordered compare sets as well as an equal one, so `x / NaN` raised `Divide by zero` where the interpreter computes NaN. It surfaced once [#1397](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1397) let methods with a Float `?:` compile. ARM64 compares with `fcmp`, which leaves the flag clear, and was right.
- **`Float->NaN()`, `Float->Inf()` and `Float->NegInf()` compile** ([#1423](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1423)): neither backend knew the three opcodes, and one unsupported opcode returns the whole method to the interpreter. `$Float:Inf()` is a one-opcode method that the `-opt s3` inliner pastes into its callers, so every method that wrote `best := Float->Inf()` ran interpreted, among them `System.ML.KDTree:Nearest` and the searches and min/max scans in `ml_neighbors`, `ml_cluster`, `ml_tree`, `ml_bayes`, `ai_search`, `ai_game`, `ai_optimize` and `math`. Answers were right; only `OBJECK_JIT_REPORT=1` could see it. The three are float literals now, from the same expressions as the interpreter's handlers, so a compiled method gets the same bits, including the same quiet NaN.
- **A multi-dimensional array access gives back its index register** ([#1424](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1424)): `a[i, j]` folds its indices into the first one's register, and a later index that was already in a register (computed, held in the local-register cache, or the copy of a pinned loop local) was added in and then dropped without being returned to the pool. After eight such accesses `GetRegister` found the pool empty with nothing live on the working stack, and the method went back to the interpreter, 10 to 20 times slower; the generated code was always correct. Both backends had it. `EllipticEnvelope`'s distances went from 1.68 s to 0.14 s, and the BERT classifier head's Adam step now compiles. A failed compile now reports the failing instruction, its opcode, the inlined callee and the reason (`no free integer register: the working stack holds 0 integer and 1 float register(s)...`), and `OBJECK_JIT_REPORT=1` audits the register pool at every label.
- **A loop's collection safepoint keeps the values pending across it** ([#1425](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1425)): the JIT polls for a stop-the-world collection at every loop header and parks the thread with a plain call to `MemoryManager::SafePoint`, on the assumption that the working stack is empty there. When the `-opt s3` inliner pasted a copy loop into an expression after an earlier operand had been evaluated, that operand was still pending in a caller-saved register, which the call clobbers, and a young object it points to may be moved by another thread's collection while the thread is parked; the next use was a Nil dereference or a segmentation fault in compiled code. Only a multithreaded program whose loop polled during another thread's collection reached it, which is why it was rare (10 times more frequent with a small nursery) and surfaced as an intermittent failure of the new SQL-expression concurrency test. The slow path now spills every register the working stack holds into scratch slots the collector marks and relocates, reloads them after the park, and leaves the method to the interpreter if there are more values than slots; the fast path is unchanged. AMD64 and ARM64.

### Libraries

- **`System.NLP` works on real text** ([#1286](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1286); design in `docs/NLP_DESIGN.md`): sentiment scored 4 of 10 ordinary sentences ("not good" was positive), the "Porter" stemmer was five suffix rules (`running` to `runn`), the tokenizer split on the space character only, the sentence splitter broke on `Dr.` and `3.50`, a TF-IDF refit kept the old vocabulary, and `NormalizedEditDistance` crashed on Nil. The stemmer is now Porter (1980) as Porter's reference `porter.c`, agreeing with NLTK on 8,559 of 8,559 words; the tokenizer handles contractions, decimals, hyphens, Unicode and all whitespace; sentiment is VADER-style (negation, boosters, capitals, `!`, `but`, a ~600-word lexicon, `LoadLexicon` for VADER's own file) and on Pang and Lee's 10,662-sentence corpus agrees with NLTK's VADER on 95.5% of labels when given VADER's lexicon; TF-IDF uses smoothed IDF and L2 normalization by default.
- **`Data.JSON`, `Data.CSV` and `Query.RegEx`** ([#1288](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1288), [#1289](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1289), [#1290](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1290)): a JSON number with an exponent (`-1.2e-05`, which embeddings routinely contain) made the whole document fail to parse, so one such value lost an entire LLM response; the lexer follows RFC 8259 and the invalid forms it used to accept (`01`, `1.`, `.5`, `1e`) are errors. A quoted CSV field containing a newline left the whole table unparsed, a short row failed without saying which, and rows longer than the header were accepted silently; the reader is single-pass RFC 4180, `GetError()` and `GetErrorRow()` name the row, `New(data, Char)` takes a delimiter, and `CsvRow->ToString()` and `CsvTable->FormatRow` write records that read back. Regular expressions gained real character classes (`[a-zA-Z]`, `[^abc]`, `[.,;]` and `\d \w \s` inside brackets were all rejected before), `\b` as a word boundary (it compiled to a backspace), empty matches for `x*` and `x?`, and capture groups (`Result->GroupCount`, `GetGroup(i)`, `GetGroupStart(i)`; `(?:...)` does not capture).
- **`Data.JSON` decodes strings on parse and escapes once on output** ([#1403](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1403)): the parser kept a string's escape sequences as written, so `GetValue()` of the string `"x\"y"` returned `x\"y`, and parsing then serializing doubled every escape (`{"a":"x\"y"}` came back as `{"a":"x\\\"y"}`) and wrote `/` as `\/`. Every consumer had to know to call `JsonElement->Decode`; some did (the MCP handler's ids, the language server, the Ollama, Gemini and OpenAI error and text fields, `Query.Structured`'s JSON cells) and some did not (`Response->GetText()`, inference errors, the ONNX tokenizer's vocabulary), and the stream parser appended every escape letter twice and ended a string at an escaped quote. `GetValue()`, `GetString()` and `GetNames()` now return the text itself (`\" \\ \/ \b \f \n \r \t \uXXXX`, a surrogate pair as one code point), an unpaired surrogate escape becomes U+FFFD rather than an error (so one damaged character does not lose a whole model response), an invalid escape (`\x`, a short `\u`) or an unterminated string is a parse error naming the offset, `/` is no longer escaped, and non-ASCII text is still written as `\u` escapes so output stays plain ASCII. `Decode` is deprecated and unchanged, and every internal caller is removed; JSON that was pasted together unescaped (`CsvRow->ToJson`, `KMeansGrouping->ToString`, JSON-RPC method names, Ollama upload names, the OpenAI realtime voice) is escaped, `Data.JSON.RPC` echoes a string id as sent, and the code-doc tool's hand-rolled escaper uses `Encode`. A round trip of 100,000 random strings is the identity. Parsing and serializing a 100,000-object document took 2.66 s and 2.79 s against 2.89 s and 3.67 s. Found on the way: the language server's formatter scanner never tested for the end of the text and spun at full CPU on an unterminated string, character or `#~` comment, a `#` comment on the last line with no newline, or valid code with a literal ending in an escaped backslash and no later quote.
- **Kernel SVMs** (`System.ML`, [#1426](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1426); design in `docs/ML_KERNELS_DESIGN.md`): `KernelSVM`, a C-SVC with linear, RBF, polynomial and sigmoid kernels on LIBSVM's SMO solver (WSS3 working-set selection, shrinking, an LRU kernel cache sized in MB) with scikit-learn's `SVC` parameters (`C`, `gamma` 'scale'/'auto'/value, `degree`, `coef0`, `class_weight`); and `OneClassSVM` (`nu`, scikit-learn's sign conventions, `IsOutlier`) on the same solver. Checked against an exact KKT enumeration and an independent SMO on 488 problems. At the default tolerance `OneClassSVM` can flag more than the `nu` fraction of its training rows; the documentation says so.
- **Anomaly detection** (`System.ML`, [#1426](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1426); design in `docs/ML_OUTLIERS_DESIGN.md`): an `OutlierDetector` interface (true is an outlier, a higher score is more anomalous) with `ZScoreDetector`, `IsolationForest`, `LocalOutlierFactor` (novelty mode), `EllipticEnvelope` (FastMCD), `DbscanDetector`, `AutoencoderDetector` (on the new multi-layer `DenseNetwork`: any depth, ReLU/sigmoid/tanh/linear, minibatch Adam) and `OneClassSvmDetector`; `ConfusionMatrix`, `Metrics->FalsePositiveRate`, and `SensitivitySweep`/`SweepResult` to sweep any detector's sensitivity. Example: `programs/examples/ml/anomaly_sweep.obs`.
- **Forests for labs** (`System.ML`, [#1426](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1426)): `DecisionTreeClassifier` and `RandomForestClassifier` gain `min_samples_leaf`, unlimited depth, `GetFeatureImportances`, `Apply`, depth and leaf counts and fit times; the split search is one sorted sweep per feature instead of a quadratic one (a depth-12 tree on 2,000 x 41 rows: 52 s to 0.04 s) with bit-identical splits. Examples: `svm_lab.obs` and `rf_lab.obs`, on NSL-KDD or a synthetic stand-in.
- **BERT embeddings** (`System.NLP`, [#1426](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1426); design in `docs/ML_BERT_DESIGN.md`): `WordPieceTokenizer` (Hugging Face `BertTokenizer` parity on 39,154 cases, Unicode tables generated from `unicodedata`), `ApiSequenceTokenizer` for API-call traces and `TokenEncoding`; the new library `nlp_onnx.obl` with `System.NLP.Onnx.BertEmbedder` (CLS, MEAN or POOLER sentence embeddings from a BERT-family ONNX export, with a CPU fallback when the default provider cannot open the model); and `System.ML.EmbeddingClassifier`, a trainable binary head over fixed-size embeddings (ReLU hidden layers, class-weighted binary cross-entropy, Adam or SGD, per-epoch loss history). Example: `programs/examples/ml/bert_malware_lab.obs`, a frozen-encoder malware lab.
- **`System.ML.Lab`** (the new library `ml_lab.obl`, [#1426](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1426); design in `docs/ML_LAB_SUPPORT_DESIGN.md`): `Partition->TrainTest` (stratified, seeded, exact), `Resample->ToPositiveRatio` for class-imbalance runs against a fixed test set, `Stopwatch`, `Labels`, `Experiment` over any model through a `Trial`, a `ResultTable` with text, Markdown, CSV and JSON output, and `Chart` writing standalone SVG line, bar and loss-curve charts. Example: `programs/examples/ml/lab_sweeps.obs`. `-lib @ai` now includes `ml_lab` and `json`, and `-lib @vision` includes `nlp_onnx` and links again (it lacked `models.obl` since `onnx.obl` began linking it).
- **`System.Time.Timer` measures wall-clock time** on Linux and macOS ([#1426](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1426)); it was process CPU time there, so a timer around a sleep or a blocked thread read nearly zero.
- **`McpServer`** ([#1394](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1394), [#1396](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1396)): a Nil, incomplete or ill-typed configuration, and any request body that was JSON but not an object, an object without `method`, or a `resources/read` without `params`, dereferenced Nil, and the VM ends the process on that, so one request from any client killed the server. `Serve` now validates `http_port` (0 to 65,535; 0 asks the system for a port), `http_session_timeout`, `debug`, `name`, `version` and `instructions` and refuses a bad configuration on standard error naming the key, with `McpServer->GetLastError()` giving the reason; `McpRequestHandler` answers a malformed request with the JSON-RPC 2.0 error the MCP specification mandates (-32700 parse error, -32600 invalid request including a body over 4 MiB, -32601 method not found, -32602 invalid params, -32603 internal error for a tool callback that raises, dereferences Nil or returns Nil), echoes a string or large id as sent, and keeps its thread. `WebServer->Serve(filename)` was an empty stub that loaded the file and served nothing; it now says on standard error that it is not implemented.
- **`System.ML`** ([#1287](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1287), [#1341](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1341)): `Matrix2D->Softmax` normalized over the whole matrix, so with one sample per row each row summed to about 1/rows; it now normalizes each row, subtracting the row's maximum so `Softmax([[1000,1001]])` stays finite, with `SoftmaxColumns` for the other axis. `Tanh` returned NaN past about 710, `Sigmoid` could overflow `Exp`, `RandomNormal` could take `Log(0)`, and `StandardScaler` turned a constant column into -1s through a rounding-step deviation near 1e-17. `KMeans->Group` keeps its labels' order.
- **LLM clients** ([#1292](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1292), [#1293](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1293), [#1340](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1340)): `API.OpenAI` and `API.Google.Gemini` had no regression test that gates CI, so each response parser is now a public function testable offline against recorded bodies, which found that `Response->GetText()` returned Nil for every reasoning-model reply (a reasoning item precedes the message), four Nil dereferences (function-call outputs, refusals, `b64_json` images, which gpt-image-1 always returns), `DALLE3_1024_1792` sending `1792x1024`, and every Batch call going to `/batch` rather than `/batches`; in Gemini a blocked prompt crashed, API errors were printed but never recorded, and `GenerationConfig` was an empty class. `API.Ollama` is brought up to Ollama's current REST API: tool calling (`Reply->GetToolCalls`, `Chat->AddToolResult`), structured output from a JSON schema, `/api/embed` with batches, `think`, `keep_alive`, the full option set, reply metadata, `/api/ps` and `/api/version`; chat history was escaped twice on the way back to the server, chat images were sent where the API ignores them, and `/api/show` and `/api/ps` replies from current servers crashed. Gemini corpus query results were run through `Url->Decode` although the response is JSON, and 22 error sites never unescaped `error/message`.
- **SDL2** ([#1294](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1294)): game controllers worked on no platform, because `GameController->New` called a native, `sdl_gamecontroller_open`, that did not exist; a checker now confirms all 425 natives the Objeck side calls exist. Window, controller, drop-file, touch, text-editing and user events can be read; blend modes, batch drawing from one `Int[]`, float variants and `Texture->Update`; text measuring, font metrics, styles and wrapped rendering; logical size and scale; `Renderer->ReadPixels` and `SavePNG`; the performance counter and `GetTicks64`; a queue-mode `AudioDevice` for generated audio.
- **ODBC** ([#1331](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1331), [#1337](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1337), [#1370](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1370)): running a `ParameterStatement` twice read memory the native library had freed after the first run (0xC0000005 under the JIT, heap corruption without), and bound parameters pointed into `IntRef`/`FloatRef`/`Byte[]` objects a collection could move before the run; the statement now owns its handle and maps and every value is copied. `SetReal` bound a double where `SQL_C_FLOAT` reads a float, and `SetBlob` bound no length so a blob stopped at its first zero byte. Text was read through one 8 KB buffer and silently cut short, a NULL was converted from that buffer before anything was written to it, and a refused `SELECT` freed its diagnostic with its statement and read as an empty result; `ResultSet->IsOpen()` and `GetBytes(column)` are new. A zero-row `UPDATE`/`DELETE` was reported as -1 on every platform. **Every macOS package built by `tools/deps/build_macos_deps.sh` shipped a `Database.ODBC` that could not load any driver**: libiodbc's configure decides whether to prefix entry points with `_` using a test program that Apple clang 15 refuses to compile, so it answered "yes" and the driver manager looked up `_SQLAllocHandle` and found nothing. Found the first time the ODBC tests ran on the macOS leg.
- **Smaller fixes** ([#1341](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1341), [#1348](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1348), [#1352](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1352), [#1356](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1356), [#1359](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1359)): `Byte[]->ToHexString()` returned `[0x76,0x4f,...]` while its callers used it as a digest, and `Int[]->ToHexString` gave `],]`; `ByteBuffer->ToString` dumped the whole 512-byte backing array, `Compare` cast its argument to `String` and always raised, the first `Append` to a buffer built from an empty array raised, and `New(Byte[])` kept the caller's array despite being documented as a copy (`Wrap` now does that on purpose); `XmlElement->ToString` writes attributes in insertion order, a parsed self-closing tag serialized to nothing, and `GetDecodedText()` returns text untrimmed, which `xml:space="preserve"` needs.

### Tooling

- **Debugger** ([#1345](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1345), [#1355](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1355), [#1360](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1360), [#1377](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1377), [#1341](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1341), [#1404](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1404)): a DAP `evaluate` that failed answered `success: true` with `<error>` and printed the reason into the program's output; it now answers `success: false` with the reason as `message`, the same text the console's `p` prints. `stackTrace` honours `startFrame`/`levels` and reports `totalFrames`, since a 16,384-frame stack is no longer sent whole. `print` and DAP expansion know the open-addressed `Hash` and the unboxed collections. `obd` sends every output event of a run before the `terminated` that ends it: the adapter forwarded stdout and stderr from two reader threads and sent `terminated` the moment the run returned, so the program's last lines could still be in a pipe and a client that stops listening at `terminated` lost them (the macOS DAP flake). `terminated` now waits, for at most two seconds, until both readers have reached end-of-file.
- **Language server** ([#1330](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1330), [#1342](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1342)): the editor API index held no enums at all, so hover on `Query.Structured.ColumnType` showed nothing and completion after `ColumnType->` offered keywords; it now carries every documented library enum and consts set (90 enums, 1,012 items) under the names the compiler gives them, hover shows an enum's items and an item's value, and the analyzer completes items of a program's own enums, including in a class with no fields. The 19 undocumented library enums are documented, nested enums get `Klass->Name` pages in the HTML reference, and an enum in a private class is no longer listed. The formatter no longer hangs the server (see `Data.JSON` above).
- **Playground** ([#1363](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1363)): libraries are inferred from a program's `use` lines and qualified names, with each library's own dependencies added (`use Query.Structured;` links eight libraries, which `obc` does not chase), a bundle the sandbox cannot offer is refused before a container starts with a one-line reason, and compile errors come back as line/column diagnostics with a hint naming the bundle to `use`. 42 demos in 10 categories, eight new for typed errors, named arguments, generic methods, `ref`, jagged arrays, the unboxed collections, SQL and indexes; a rebuilt UI with light and dark modes, a searchable examples menu, clickable errors and a plain text area if the editor CDN is unreachable. The library map is generated from `lib_src` and a test fails when it is stale.
- **Examples and docs** ([#1283](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1283), [#1344](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1344), [#1361](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1361), [#1279](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1279), [#1285](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1285)): 86 of the 281 example programs no longer compiled, written against bundles and classes long renamed (`HTTP`, `XML`, `RegEx`, `StringMap`, `IntVector`, `Console->ReadString`); 85 are ported and the three the compiler itself refused led to [#1346](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1346) and [#1347](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1347). `errors_29.obs` ships as the `try`/`on`/`finally` example. The getting-started guide covers typed errors, named arguments, `ref`, generic methods, jagged arrays, the unboxed collections and `0o` literals. A one-page architecture diagram, `docs/architecture.md` brought up to date (it still said Objeck had no `try`), and about 55 MB of third-party manuals, plus unreferenced images and binaries, removed from `docs/`. Design documents precede the next library work: SQL expressions ([#1395](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1395)), anomaly detection ([#1400](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1400)), kernel SVMs ([#1401](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1401)), `System.ML.Lab` ([#1402](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1402)) and BERT embeddings ([#1408](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1408)).

### CI and release

- **ODBC is tested on every platform** ([#1370](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1370)): the five regression tests that open a SQLite data source ran only on the two Linux legs and printed `SKIP` on windows-x64, windows-arm64 and macos-arm64, so the native library had no coverage where it ships as a DLL or dylib. Each leg now installs a checksummed SQLite ODBC driver (the Windows ARM64 one compiled from source, since upstream ships none) and registers a per-user DSN, and `OBJECK_REQUIRE_ODBC=1` turns the skip into a failure. The first macOS run found the libiodbc defect above.
- **Collision-safe regression suites** ([#1372](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1372)): two suites on one machine (an agent's run beside another's, or two CI jobs on one runner) collided on fixed ports, fixed fixture names and the shared temporary folder; with every server test on port 0 and every file per process, two whole suites running at once in two trees pass 674 of 674 on both sides.
- **`TESTS.md` is regenerated on master, not in every pull request** ([#1305](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1305)): every PR that added a test committed the manifest, so each merge conflicted every other open PR, about ten times in one day. A workflow regenerates it after a push to master and commits only on change.
- **Debian packages for Ubuntu 24.04** ([#1281](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1281)) are built from the published tarballs, install-tested in a clean container on native x64 and ARM64 runners, uploaded and added to the signed `SHA256SUMS` by `release-deb.yml` after every publish; first published beside v2026.10.1.
- **Smaller** ([#1282](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1282), [#1369](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1369), [#1374](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1374), [#1381](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1381), [#1368](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1368), [#1406](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1406)): `obc --version` reads `Copyright (c) 2007-2026`; the HTTP/3 downgrade test checks at run time that its negative host does not advertise `h3` (example.com started to); two independently green PRs did not build together and were repaired at once; a windows-arm64-only test failure was the console host wrapping a double-width character at cell 120, not the code under test; `core/compiler/web`, which nothing built, is removed; `tls_write_byte_result` waits for the server to hang up before expecting a write to fail, after a Windows ARM64 run in which all 300 writes succeeded because nothing had waited for the peer to close.

### Upgrading

Every behaviour a program could notice, with the change that made it:

- **Library format** (magic `0xffb9`, [#1324](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1324)): every `.obl` must be rebuilt with this toolchain. An `0xffb8` library is refused with "was built before enums were named with their bundle. Rebuild it with this toolchain."
- **Compiled programs**: an `.obe` compiled by an earlier release is refused by this runtime, which requires an exact toolchain version match; the message names the file, both toolchain versions and which side is older, and says to recompile the source with this `obc` ([#1387](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1387)).
- **`obu` in a non-ASCII install directory**: a v2026.10.1 `obu` run from an install directory whose name has a character outside the ANSI code page crashes (0xC0000409) before doing anything, and nothing in this release can reach it, so such an install must be upgraded to v2026.10.2 by hand; from v2026.10.2 on `obu` handles that path ([#1357](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1357)).
- **`Directory->List`** no longer returns `.` and `..` on Windows; it never did on POSIX ([#1327](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1327)).
- **Integer literals**: `010` is a compile error that suggests `0o10` (octal) or `10`; `0o17` is octal; a bare `0b` or `0o` is an error rather than 0; `-9223372036854775808` is accepted ([#1355](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1355), [#1345](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1345)). A float literal outside the double range is a compile error ([#1332](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1332)).
- **Name resolution**: an unqualified class or enum name that two `use`d bundles declare is a compile error; a class with no `New` of its own cannot be instantiated through its parent's; a class's enum must be qualified from another bundle; implicit narrowing of `Base[]` to `Kid[]` is a compile error ([#1336](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1336), [#1343](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1343), [#1299](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1299)). `programs/frameworks/openai/openai_batch.obs` was the one program in the repository that relied on first-use-wins.
- **`System.Time.Date` must be written in full** when both `System.Time` and `Database.ODBC` are in scope, since both declare a `Date` ([#1331](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1331)).
- **`Byte->CompressBr`/`UncompressBr`** are `CompressDeflate`/`UncompressDeflate` (raw RFC 1951 deflate, which is what they always produced); compiled programs keep running, source needs the new name. `Web.HTTP.Server.Response`'s `Compression->BR` is removed; `DEFLATE` sends the zlib format, which is what HTTP's `deflate` means ([#1314](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1314)).
- **`Byte[]->ToHexString()`** returns two lower-case hex digits per byte with no prefix or separators; `Int[]->ToHexString` returns `[0xff,0x10]` ([#1348](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1348)).
- **`ByteBuffer->New(Byte[])`** copies the array; `ByteBuffer->Wrap(Byte[])` keeps it ([#1359](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1359)).
- **`Collection.Hash`**: iteration order (`GetKeys`, `GetValues`, `GetKeyValues`, `ToString`, `Each`) changes; it was never specified. Keys of different classes are always distinct even when their `Compare` would say equal. `SetAutoResize(false)` no longer matters. `XmlElement->ToString` writes attributes in the order they were added, and `KMeans->Group` keeps its labels' order ([#1341](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1341)).
- **`Matrix2D->Softmax`** on a matrix with several rows and several columns now normalizes each row; the old whole-matrix values were wrong ([#1287](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1287)).
- **`Data.JSON`**: a document containing a malformed number (`01`, `1.`, `.5`, `1e`) now fails to parse; `JsonElement->New(Float)` writes the shortest round-trip text, so request bodies carry `0.5` rather than `0.500000` ([#1288](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1288), [#1304](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1304)).
- **`Data.JSON` decodes strings when it parses** ([#1403](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1403)): `GetValue()`, `GetString()` and `GetNames()` return the text itself, so escapes such as `\"`, `\n` and `\u00e9` are the characters they stand for. A program that called `JsonElement->Decode` on a value read from a parsed document must stop: the value is already decoded, and decoding it again corrupts text that holds a backslash (`C:\new` would gain a line break). Parse then serialize is now the identity where it doubled every escape, `ToString()` no longer writes `/` as `\/`, and a document with an invalid escape (`\x`, a short `\u`) or an unterminated string no longer parses (`GetError()` names the offset). `Decode` is deprecated and unchanged; it still unescapes text that holds JSON escapes.
- **`System.NLP.TF_IDF->New()`** smooths IDF as scikit-learn does, `ln((1 + N) / (1 + df)) + 1`, and scales each vector to unit length (`smooth_idf` and `l2_normalize` are true; `remove_stopwords` and `stem` are false). v2026.10.1 weighted by the raw `ln(N / df)` and did not normalize, so vectors, similarities and anything thresholded on them change; `TF_IDF->New(smooth_idf : false, l2_normalize : false)` gives the old weighting, though the new tokenizer splits text differently. A second `Fit` replaces the vocabulary instead of adding to it ([#1286](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1286)).
- **`Data.CSV`**: a row longer than the header fails the parse and `GetError()` names the row ([#1289](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1289)).
- **`Query.RegEx`**: `a$b` no longer matches `a`; `(?` is an error unless it begins `(?:`; `(a+)+b` is slower because it now captures, `(?:a+)+b` is not ([#1290](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1290)).
- **`Query.Structured`**: CSV cells are typed by their whole text (`1-2` is a string, not 1); `Average` skips non-numeric cells; `a or b and c` means `a or (b and c)`; a query with trailing junk fails ([#1291](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1291)). In the new columnar engine NaN satisfies only `<>`, is ignored by zone maps and sorts after every number ([#1351](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1351)); that is a property of the engine rather than a change, since v2026.10.1's SQL path answered the same way. `+ - / %` are symbols in SQL, and `.` is a symbol between two quoted strings; both were errors before ([#1367](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1367), [#1366](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1366)). `Table->GetError` and `Catalog->GetError` answer the calling thread's own last error ([#1378](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1378)). `where a = b` compares two columns when `b` is one (it compared `a` with the text `'b'`), a literal may stand on the left, an Int overflow in the select list gives an empty cell rather than a wrapped number, and two Bools in an OBJECT column order false before true ([#1398](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1398)); an `ON` that does not compare one column from each side is no longer refused but tests every pair, `ORDER BY` may name a grouping column the select list does not show, and an expression in a grouped query's select list, `HAVING` or `ORDER BY` is answered rather than refused ([#1416](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1416), [#1417](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1417)). A URL cache written by v2026.10.1 is not read, so the first fetch of each URL downloads it once more ([#1384](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1384)).
- **`API.OpenAI` `Response->GetText()`** returns the text of a reasoning-model reply instead of Nil ([#1292](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1292)). **`API.Ollama`** clears `GetLastError()` at the start of each request, `Delete` and `Copy` report the server's error text, and `Create(name, modelfile)` works only on servers before 0.5.5 ([#1293](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1293)).
- **`Serializer->Serialize`** compresses at zlib level 1 rather than 9: about 14% larger, and 5 s to 0.23 s on 32 MB; `Byte->CompressZlib` keeps level 9 ([#1304](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1304)).
- **Interpolation**: `"{$s}"` with a Nil `String` or object prints `Nil` instead of raising ([#1306](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1306)).
- **Call stack**: the default depth is 16,384 frames (`--call-depth`, `OBJECK_CALL_DEPTH`; 256 to 1,048,576), `runtime.call.depth_max` reports it, and running out of stack, native stack included, is a recoverable `RuntimeError` compiled or interpreted. VM threads reserve 16 MB of native stack on Windows and `obr`/`obi` 32 MB for the main thread ([#1377](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1377)).
