- **`--gc-threshold`** no longer grows on its own: it holds exactly as given while a major collection leaves at least a quarter of it free (so `--gc-threshold=64k` still collects about every 64 KB of allocation for a program with little live data); while more than three quarters of it survive, the next major waits until the heap reaches twice the live size; and the given value returns once the live heap fits under it again. `OBJECK_GC_TRACE` reports the first time a program outgrows its threshold ([#1311](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1311), [#1383](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1383)). Without the flag the threshold is twice the live heap after each major collection, never below 8 MB.
- **POSIX only: `SIGPIPE` is ignored from VM start**, so a write to a closed stdout pipe fails instead of ending the program, as in Python; Windows never had `SIGPIPE`, so nothing changes there ([#1373](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1373)).
- **Sockets**: `GetLastError()` is Nil when the thread's latest socket call worked ([#1371](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1371)); a UDP client is connected to its peer and hears only it, moving to the next address its name resolves to when one is refused ([#1376](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1376)); `UDPSocketServer` binds the dual-stack wildcard ([#1373](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1373)); on Windows a second TCP server on a taken port now fails rather than sharing it ([#1348](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1348)); `TCPSecureSocket->SetVerifyPeer(strict)` changes nothing and returns whether verification was required ([#1349](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1349)); `McpServer->Serve` accepts `"http_port": 0` and reports the port through `GetBoundPort()` ([#1380](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1380)).
- **HTTP**: a 302 is framed like any other response and follows the normal keep-alive decision; `Url->Encode` escapes non-ASCII text as UTF-8 and no longer escapes `~`; `Request->GetParam` sees names that were encoded and values containing `=` ([#1300](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1300), [#1328](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1328), [#1340](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1340)).
- **`TableServer`** sends tables as `.otab` format 1 so clients of any version read them; a client of this version reads both ([#1365](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1365)). It no longer serializes requests, so a program that changes published tables from its own threads must do it between `BeginWrite()` and `EndWrite()` ([#1389](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1389)).
- **`McpServer->Serve`** refuses a configuration that lacks `http_port`, `http_session_timeout`, `debug`, `name`, `version` or `instructions`, or holds one of the wrong type or out of range, with a message on standard error naming the key; each of those took the VM down before, the first three at `Serve` and the strings at the first `initialize` ([#1394](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1394)). `WebServer->Serve(filename)` reports that it is not implemented.
- **`System.Time.Timer`** measures wall-clock time on Linux and macOS, where it measured process CPU time ([#1426](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1426)).
- **API compatibility**: against v2026.10.1's editor index (`objk_apis.json`) no class is removed (450 classes become 498 on master and 531 with the lab work in [#1426](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1426)) and the only functions removed are `Byte->CompressBr` and `UncompressBr`, which are `CompressDeflate` and `UncompressDeflate` above.

## [v2026.10.1] - 2026-10-20

**Objeck gains structured error handling (try/on/finally over Error objects), named arguments, method-level generics, reference parameters, expression array literals and jagged arrays; the language server is rebuilt around LSP 3.17; and more than a hundred defects found by Rosetta Code tasks are fixed.**

> **Upgrading:** the language grows -- `try`, `on`, `finally` and `ref` are contextual, so no existing name stops compiling -- and every `.obl` is a new format (magic `0xffb8`): rebuild any library of your own with this toolchain. Standard-library parameter names are now public API, because a call can name them.

### Language

- **Errors you can raise, catch by type, and clean up after** (#1267, #1273, #1274): System.Error and its subclasses are raised with Runtime->Raise and caught with try { } on(e : ParseError) { } finally { }. Clauses are tested in order, an error no clause names goes on after finally, and return, break and continue leave through finally. The runtime's own faults are NilError, IndexError, DivideByZeroError, CastError and RuntimeError, and a failed Runtime->Assert or library call now recovers instead of ending the program. ?-> still recovers from anything, and Runtime->GetLastError() says what it was.
- **Named arguments** (#1263, #1268): F(data, rate: 0.1, epochs: 50) in any order, skipping any defaulted parameter, for the program's own methods and constructors and for library ones: every .obl records its parameter names, so standard-library parameter names are now public API. Arguments are still evaluated in the order written.
- **Method-level generics** (#1266, #1275): function : Max&lt;T : Compare&gt;(values : Vector&lt;T&gt;) ~ T, inferred from the arguments and checked against the bound, in the program and in libraries. Vector, List and CompareList gain Map&lt;R&gt;, so a map can change the element type: words-&gt;Map(\(s) =&gt; s-&gt;ToUpper()) is a Vector&lt;String&gt;.
- **Reference parameters** (#1277): function : Swap(ref a : Int, ref b : Int) and Swap(ref x, ref y): copy in, copy out, as Swift's inout, with 'ref' at the call site too. A local, a parameter or a field can be passed.
- **Array literals hold expressions; jagged arrays** (#1264, #1265): [x, x * x, Square(x + 1)] is built when evaluated, and objects of several classes make a Base[]. T[][] is an array of rows of any length, as a local, parameter, field or return, with ragged literals.

### Language server

- **The language server, rebuilt around LSP 3.17** (#1221-#1259): pull diagnostics, real completion documentation and MarkupContent hover, rename with validation and organize imports, hierarchical document symbols, one configuration per workspace folder, semantic token ranges and deltas, a Run code lens, links for 'use', progress reporting, and one server per VS Code window over stdio. Analysis is reused until the text changes and edits are debounced; positions are UTF-16 on every platform.

### Packaging

- **Debian packages for Ubuntu 24.04**: sudo apt install ./objeck-ubuntu24.04-x64_2026.10.1.deb (or -arm64) installs the toolchain under /usr/lib/objeck with the commands on PATH, and apt installs the system libraries it needs; OpenCV, SDL2, unixODBC and LAME are recommended rather than required, since only the libraries that wrap them load them. The package holds the release's own Linux binaries, unchanged; apt remove takes it all out. Built from the published tarballs by `.github/workflows/release-deb.yml`, which installs each in a clean container before uploading it and adding it to `SHA256SUMS`; design in `docs/DEB_PACKAGE_DESIGN.md`.

### Libraries

- **Libraries** (#1108, #1169, #1251, #1262): Condition, Semaphore, CountDownLatch and Barrier; set algebra; Hash, Heap and sort additions; String helpers; System.Math.Rational and Complex; System.Base->ToString for every class.

### Bug Fixes

- **JIT and recovery fixes** (#1190, #1198, #1205, #1260, #1271, #1272, #1273): a deferred load materialized in one branch arm; an error in a tail-call loop and a bounds error mid-argument-list crashed recovery; more than 16 nested guarded calls lost their inner handlers; a NaN compared as equal (x64) and as less (ARM64); negative array sizes; ?-> deferred correctly from a bridge interpreter.
- **Compiler fixes** (#1168, #1197, #1204, #1207, #1208, #1249, #1261): wrong code in call chains and closures, generic type arguments through chained calls, primitive receivers, unary minus on a parenthesised operand, critical() locks released on return, a tail-call label that collided with an inlined callee's (an infinite loop at -opt s3), a returned literal swapped with a leaving block's, and typos that used to declare a variable silently.
- **VM and library fixes** (#1192, #1193, #1195, #1199, #1200, #1201, #1211, #1212, #1240, #1257, #1268): Float->ToString of large values, GMT dates and pre-1970 times, file I/O edge cases (Flush, ReadLine, empty files), multi-dimensional serialization, reflection metadata, String edge cases, "\e" as ESC, Byte arithmetic, and Ints no longer printed with a locale's digit grouping on macOS and Linux.

### Platforms

- **Linux ships an environment helper and an optional desktop entry** ([#1088](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1088)): the Windows and macOS launchers set up an environment and open a shell, and the Linux tarball had no equivalent -- the README asked you to export `PATH` and `OBJECK_LIB_PATH` yourself. Sourcing `env.sh` does that, `objeck-shell` starts an interactive shell with it applied, and `install-desktop.sh` adds an "Objeck" entry to the application menu. It writes only under `XDG_DATA_HOME`, so it needs no root and cannot collide with a distribution package, and it is a separate opt-in script rather than part of extraction. The entry sets `Terminal=true`, so the desktop environment supplies the terminal instead of this guessing which emulator is installed. `Exec` is quoted, because the Desktop Entry specification splits that value on spaces and an install path with a space in it would otherwise launch nothing.

### Build

- **The MSYS2/MinGW build had not compiled for some time, in five independent ways** ([#1087](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1087), [#1089](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1089), [#1091](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1091)): four of the five were the same mistake -- `_WIN32` used where `_MSC_VER` was meant. MinGW-w64 defines `_WIN32` *and* compiles with GCC, so every MSVC-only construct behind that guard reached a compiler that has never had it. `core/vm/arch/memory.h`'s write barrier used `ReadNoFence64` and `_InterlockedOr64`, which took out `obr`, `obd`, `obi` and the module at once, since every translation unit includes that header. `core/lib/diags/diags.cpp` used `__try`/`__except`, so `libobjk_diags.dll` -- the LSP's native half -- could not be built at all. The VM linked no `bcrypt` although `common.cpp` relies on a `#pragma comment(lib, ...)` that only MSVC obeys, so every link containing it failed on `BCryptGenRandom`. A stale `sys.a` produced `undefined reference to 'logger'` on a tree that compiles cleanly, because the copy that publishes the archive to its two consumers lived *inside* the recipe that builds it and therefore ran only on a rebuild. The deploy scripts also had no failure handling whatsoever: every stage built and then copied unconditionally, so one bad header produced two hundred lines of consequences ending in `cp: cannot stat`. They now use the same progress UI as the POSIX deploy, check prerequisites up front naming the `pacman` line that installs what is missing, and reach 18 of 18 stages.
- **The ONNX and OpenCV bindings compile against OpenCV 5** ([#1091](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1091)): `contourArea`, `boundingRect`, `getRotationMatrix2D`, `approxPolyDP`, `estimateAffinePartial2D` and `LMEDS` moved into `opencv2/geometry/2d.hpp` and `opencv2/geometry/3d.hpp`, neither of which `opencv.hpp` includes. Nothing left namespace `cv`, so this is two version-guarded include lines rather than the Homebrew `opencv@4` pin an earlier release reached for.
- **The JIT archive compiled one source twice, with different flag sets** ([#1092](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1092)): each backend directory named `../jit_common.o` as a prerequisite of its archive *and* carried a `%.o: %.cpp` rule matching it, while the object is actually built by the parent makefile inside the `jit_common.a` sub-make. So `make` had two ways to produce one file and used both. Run serially that is waste and the second copy wins, which leaves the archive carrying an object built with the wrong flags; under `-j` the two compiles race and `ar` can reach the file mid-rewrite, which is the `ar: ../jit_common.o: No such file or directory` another machine hit on this tree. The object is now named only inside the recipe, where `make` has no way to try to build it, while `jit_common.a` stays a prerequisite so the sub-make that does build it still runs first; the `ar` command line is unchanged. Found with `make -n`, which shows it deterministically -- six clean parallel rebuilds passed both before and after the fix.

### Release

- **A manifest regenerated after Windows signing is signed again** ([#1081](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1081)): signing rewrites the installers, so `SHA256SUMS` is rebuilt over them, which invalidates the signature -- and `obu` refuses a manifest whose signature does not verify, so this is the difference between a verifiable release and one that reports tampering. The workflow meant to repair it had never once completed: it signed the manifest, verified it against the committed public key, and then could not upload it, because `cd "$WORK"` moved out of the checkout and every `gh` call after that lost the repository it was inferring. v2026.10.0's manifest was re-signed by hand. The step now carries `GH_REPO` explicitly.
- **Every Sourceforge link in the repository returned "Page not found"** ([#1085](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1085)): the project's unix name is `objeck-lang`, not `objeck`, so the README's "Alternative" download link, the one in `docs/release_process.md`, the URL the publish job prints on success and the one it writes into the run summary all 404'd. The real listing was complete throughout -- all 12 assets of v2026.10.0 plus both source archives -- mirrored by a GitHub webhook rather than by the upload job, whose SFTP path carried the same wrong slug and would have created nothing. Also ignores `sign_release.cmd`'s staging directory, which is removed on the way out but not when the script exits early: v2026.10.0 left 78 MB of signed installers untracked in the repository root, exactly where `git add -A` would have taken them.
- **The objeck.org deploy covered half the site, and could not run** ([#1084](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1084)): v2026.10.0 published with `objeck.org/api/latest` correctly at v2026.10.0 while the page a reader actually lands on still offered "Download v2026.9.7". Two unrelated causes, neither of which would have gone red. `deploy_website.sh` only ever deployed the generated API reference; `docs/web/` holds the site's own pages and nothing deployed them, so every release depended on someone remembering an undocumented hand-upload. It now does both halves by default, with `--api-only` and `--site-only` to narrow it. The site pages cannot use the reference's upload-beside-and-swap, because they live directly in `~/public_html` beside `api/`, `.htaccess` and `objeck-release.pub`, so they go per file: hashed against the live copies so only what differs travels, staged remotely, hash-checked there, and moved into place one atomic rename at a time, leaving a remote file with no local counterpart alone rather than treating it as stale. It also refuses a `readme.html` whose Download button names a version other than the one being deployed -- that button is hardcoded, it sat on v2026.8.1 through the whole of v2026.8.2 and on v2026.9.7 while v2026.10.0 was live, and uploading a page that offers the previous release is worse than leaving the old one because it looks freshly deployed. Separately, `release-publish.yml`'s `deploy-docs` job had no checkout at all.
- **The running release draft was titled with a variable rather than a tag** ([#1082](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1082)), and the four download markers were advanced to v2026.10.0 once that tag existed ([#1083](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1083)).

### Infrastructure

- **Release verification runs on all five platforms from one dispatch** ([#1095](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1095)): the gate wanted five machines booted by hand, although all five targets have had native hosted runners for a while and `ci-build.yml` already exercises them. What was missing was the record: `rc-results/` is gitignored, so each verdict file existed only on the machine that produced it, and v2026.10.1 reached a point of having windows-x64 passing at one commit and linux-x64 at another, with three platforms unverified. `verify-platforms.yml` runs the same `verify_platform.sh`/`.cmd` a maintainer runs locally, on each selected target, and its summary job refuses to pass unless every requested leg reports `verdict=PASS` at the *same* commit -- the check both scripts' headers attribute to a `tools/cicd/pre_release.sh` that does not exist in this repository. Rather than copy `ci-build.yml`'s dependency setup, it revives `.github/actions/setup-objeck-deps`, a composite action nothing used and that had drifted badly enough to be a trap: it was missing `expect`, without which the debugger suite exits 0 having run nothing, `libsqliteodbc`, the GL packages, the ODBC test DSN, the Xcode selection and the entire Windows vcpkg install.
- **A checkout made on Windows cannot build the POSIX ONNX library** ([#1094](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1094)): nine paths in this repository are stored as git symlinks -- the ONNX Runtime SONAME links for both Linux architectures and the macOS LAME and SDL dylibs -- and a clone made on Windows has `core.symlinks=false`, so git materializes each as a small text file holding its target's name. `libonnxruntime.so` is 19 bytes of ASCII reading `libonnxruntime.so.1`, and `ld` reported "file format not recognized; treating as linker script" 169 seconds into a build, two minutes of which had been spent compiling `onnx.cpp`. There is nothing to fall back to, since the SONAME link is needed to load the library as well as to link it, so the gate gained a `symlinks` precondition that catches all nine in milliseconds, and `require_vendored_ort` -- which existed for this class of failure but tested for a *directory* -- now checks the library itself.
- **The gate summary read CRLF verdicts as failures** ([#1096](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1096)): the first five-leg run had every leg pass at one commit and the summary failed it, reporting `::error::windows-x64 verdict=PASS`. `verify_platform.cmd` writes its result file with cmd `echo`, so the Windows legs' files are CRLF while the POSIX legs' are LF, and `[ "$v" = PASS ]` was comparing `"PASS\r"`. The same CR made the two Windows commits differ from the three POSIX ones as strings while naming the same commit. One bug, counted three times.
- **The GC stress probe's diagnostics had never fired** ([#1086](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1086)), and the `bump-version` skill no longer tells you to advance the README download badge during a bump, which is the bug [#786](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/786) was opened to undo ([#1093](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1093)).

## [v2026.10.0] - 2026-10-02


**Any ONNX model can be run from Objeck, either in-process or on a model server, behind one interface; releases now ship a signed manifest; `System.ML` gains what it needs to evaluate a classifier under a false-positive budget; and a compiler bug produced an infinite loop at the default optimization level.**

> **Upgrading:** programs that use `API.Onnx` or `API.Inference` must add `-lib models` and `use API.Models`. The two libraries each defined their own `Tensor`; there is now one, in a library they share. Library dependencies are not transitive in Objeck, so this is a compile error until the `-lib` list is updated -- including for programs that never mention a tensor. Two helper methods containing the same construct were inlined into one shared label space, so the second one's jump resolved to the first one's label -- a backward jump into already-executed code. `-opt s3` is the default, so this needed no flag to reach. Separately, out-of-fold scores, metrics at a chosen threshold, bootstrap confidence intervals and per-group recall make the whole evaluate-under-a-budget protocol expressible without hand-written glue.

### Compiler

- **A program could compile to an infinite loop at the default optimization level** ([#1037](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/1037)): the s3 inliner advanced its label offset only when the *same* method was inlined a second time. Labels are numbered from each method's own start, so two *different* callees containing the same construct carry the same numbers -- two helpers each evaluating `->Abs()` is enough. Under one shared offset the second body's `LBL` landed on the first body's and its `JMP` resolved to the first, which is a backward jump into already-executed code. Correct at `-opt s0`, `s1` and `s2`, and unaffected by disabling the JIT, so the bad jump was in the bytecode. The offset now advances for every inlined body, and the conflict check -- which compared a candidate only against the *caller's* labels, and so could not see an inlined-against-inlined clash -- now records inlined labels too. Rebuilding all 32 libraries with the fixed compiler changed exactly one, `misc.obl`, whose committed bytecode was therefore wrong; the suite passed against it, so no exercised path looped.
- **A generic array parameter did not resolve across a library boundary** ([#998](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/998)): `Vector[]<T>` as a parameter compiled inside a library and callers in that same library reached it, but a program linking the library was told the method did not exist. `TypeParser::ParseGenerics` ended its loop on the closing `>` rather than past it, so the parameter path read that `>` where it scanned for the dimension and left the dimension at 0. `ParseType`, which decodes return types, already stepped past it -- which is why the same type worked as a return value and failed as a parameter. A reader-only fix: the `.obl` encoding was always correct.

### Runtime
- **A recovered `?->` compared wrongly against `Nil` when the comparison was written inline** ([#1067](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/1067)): `if(x?->y() = Nil)` took the wrong branch once the failing method was hot, while `r := x?->y(); if(r = Nil)` was correct in every mode -- so the idiom `?->` exists for was the one that broke, and only after warm-up, which means a program could behave correctly in testing and wrongly in production. Recovery itself was never at fault: the handler stored `Nil` and a local read it back correctly, which is what made the two spellings disagree so confusingly. `TryErrorRecovery` was ASSIGNING the handler's saved operand-stack position rather than treating it as an unwind target, so a position *above* it was raised instead of lowered and the comparison read a stale slot. The saved position is now a floor, never a value to assign. `programs/regression/try_chain_inline_compare.obs` holds both spellings to the same answer, deliberately without a JIT opt-out: the two modes agreeing is the entire property under test, so a marker there would remove the only thing it checks
- **`Try()` now recovers from an error raised inside compiled code, so the language's error semantics no longer depend on what happened to be JIT-compiled** ([#925](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/925)): #900 made `Try()` recover from a failing cast, but only where the interpreter owned the error. Inside a compiled method the cast was raised in a JIT callback, which is static, holds no interpreter, and so could not see the handler stack -- `jit_common.cpp` built a THROWAWAY `StackInterpreter` just to print a stack listing, which was the clearest evidence of it. Whether a guarded chain recovered or aborted therefore depended on how many times the failing method had been called: the same program, run twice, could do either. Demonstrated against the pre-fix build, where `try_recovers_cast.obs` aborts with exit 1 compiled and passes with exit 0 interpreted. Three changes close it. The handler stack moved from `StackInterpreter` instance fields to per-thread storage -- a `Try()` is inherently per-thread, and the hazard was that all four constructors zeroed it while `jit_common.cpp` builds interpreters at three sites on a thread already running Objeck code, so the naive move would have wiped a live handler the moment compiled code took any callback. `StackCallbackBody` returns a status instead of calling `VmExit`, and both backends test it and leave through the epilogue, where the interpreter already recovers a guard stub's status. The status is `int64_t`, not `long`: on Win64 `long` is 32 bits, so -5 reached the return register zero-extended as `0x00000000FFFFFFFB`, which the 64-bit compare reads as positive -- the branch never fired and the method carried on past a reported error with nothing pushed. The diagnostic is printed only when no handler is active, so a recovered cast is as silent as in interpreted code and stdout is identical between the two. **Compiled recursion is still fatal** and is not reachable this way: compiled frames consume the real C stack, so the process dies in the OS with no frame left to return a status from; `try_recovers_depth.obs` keeps its opt-out and says why. **A JIT-to-JIT call whose callee fails now recovers too**, which was the third and last gap: `EmitNativeCallSite` called `JitNativeCallError`, which printed and exited, so a compiled method calling a compiled method that failed could not recover even though the failing frame had already returned and the handler was intact. The call site now branches to its own teardown with the status still in the return register -- the same exit a bridge callback's status already took, so the two directions share one handler -- and `CallCompiled` reports its callee's status through an out-parameter instead of exiting. A status no `Try()` takes reaches the interpreter's own switch, which reports per status *and* unwinds, so the unguarded case gains a stack listing it never had and `JitNativeCallError` is deleted rather than left dead. Frame bookkeeping had to be fixed first and is what made the first attempt fail: the native prologue pushes a frame record, the only matching decrement was in the `RTRN` handler, and a guard stub never reaches `RTRN` -- so a natively entered method that failed left its record registered and the unwinder walked entries that were not frames. Nothing could see that until now, because recovery pops frames to the handler's depth and swept it away, and the unguarded path exited without unwinding; the pop therefore moves to the head of the teardown, the one point every exit reaches. On ARM64 that placement is forced rather than chosen -- its epilogue computes branch distances as literal constants, so a block inserted above the teardown shifts all ten of them

- **`--nursery` now reduces memory on Windows instead of being ignored** ([#841](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/841)): the young region was reserved *and committed* at its full 128 MB, so lowering the limit changed where the collector drew its boundary but not what the process charged to the system -- POSIX got this for free, because `mmap(MAP_ANONYMOUS)` commits lazily. Windows now reserves the full region and commits only the configured limit. The growth half is still open: the AMD64 JIT inlines the nursery bump, so raising the limit at runtime needs a commit check emitted into that fast path on every backend that inlines it.
- **A native library could store an object the collector tracks without a write barrier** ([#864](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/864)): a native library may store an object it *allocated* anywhere, because `AllocateObjectNative` puts those in the old generation. An object it *received* is a different matter: storing that reference without a barrier leaves the collector unaware of it, and three sites in the ONNX binding did exactly that. The VM now exposes a barrier for native code to call, `docs/native_interface.md` documents when it is required, and `tools/cicd/check_native_write_barrier.py` holds every native store to it as part of CI -- so the next missing barrier is a failed build rather than an intermittent collector crash.

### ONNX

- **`linux-arm64` ships `libobjk_onnx` for the first time** ([#1048](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/1048)): `API.Onnx` was unavailable on that platform and two regression tests skipped for it, because only an x86-64 ONNX Runtime was vendored -- `deploy_posix.sh` noted that the copies "put a 16 MB x86-64 `libonnxruntime` into the aarch64 tree", which is what the v2026.9.1 tarball carries. That was never a platform limitation: ONNX Runtime publishes an aarch64 Linux build, and one for the version already pinned, so no version bump was needed. `build.sh` and `deploy_posix.sh` already looked for it and `verify_native_libs.sh`'s own comment already said to vendor it; the directory was simply empty. The arm64 exemption is removed in the same change, because `verify_native_libs.sh` declared onnx *optional* there -- so a leg that failed to build it would still have exited 0, and a green build would have been no evidence the runtime links.
- **Any ONNX model can be run, not only the eight families with a wrapper** ([#1047](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1047)): `API.Onnx` had eleven native entry points and eight of them were per-model-family inference functions over 3,100 lines of per-family C++. `API.Onnx.Session` is the chokepoint they never had -- hand it named tensors, get the model's outputs back as named tensors -- with `GetModelInfo()` reporting the names, element types and shapes a model expects, and a dimension the model leaves symbolic reported as -1 rather than guessed at. **The caller never states an element type**: tensors cross as `Float[]` and the model's own declaration decides what each input becomes, so FP32, FP16, INT64, UINT8 and BOOL models are driven identically; an `Int[]` tensor is exact to 2^53 and refused rather than rounded past it. Preprocessing and decoding move above the native layer, into Objeck, where they can be written once and reused -- eight verticals with no chokepoint is why #1028 and #1030 each had to be fixed eight times. Verified against real models rather than argued for: the generic path and `ResNetSession` agree exactly on `resnet34` (confidence gap 0.000000), and a Phi-3 prefill runs through it with 67 inputs -- three INT64 and sixty-four empty FP16 cache tensors -- turning "The capital of France is" into "Paris". The eight per-family classes all stay.
- **A failed session was undetectable, and two families then dereferenced Nil** ([#1028](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/1028)): `onnx_new_session` set its return slot only on the success path, so a failed session returned whatever the caller had put there. It came out as 0 because `IntRef->New()` defaults to 0 -- a real failure signal resting on the caller's initialisation, which is why it went unnoticed. Nothing exposed that signal, so a dead session was indistinguishable from a live one; inference then returned without writing its result slot and YOLO and ResNet called `Set()` on the Nil. All seven session classes now answer `IsOpen()` -- `Phi3VisionSession` requires all three of its sessions, and `FaceSession` has a separate `IsRecognizerOpen()` because the recognizer is optional by design -- and both dereferences are guarded. A new regression test points a session at a path that does not exist, which is the one ONNX failure path testable without shipping a model.
- **The FP16 input tensor wrapped a buffer that had already been freed** ([#1045](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1045)): `make_tensor_match_input_type` converted into a `std::vector<uint16_t>` local to itself and returned an `Ort::Value` wrapping it. Both `CreateTensor` overloads it uses take, in the ORT header's own words, "a user supplied buffer" -- ORT wraps the pointer and does not copy -- so the vector was destroyed on return and `Run` read whatever had replaced it. The FP32 branch of the same function is correct, which is why this survived: it wraps the caller's own buffer, and only a model whose declared input is FP16 reaches the other branch. No model in this tree is FP16. The buffer is now a required non-const reference parameter, so a caller cannot forget it and cannot pass a temporary either; the signature enforces the lifetime.
- **Closing a session twice corrupted the heap, and `IsOpen()` lied after the first close** ([#1046](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/1046)): `close_session` deletes the `Ort::Session` and nulls its own *local* copy, which the caller never sees, and none of the seven per-family `Close` methods cleared the handle. So `IsOpen()` -- which #1028 had just made the documented way to tell a live session from a dead one -- stayed true forever, and a second `Close` was a double free: reproduced against `yolov11-ft.onnx` as `0xC0000374` on one run and `0xC0000005` on another, with nothing printed after it. Ten handles across seven classes now clear, and `Close` returns early on a zero handle.
- **Two session-creation failures still went unreported** ([#1044](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1044)): #1028 fixed the `catch` and missed two early refusals -- an execution provider the build cannot supply, and a CUDA build whose linked onnxruntime does not carry CUDA. Both printed a diagnostic and returned without touching the result slot, reading as failure only because `IntRef->New()` defaults to 0, which is the exact fragility #1028's own message complained about. All four exits now write it.
- **Every inference printed to the program's stdout** ([#1030](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/1030)): `common.h` wrote progress and timing to `std::wcout` at thirteen sites spanning every model family -- four behind a `// #ifdef _DEBUG` that had been commented out and never restored, eight never guarded -- plus the CoreML fallback notice in `onnx.cpp`. All now write to `std::wcerr`, where every error path in the same file already did. It matters beyond tidiness: `run_differential.py` compares stdout across optimization levels and JIT settings, so a timing value that changes run to run makes any ONNX test diverge by construction. The sixteen inert commented-out guard directives are removed with it.

### Libraries

- **One `Tensor` and one `Engine` interface, shared by both backends** ([#1062](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1062)): `API.Onnx` and `API.Inference` each defined their own `Tensor`, which was deliberate and temporary -- the generic ONNX run shipped before a shared library existed. `API.Models` (`models.obl`) now holds `Tensor`, `TensorSpec`, `EndPoint` and an `Engine` interface that `API.Onnx.OnnxEngine` and `API.Inference.HttpEngine` both implement, so calling code names a backend once rather than at every call site. **This is a breaking change**: every program linking `onnx` or `inference` needs `-lib models`, because library dependencies are not transitive -- including programs that never touch a tensor, which the regression suite established rather than the design. Merging the two tensors also removed a limit that should never have applied: `API.Onnx.Tensor` converted `Int[]` to `Float[]` *at construction* and refused anything past 2^53 there, imposing the native crossing's constraint on an HTTP server that receives integers as JSON integers with no range limit at all. Integers stay integers now, and `AsFloats()` refuses at the one boundary that cares.
- **`API.Inference`: a model server over HTTP** ([#1051](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1051)): the sibling of `API.Onnx` -- the same models, reached over HTTP rather than loaded in-process. For a model that is not an ONNX file you have locally: served from a GPU machine, a TensorFlow model you would rather not convert, or anything already deployed behind Triton, KServe, TorchServe or TF Serving. Two wire protocols, because no single one covers the field: KServe v2 (`OPEN_INFERENCE`, flat data with shape and datatype stated) and TF Serving v1 (`TF_SERVING`, where the nesting carries the shape and there is no datatype field). `Client->DescribeRequest` returns the body the client would send without sending it, which is usually faster than reading a rejection -- the two protocols shape the same tensors very differently and a tensor nested to the wrong depth looks identical in Objeck. A failed call returns Nil with the reason in `EndPoint->GetLastError()`, and a response body that will not parse is reported verbatim, since both protocols report errors as JSON and anything else means a proxy or a truncated response answered. 49 assertions, none of which need a server.
- **`API.Ollama` advertised streaming completions and had none** ([#1027](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/1027)): the bundle comment claimed support while all eight requests the library can make send `"stream": false`, with no alternative path. `code_doc` generates the published API pages from that comment, so the claim shipped to users. It now states the actual behaviour -- a request completes before returning, so a long generation produces nothing until it finishes -- which is more useful than silence, since first-token latency is most of the perceived speed of a local model.

### Machine Learning

- **Out-of-fold scores, a fold row mapping, and strata keys** ([#1035](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1035)): `CrossValResult->GetOofScores` returns one score per row from the fold model that did not train on it, which is what a threshold has to be chosen from if it is not to leak a row into its own score. `FoldSplit->GetTestRows` reports which original rows a fold's test matrix holds: `Split` fills it by walking rows in ascending order while `StratifiedKFold` returns a shuffled index vector, so pairing a score with the fold vector's k-th index silently scrambles them -- measured on a 3000-row set, a pooled AUC of 0.997 read 0.539 while every per-fold AUC still read 0.99. `Evaluate` gains an overload taking a stratification key, so folds can preserve the distribution of attack families or any other sub-population rather than only the binary label. `LogisticRegression` implements `ScoreModel`, so a linear baseline goes through the same path as the tree classifiers.
- **Metrics at a given threshold, and bootstrap intervals** ([#1036](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1036)): `Metrics->AtThreshold` returns a `ThresholdResult` -- the four confusion counts with recall, false-positive rate, precision and accuracy read off them -- for one supplied cutoff, the inverse of `RecallAtFpr`. `Metrics->Bootstrap` resamples scored instances in pairs through a `ScoreMetric` interface (`AucMetric`, `RecallAtFprMetric`, `AveragePrecisionMetric`) and returns a `ConfInterval`. A resample can draw a single class, which leaves AUC undefined; those are discarded rather than averaged in, and the usable count travels with the interval, because an interval built from a fraction of the requested draws is not the interval that was asked for. `Metrics->Percentile` is exposed for statistics the bundle does not implement.
- **Base-rate precision, label shuffling, and per-group recall** ([#1038](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1038)): `Metrics->PrecisionAtBaseRate` reprojects a measured operating point onto an assumed prevalence, with an overload taking a `ThresholdResult`; documented as a bound rather than a prediction, since it assumes both rates carry over. `Metrics->ShuffleLabels` is a seeded permutation for the shuffled-label control, returning a copy so the real labels survive to score against. `Metrics->RecallByGroup` returns a `GroupRecall` per group carrying the positive count beside the rate, and reports a group with no positives rather than omitting it. `Metrics->UnseenGroups` is the set difference for categories never trained on.

### Infrastructure

- **The release key is published where the repository cannot vouch for it, and a weekly job checks it** ([#723](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/723), phase 4): `obu` refuses a manifest it cannot verify, and its refusal message sends the reader to the public key -- which, until now, they could only obtain from the repository they were trying to verify. That proves nothing. The key is now served from <https://www.objeck.org/objeck-release.pub>, a different host from both this repository and the release assets, byte-identical to the committed copy (113 bytes, sha256 `864c6a99...6c04`). Two things already claimed this was true while it was not -- `README.md` and the message compiled into `obu` -- so a user who had just hit a verification failure was sent to a page without the key. Both now name the exact URL rather than the site, and `obu` says which copy to prefer. The published copy is also the one nothing generates: it is uploaded by hand, lives outside the repository, and so no commit-time check can see it drift -- the only thing that would have noticed a stale one is a user comparing digests, which is exactly the person a stale key misleads. `verify-signing-credentials.yml` gained a weekly `published-key` job for it, which fails on a mismatch, only warns when the site is unreachable (a site briefly down is not a credential problem, and a guard that cries wolf gets switched off), and uses `curl --fail` so a 404 body is not hashed as though it were a key -- an error page has a perfectly good digest, it is just the wrong one
- **`obu`'s install lock followed a symbolic link** ([#1075](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1075)): `.obu.lock` sits in the install root, so anything able to write there could plant a symlink at that name and `obu` would follow it -- `open` with `O_CREAT` and no `O_NOFOLLOW`, creating the target if it did not exist and holding it open for writing. Nothing is ever written through that descriptor, so the reach is creation of a path of the attacker's choosing at mode 0600 owned by whoever ran `obu`, plus an open handle: an unexpected-file-creation and denial-of-service primitive rather than disclosure or execution, and it needs an install root writable by someone other than the user running `obu` -- a shared install, or a loose umask, with `obu` then run by the victim. Measured rather than argued: without `O_NOFOLLOW` the updater suite leaves the symlink's target behind. The lock now refuses a symlink, compares the inode against the name itself with `lstat`, and reports `ELOOP` distinctly instead of announcing a tampered install root as "another obu operation is in progress". Coverity's time-of-check/time-of-use finding on the same function (CID 1680315) is a **false positive** and was dismissed as intentional: the `stat` it flags runs *after* the descriptor is open and exclusively locked and decides only whether to keep or discard it, which is the standard idiom for making `open`+`flock` safe when release unlinks. It is also load-bearing -- with a symlink planted, that re-check is what makes the case fail safe on an unhardened build
- **A substituted release manifest can no longer pass unnoticed** ([#723](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/723)): `SHA256SUMS` is served from the same place as the assets it describes, so anyone able to replace an asset could replace the manifest to match -- `obu` then verified the substituted archive against the substituted manifest and reported success. The Linux and macOS archives carry no platform signature at all, so for those the manifest was the only integrity claim, and it was unsigned. Releases now ship `SHA256SUMS.minisig`, an Ed25519 signature made in CI, and the signing step verifies its own output against the public key committed to the repository before uploading it -- a secret that did not match would otherwise be discovered by a user running `obu update`. `obu` then *requires* it: `update` and `verify` both check the signature against the key compiled into the binary before reading a line of the manifest, and a missing, unparseable, truncated or wrong signature refuses the operation rather than falling back to hashes alone. The Ed25519 verification is TweetNaCl, vendored unmodified from `tweetnacl.cr.yp.to` with its provenance recorded, because a verifier with a bug that returns true is a silent total failure -- the feature looks present and protects nothing -- and that is not a risk worth taking for code nobody here would be better at writing. It is held to behaviour in both directions by `tools/cicd/test_obu_verify.py`, against a fixture signed with the real release key: a genuine signature accepted, and a missing, tampered, truncated, unparseable or prehashed one rejected, plus a tampered trusted comment and a substituted manifest. Regressing the verifier to always return true fails nine of its ten cases, and the updater suite's own two signature cases besides. Phases 2 and 3 of `docs/release_integrity.md`, in that order deliberately -- an `obu` demanding a signature before any release carried one would have rejected every release it could see. **A release published without `SHA256SUMS.minisig` is now one no `obu` will install**, so the publish fails when the signing secret is absent unless signing is explicitly declared manual. That also makes the manifest's post-publication repair a two-part operation: Windows MSI signing happens after publication, on the machine with the hardware token, and rewrites the installer bytes, so `SHA256SUMS` is regenerated over the published assets -- which invalidates the signature made during publication. `resign-manifest.yml` signs the published manifest again, in CI, so the secret never reaches the signing machine; `update_sha256sums.ps1` and `post_release.sh` both request it and fail if it does not succeed, `post_release.sh` gate `[4b]` verifies the published signature against the published manifest using `obu` itself, and `tools/cicd/check_manifest_resign.py` fails the build if a third tool ever replaces the manifest without arranging for a signature over it. Both existing tools did exactly that, which before phase 3 was merely useless and after it would have made every signed release uninstallable.
- **The LSP offered a quick fix naming the wrong library** ([#1052](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/1052)): the class-to-bundle index is keyed by short class name and the generator emitted one JSON key per class *definition* -- duplicate keys, of which a parser keeps the last. Twenty-two names are defined in more than one bundle (`EndPoint` in four), so 433 definitions arrived as 406 and the rest were simply gone. This is an applied edit, not a hint: for `Client` the surviving entry was `Data.JSON.RPC`, so anyone who meant `API.Inference.Client` got `use Data.JSON.RPC;` and `json_rpc.obl` written into their build file, leaving the program still not compiling. Each name now carries every bundle that defines it and the editor offers one fix per candidate.
- **The quick-fix path had no coverage at all** ([#1055](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/1055)): its only test asserted that the handler *ran*, and returned an empty array -- for `Vector`, its own fixture's class. The fixture declared `v := Vector->New()`, and the native matcher needs a resolved type, which an inferred local does not have, so the test could not reach the path for any class. That is how #1052 shipped. Two fixtures now assert what the fix produces, including one for a name defined in two bundles, which fails against the old index.
- **The editor API index had silently fallen four releases behind** ([#1050](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/1050)): `objk_apis.json` is what the language server answers completions and hover from, and the copy under `clients/vscode` is packaged into the `.vsix`. Both are tracked, both are generated, and nothing regenerated or verified either -- so the index changed only when someone remembered, and nobody had. Regenerating took `API.Onnx` from 48 entries to 58: the whole generic run API shipped with no editor support, and several `System.ML` releases before it had too. `check_doc_lists.py` does not cover this and is not meant to -- a library can be in all six lists and still be absent from the index, which is the state the tree was in. CI now regenerates and compares the **class set** rather than the bytes, since the index is emitted on whatever machine the maintainer used and a guard that reddens CI over whitespace gets disabled. Its own self-test carries ten cases, each confirmed to fail against a deliberately regressed checker.
- **The generated POSIX Makefiles are build output, not source** ([#1005](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/1005)): `deploy_posix.sh` copies an architecture's Makefile over four working-tree paths, two of which were tracked, so building for one architecture left tracked files modified while the other two were not ignored and could be committed by a bulk add.
- **A missing `expect` skipped the debugger suite and reported success** ([#1022](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1022)): the runner exited 0 when `expect` was absent, so every debugger test was skipped and the leg passed. It now fails unless `OBJECK_ALLOW_SKIP=1` is set, and `expect` is listed as a test dependency.
- **A refused Coverity upload was read as a submission** ([#1031](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1031)): Coverity declines an upload with HTTP 200 and a plain-text reason, so `curl` exits 0 and `--fail-with-body` never tripped. `cov_scan.sh` printed "Submitted" over a rejected upload and then deleted the intermediate archive its own retry hint had just promised was kept. The response body is now required to say the submission succeeded.
- **The release body gate flagged real shell as an unsubstituted variable** ([#1020](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1020)): the check scanned the whole body including fenced code, so a legitimate `$PATH` in an example read as a template variable that had not been filled in.
## [v2026.9.7] - 2026-09-28

**Supervised classification becomes usable end to end, three defects that all treated a
`Bool` array as int-width are fixed, and float comparisons involving NaN follow IEEE.**
`System.ML` could fit a model but not honestly evaluate one: there was no way to hold a
scaler's statistics across a train/test split, no stratified fold, no metric that took a
score rather than a decision, and no classifier that split continuous features. All of
that is here, with a cross-validation runner over a common interface. Separately, a
`Bool[]` returned from a method was mishandled in three different places — one refused to
compile, one silently read the wrong bytes, and one aliased instead of copying. And on
Windows every comparison involving NaN returned true, so NaN compared equal to 1.0 while
being both less than and greater than it.

### Machine Learning

- **A scaler can now learn statistics once and reapply them** ([#980](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/980)): the static `StandardScaler` could only standardise whatever matrix it was handed, which cannot express the one correct thing to do across a train/test split. Scaling test data by its own statistics leaks the test distribution into the evaluation and quietly flatters any model measured at a fixed false-alarm rate. `FeatureScaler` gains `Fit`, `Transform` and `FitTransform`
- **`TableEncoder` turns a mixed `CsvTable` into a model-ready matrix** ([#981](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/981)): the value vocabulary is fixed at `Fit`, so a test split missing a category, or carrying one the training split never saw, encodes against the same columns instead of silently changing the feature space
- **`StratifiedKFold` keeps the class ratio, from a seed** ([#982](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/982)): `KFold` shuffles with the global generator and ignores labels. On imbalanced data an unseeded split cannot be reproduced, and an unstratified one lets the rare class land unevenly, so a fold may hold too few positives to measure anything at a low false-positive rate
- **Score-based metrics** ([#983](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/983)): `RecallAtFpr`, `ThresholdAtFpr`, `AucRoc`, `AveragePrecision`, `RocCurve` and `PrCurve`. The existing metrics take `Bool` predictions, which are already thresholded, and a `Bool` array cannot say what recall would be at a different false-alarm rate. Instances sharing a score share an operating point, because splitting them would invent an ordering the scores do not express
- **Classifiers that split continuous features** ([#984](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/984), [#986](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/986), [#987](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/987)): `DecisionTreeClassifier`, `RandomForestClassifier` and `GradientBoostedClassifier`. The existing trees take `Bool[,]`, so continuous data had to be quantile-binned first — and the binning discards the thresholds the tree exists to find. All three return a score rather than a label, so they can be evaluated at a chosen false-alarm rate
- **A cross-validation runner** ([#999](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/999)): `ScoreModel` is a common interface the three classifiers implement, and `CrossValidation->Evaluate` runs the folds, fitting the scaler on the training split only. `CrossValResult` reports per-fold values *and* the mean and spread, because a single fold's AUC is extremely noisy when the positive class is rare
- **Seven unguarded ratios in `System.ML`** ([#989](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/989)): two were reachable from public API with ordinary data and took the VM down — `LinearSolver->Calculate` on a target column that happened to be constant, and `Score` on a zero-row matrix in four classes, which guarded their arguments against `Nil` but not against a matrix that was merely empty

### Compiler

Three defects in one family: a `Bool` array returned from a method was treated as
int-width in three separate code paths. A `Bool[]` held in a local was correct throughout,
which is what made each of them look like something else.

- **`if(obj->Predict(x) = Nil)` would not compile when the call returned `Bool[]`** ([#985](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/985)): rejected as "Invalid mathematical operation", while the identical comparison on `Float[]`, or on a `Bool[]` local, compiled. `IsBooleanExpression` applied its array-dimension test only to variables
- **An indexed `Bool[]` call result read several bytes instead of one** ([#988](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/988)): a `Bool[]` is allocated as `NEW_BYTE_ARY`, but the path for subscripting a call's result classified it as reference width. The value then depended on neighbouring elements and on the platform's memory layout, so a *different* expression misevaluated on each backend — x64 got the left operand of `&` wrong, macOS ARM64 got `|`, Windows ARM64 got `=` against a literal. A silent wrong answer, not a compile error
- **`Bool->New[src]` aliased instead of copying** ([#995](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/995)): `BOOLEAN_TYPE` had no arm in the array copy constructor's switch, so nothing was emitted and the source reference was left on the stack as the "copy". Writing through the source then changed it
- **A library's array copy constructor crashed the compiler** ([#958](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/958)): the call was emitted with class ids local to the library being written, and a consuming build then dereferenced a map it had never heard of — a bare SIGSEGV out of `obc` with no message and no output file
- **A toolchain-version mismatch named the wrong side** ([#1010](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/1010)): the error read "the LIBRARY appears to be compiled with a different version", which sends you to rebuild libraries that are already current — the stale side is usually the reader. It now says which side is which and names both versions, after costing two separate sessions a rebuild each
- **`obc` names the library that would supply an unresolved class** ([#1015](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1015)): `Unable to resolve external library class: X; check library path` was true and unactionable -- the path is almost always right and one entry is missing from `-lib`, and finding which one meant grepping `lib_src`. The linker now scans the libraries it did not load and says `Add it with '-lib net_server'`
- **The `@web` library group never worked** ([#1014](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1014)): `-lib @web` failed on any program, including one that never touched HTTP, because the group omitted `net_server.obl` and the linker loads exactly what a group lists. The other six groups were fine
- **A `@` group expanded or not depending on where it sat in the list** ([#1016](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1016)): building a library, `-lib @std` worked and `-lib @std,misc` tried to open a file named `@std.obl`. One of the two expansion paths carried a `!is_lib` guard the other never had; both arrived in the same commit, so the difference was an oversight. Groups now expand in any position, for either `--target`, and are documented in `docs/cli_options.md` -- `obc`'s own usage had never mentioned `@` at all

### Runtime

- **Every comparison involving NaN returned true on Windows** ([#1000](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/1000)): NaN compared equal to 1.0 and was simultaneously less than and greater than it. Two separate causes in opposite directions. The interpreter's C++ is IEEE-correct, but the VM is built with `/fp:fast`, which permits assuming operands are not NaN — and since that flag is set only in the Windows project, the same program answered differently on Linux and macOS. The amd64 JIT had the opposite error for equality alone, because `ucomisd` sets the zero flag for *both* equal and unordered and only the parity flag separates them
- **The old generation moved to open addressing** ([#871](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/871)): Windows `binarytrees` went from 2.392s to 1.789s
- **Twelve library divisions aborted the VM on ordinary input**: `CsvColumn->Average(1, 1)` — the average of an empty range on a populated table — was confirmed as a live crash before anything was changed

### Updater

- **`obu verify <archive> <SHA256SUMS>`** ([#723](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/723)): the integrity check `update` already performs, exposed for anyone who downloads with `curl` instead. A mismatch and a check that could not be carried out are different exit codes, so a script can tell a wrong file from an inconclusive result. It checks the manifest's signature first, so `SHA256SUMS.minisig` must be downloaded beside `SHA256SUMS`
- **A transient lock no longer aborts an update** ([#1006](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/1006)): the swap renames the install tree aside, and Windows denies renaming a directory while any other process holds a handle inside it — antivirus scanning a binary whose bytes just changed, the indexer, a just-exited child. One attempt saw only "Access is denied" and the whole update unwound. It now retries with a short backoff. It always failed safe, restoring the tree, but the only recourse was to run it again

### Tooling

- **`refresh_deploy.ps1`** rebuilds the toolchain and refreshes `deploy-<arch>` without `devenv`, which ships only with the full Visual Studio IDE. It never deletes the tree, refreshes the `.obl` set as well as the binaries, and records what it installed with a hash per file — because a deploy binary replaced by another machine's build reporting the same version string is invisible otherwise, and invalidates every measurement taken against it
- **`audit_ratios.py`** inventories the divisions in Objeck sources and shortlists the unguarded ones. Advisory rather than a gate, and it refuses to report anything until it has classified a set of embedded fixtures correctly
- **`refresh_deploy.ps1` installs the native libraries it builds** ([#1008](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/1008)): `-Native` built the eleven native solutions and nothing copied the results into the deploy tree, so a stale `libobjk_diags.dll` carrying the old `VER_NUM` outlived a version bump and surfaced as two unrelated-looking LSP test failures. Each built `libobjk_*.dll` is now installed and recorded in `DEPLOY_MANIFEST.txt`
- **The native-staleness warning times off `version.h`'s commit, not its mtime** ([#1012](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1012)): an mtime records when the file was last written on this machine, so a clone, a branch switch or a `cp` restore reset it and all eight deployed natives read as stale. It falls back to the mtime only while `version.h` is uncommitted — a bump in progress, which is the one case the warning exists for
- **Every shipped example is compiled on each change** ([#1017](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/1017)): `programs/deploy` ships 31 programs and had no way to build them -- the only check that touched them verified an example was *listed* in the index, not that the listing worked. `build_examples.py` reads the `-lib` line `README.md` prints beside each one, so a wrong column fails the build rather than misleading the first person who copies it

## [v2026.9.6] - 2026-09-19

**A correctness release for the machine-learning library and for the two places Objeck
runs code that are not `obr`: the REPL and the embedding API.** `System.ML` returned wrong
numbers on fractional data, grouped points into empty clusters, and stopped the program
on shapes it could not combine rather than saying so. `obi` and embedded programs used
HTTP/1.1 without mentioning it, and on ARM64 `obi` aborted at the end of every run. Two
crashes that only ARM64 machines ever saw are fixed, one of them in the collector.

### Bug Fixes
- **`API.OpenAI`'s text `Respond` always returned `Nil`, and every image call sent its request as text** ([#901](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/901)): the text path posted the request and never read the reply, so the call was billed and answered with nothing. Separately, each image entry point passed its arguments in the text implementation's order, which is the only one they fit, so the picture was never sent; one of them also dropped the caller's tools. A CI check now compiles the library and follows every call an image entry point makes
- **`obi` and the embedding API had neither HTTP/2 nor HTTP/3** ([#897](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/897)): both run code through the module build, a separate compilation of the VM sources with its own macros and link line, and it never got the flags or the libraries `obr` has. Code evaluated in the REPL or embedded in a C++ program quietly used HTTP/1.1. Every CI leg now runs one program under both and fails if they disagree
- **`obi` aborted on Linux ARM64 with "double free or corruption"** ([#897](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/897)): the new parity check found it, and the cause predated the check. The ARM64 makefiles built the REPL's module with `-D_X64` and its VM without `-D_MODULE`, so the two halves disagreed about the layout of `NativeCode` and `StackProgram`: teardown read one object's field as another's and handed `free()` a pointer that was never on the heap
- **`obi` never exited when its input ended** ([#917](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/917)): piping a program into the REPL, or closing its input, left it running forever, because the input loops did not treat a failed read as end of input
- **A Nil element in a `String[]` crashed the VM inside a native library** ([#896](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/896)): `APITools_GetStringsValues` built a string from the null pointer it is handed for an unset element. ONNX session options and DeepLab labels reach it
- **ONNX refused an empty image instead of crashing** ([#908](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/908))
- **The compiler released a library file buffer with the wrong deallocator** ([#918](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/918)): `~Library` freed with `delete[]` memory that `malloc` had provided. Harmless in practice, undefined behaviour under a sanitizer or a custom allocator
- **`Try()` recovers from an invalid cast and from a full call stack** ([#900](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/900)): both ended the program even inside a guarded chain, unlike every other runtime error. `ObjInstCast` printed and exited without consulting the handler stack, and `PushFrame`'s overflow path was a bare exit; a compiled callee's guard-stub status ended the program too. All three now recover when a handler is active, so `a?->b()` yields `Nil` and the call stack is usable again. Inside JIT-compiled code both remain fatal: a failing cast is raised in a callback that cannot reach the handler stack, and compiled recursion overruns the native stack first
- **The collector could corrupt a class's closure-declaration map** ([#913](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/913)): `StackClass::GetClosureDeclarations` looked the id up with `operator[]`, which inserts a default entry when the id is absent. The collector calls it from its marking threads for every `FUNC_PARM` it walks, so two threads could insert into the same `std::map` at once and leave its tree inconsistent. Nothing failed at the time; the program crashed later at exit, in `~StackClass`, walking that map. It is a lookup now
- **`Matrix2D->SumColumn` and `AverageColumn` dropped fractions, so `LinearSolver`'s R-squared was wrong on fractional data** ([#903](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/903)): both started their accumulator as an `Int` (`sum := 0;`), so every value added was truncated -- `SumColumn` of `[1.25, 2.5]` returned 3, not 3.75. `LinearSolver` takes the mean of y from `AverageColumn`, so a fit whose true R-squared is 0.9 reported 0.924. Both accumulators are `Float` now; `ml_column_sum_fractions.obs` covers the sums and the R-squared
- **`KMeans->Group` returned empty groups, and `GetDunnIndex` then divided by zero** ([#903](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/903)): `Group` filed each record under the label of its nearest centroid, but rebuilt centroid *i* from the *i*-th entry of its groups hash table, whose order need not match the labels'. When it did not, the centroids traded places on every pass and never converged, and a pass that moved them also cleared every group, so reaching the iteration cap returned them all empty -- four points in two tight pairs grouped 2/2 with the labels `["a", "b"]` and 0/0 with `["b", "a"]`, and the three-cluster sample `programs/tests/prgm301.obs` crashed reading an empty group. Each centroid now follows its own label, and every pass reassigns the records from the current centroids. `GetDunnIndex` divides the smallest distance between groups by the largest within one, which is 0 when every group is empty or has a single record; it returns 0.0 there now, so a degenerate grouping never wins a comparison between clusterings. `ml_kmeans_groups.obs` covers both label orders, a one-iteration cap and both degenerate cases
- **`Matrix2D` operations faulted on operands they could not combine, instead of returning `Nil`** ([#903](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/903)): the native matrix code rejects a shape mismatch or an empty matrix by setting the result slot itself to `Nil`, and all 14 native-backed wrappers read their result back through that slot, so `Matrix2D->DotProduct([[1.0, 2.0]], [[3.0, 4.0]])` died dereferencing `Nil`. Each wrapper now keeps its own reference to the result holder, and returns `Nil`. A `Nil` operand got past the two-matrix wrappers' `m1 <> Nil | m2 <> Nil` guard and crashed the native code with an access violation; the guard is `&` now, and the native code treats a `Nil` matrix as empty. `Inverse` of a non-square matrix never returned, because the native code ran Eigen's `inverse()` before checking the shape; it returns `Nil` now. `NeuralNetwork->Train` returns `Nil` when an input or target is not a column of the right height, where it used to fault inside the matrix code, and `Confidence` of a wrong-shaped query is 0.0. `ml_matrix_shape_mismatch.obs` covers each case
- **`MatrixReader`'s documentation contradicted its code** ([#903](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/903)): `target_offset` is the number of target columns, taken from the end of each row, not the index of the first one; `LoadMatrices` reads only CRLF-terminated rows; and a header row is read as a record. The documentation says so now, and `ml_matrix_reader_columns.obs` pins the column split for both loaders
- **Both `NeuralNetwork` samples could not load their CSV, and faulted instead of saying so** ([#916](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/916)): `MatrixReader` reads rows through `CsvTable`, which splits on CRLF unless handed another ending, and `LoadSplitMatrices` defaults to CRLF. `programs/deploy/neural_21.obs` -- which is what a release ships -- and `programs/frameworks/ml/nn6.obs` both took that default while reading `data/gender.csv`, LF-terminated since the repository's line endings were normalized in April, so the load returned `Nil` and the next line died with ">>> Attempting to dereference a 'Nil' memory instance <<<". Nothing in CI compiles or runs `neural_21.obs`, which is how it stayed broken. Both pass the LF ending now. Each also had a `Nil` check placed after the first use of the value it was checking, so it could never fire; it runs right after the load and names the file it could not read. `ml_matrix_reader_line_endings.obs` pins which ending each entry point accepts: `LoadSplitMatrices` reads an LF file when given one, returns `Nil` on that same file under its CRLF default, and `LoadMatrices`, which takes no line ending, cannot read it at all

### Infrastructure
- **A stress test for the collector's closure path** ([#920](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/920)): the race behind #913 was found by an incidental test, and two plausible-looking attempts at a deliberate one missed the path entirely. This one reaches it about 16,000 times a run with 24 ids the class map has never seen, and runs at two nursery sizes, interpreted and with every method compiled, on both ARM64 legs of every PR
- **The offline updater suite grew from 57 to 78 checks** ([#930](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/930)): version ordering, payload integrity and interrupted updates, all without a network
- **The Coverity scan scripts refuse a capture that would be reported as falsely clean** ([#905](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/905)): `cov_scan.cmd` had not got past its version step since the day it was committed. Ten literal form-feed bytes sat where `\f` belonged, so every `findstr` path named a file that does not exist; `cmd /c` quote-stripping inside `FOR /F` then broke the version extraction and the unit count, and a count stuck at 0 means "empty capture, refuse", so every good capture would have been rejected. A crashed upload read as success, because `if errorlevel 1` does not see a negative exit status. Both scripts now compare each step's status with 0, and `COVERITY_DRY_RUN=1` stops after the capture, so it can be checked before a scan is spent on it
- **Build junk and per-user IDE files removed from the repository** ([#898](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/898)): 31 committed files and about 132k lines -- MSBuild logs and `.recipe` intermediates, three `obc -asm` dumps, Xcode UI state -- with `.gitignore` rules so they do not come back. The four `.vcxproj.user` files that hold debugger settings and the two Xcode breakpoint lists are kept on purpose. In a Windows checkout the untracked build output drops from about 230 files to 4; nothing that builds or ships changed

### Documentation
- **About 40 stale documents brought in line with the code** ([#902](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/902)): every changed claim was checked against the source, the workflows or the live site, and the code examples were compile-checked. `SIGNING.md` and `release_process.md` describe the eToken flow rather than the retired CI signing; `CI_CD.md` has the real build matrix; `architecture.md` §10 describes what exists, including that there are no exceptions and that `Try()`/`?->` recovery goes through a 16-deep handler stack; and the ONNX execution providers are documented as they ship, per platform. The pass also turned up [#899](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/899): a library built with `-tar lib` stops optimizing after dead-block elimination
- **The website's AI guide runs** ([#904](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/904)): none of its nine machine-learning examples worked. All nine now compile with `-lib ml,gen_collect,csv` -- `-lib ml` alone cannot resolve `Data.CSV.CsvTable` -- and run to exit 0 both under the JIT and with `--jit=off`, with the expected-output comments matching what they print. Eight raw generics written unescaped (`Pair->New(...)<String, String>`) were being read as unknown tags and dropped by browsers. The page is hand-uploaded to objeck.org, so it needs one upload. Three of the library bugs fixed in this release were found while writing it
- **The native-interface store rule now names the real hazard** ([#907](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/907)): it prohibited storing an object *the caller gave you* into an *array*. Both halves were narrower than the hazard -- a library's own allocations are old-generation as well, and a callback's result, or anything read out of a caller's object, is young -- so it reads: never store an object you did not allocate into an array, or into an object you did allocate. The [#864](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/864) audit classified all 356 object stores in the shipped native libraries against this rule and none breaks it, so this documents current practice rather than changing it

## [v2026.9.5] - 2026-09-19

**Linux ARM64 users: v2026.9.4's binaries stop with "Illegal instruction" on most ARM64 CPUs
(anything without SVE: Raspberry Pi 4/5, Graviton2, Snapdragon X, Apple silicon in a Linux VM).
This release fixes that.** Beyond it, a correctness and hardening release across the compiler,
the garbage collector and the roughest corners of the language. Almost none of it was found by running programs: it
was found by a heap verifier that checks the generational collector's invariants inside
stop-the-world, a differential runner that holds every regression test to the same output
at `-opt s0` and `-opt s3` under the interpreter and both JIT settings, and a program
fuzzer that compares the same generated program across those configurations. Lambdas and
enums account for most of the compiler fixes. Int arithmetic now has one definition shared
by the interpreter, `obc`'s folder and both JITs, which changes what `>>>` returns for a
shift count outside 0-63.

### New Features
- **`System.ArraySort` sorts primitive arrays in place** ([#887](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/887)): `ArraySort->Sort(a)` and `ArraySort->Sort(a, low, high)` for `Byte[]`, `Char[]`, `Int[]` and `Float[]`. `Int->Sort(v)` and its counterparts still return a sorted copy and now share this implementation. It recurses into the smaller partition and loops on the larger, so its depth is bounded by log2 of the size, and switches to insertion sort below 16 elements: sorting a million random Ints went from 89.4 ms to 75.5 ms on an Apple M4 Max. `Byte->Sort` and `Char->Sort` are now `public`, like `Int->Sort` and `Float->Sort`; before, user code could not call them

### Bug Fixes
- **Linux ARM64 binaries stopped with "Illegal instruction" on most ARM64 CPUs** ([#893](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/893)): every Linux ARM64 makefile compiled with `-mcpu=native`, and CI and the release build that platform on a server CPU with SVE2, so v2026.9.4's tarball required SVE and died at the first instruction of the interpreter's dispatch (`cnth`), `--jit=off` included, on a Raspberry Pi 4 or 5, AWS Graviton2, Ampere Altra, Snapdragon X under WSL2 and Apple silicon in a Linux VM. The build now targets ARMv8-A, which every ARM64 Linux CPU implements, with `-moutline-atomics` so atomics still use LSE where the CPU has it; `make ARM64_CPU=-mcpu=native` gives a machine-tuned local build. Measured on the build runner, six CLBG benchmarks moved by between -1.0% and +0.2%. CI now runs `obc` and `obr` on an emulated Cortex-A72 (ARMv8.0, no SVE) with the JIT off and on, so a newer instruction fails the build instead of shipping
- **`INT64_MIN / -1` stopped the program with no message** ([#828](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/828)): the interpreter, `obc`'s constant folder and both JIT folders each did the arithmetic in C++, which is undefined for overflow and for shift counts of 64 or more and faults on x64 -- `0xC0000095`, in all four `-opt s0`/`s3` x `--jit=off`/`--jit=1` modes. `core/shared/int_ops.h` now defines Objeck's Int arithmetic once -- wrapping `+ - *`, shift counts taken modulo 64, `/` and `%` truncating toward zero with `INT64_MIN / -1 = INT64_MIN` and `INT64_MIN % -1 = 0`, and a zero divisor as the only trap -- and every site calls it. AMD64's `div_imm_reg` emits `neg` or `xor` for a literal `-1` divisor, and the register and memory forms branch around `idiv`; `obc` folds both cases instead of leaving them to run time. `int_semantics.obs` checks each rule with literal operands, a runtime value against a literal, and runtime operands on both sides
- **`>>>` returned 0 for a shift count outside 0-63** ([#828](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/828)): `Int->ShiftRightUnsigned` saturated where `<<` and `>>` mask. It masks the count with 63 like every other Int shift, so `-1 >>> 64` is `-1`, `-1 >>> 100` is `-1 >>> 36`, and `-1 >>> -1` is 1. `unsigned_ops.obs` asserted the old saturation and follows the new rule. On ARM64 the immediate shift encoders took `abs()` of the count instead of masking, so `x << -1` was encoded as `x << 1` and a count past 63 overwrote opcode bits; AMD64's shift immediates mask with 63
- **A divide by zero reported differently depending on how it ran** ([#828](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/828)): the JIT paths printed ">>> Divide by zero ... in native JIT code" or "... in JIT-to-JIT call". Every path now prints exactly `>>> Divide by zero <<<`
- **`obc` crashed on a bare lambda with parameters and on a lambda nested in a lambda** ([#834](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/834)): `ParseLambda` scanned the parameter list for a trailing `~` past the scanner's three-token look-ahead, so `GetToken()` returned nullptr; and `BuildLambdaFunction` nulled the capture state after analyzing a nested body, so the enclosing lambda's next capture dereferenced null. The scanner now tokenizes past its buffer on request and restores itself, so a parameter list of any length classifies correctly -- a fixed 16-token window still rejected a bare lambda of ten parameters with "Expected '~'" -- and the capture state is saved and restored.
