Two error messages that pointed nowhere are replaced: a bare lambda whose type cannot be inferred (a ternary branch, one of several call arguments) says so and suggests `FuncRef->New(...)<R>` instead of "Invalid alias" or "Invalid lambda type", and a lambda inside `"{$ ... }"` says lambdas are not allowed in string interpolation instead of "Invalid escaped string literal"
- **A lambda could not call a method on a captured object or call a captured function** ([#836](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/836)): a lambda is compiled as a static function, so `InvalidStatic` rejected an instance method called on a captured object with "Cannot reference an instance method from this context", and calling a captured `FuncRef` or function reference failed with "Undefined function/method call" because the functional-call lookup never consulted the enclosing scope. Both receiver checks accept closure entries, and the name is resolved as a capture first. An instance method called with no receiver inside a lambda is still a compile error
- **A lambda nested in a lambda could not use a variable the enclosing lambda captured**: `obc` reported "Invalid operation using classes: Var and Int", or "Dimension size mismatch or uninitialized type", even for `p[0]`. A capture was resolved only in the symbol table of the method the lambda is written in; when that method is itself a lambda body the variable lives in the enclosing lambda's closure copy, so the lookup missed and the nested lambda declared the name as a new untyped `Var` local. Capture resolution now walks outward through every enclosing lambda, adding the closure copy to each intermediate one that does not have it
- **A lambda indexing a captured array by a variable or an expression crashed `obc`** (`0xC0000005`): the lambda-capture branch bound the variable to its closure-copy entry but, unlike the explicit-entry branch, never ran the dimension check or analyzed the indices, so the index variable had no symbol entry or type and the emitter dereferenced it. Literal indexes were unaffected, which is why `p[0]` worked and `p[i]` did not
- **`each` over an array captured by a lambda would not compile**: "Cannot reference an instance variable from this context", for every element type, while the equivalent index loop over the same array compiled and ran. A captured variable resolves to a closure-copy entry that is deliberately neither local nor static, and `each` hands the collection's entry to the analyzer directly, taking the explicit-entry path whose `InvalidStatic(SymbolEntry*)` check did not exempt closure entries as the `MethodCall` overloads already did
- **A lambda capturing an array parameter -- `Main`'s `args` among them -- crashed `obr` in every mode** ([#849](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/849)): a Nil dereference in the interpreter and `0xC0000005` under the JIT, for `args`, any String or object array parameter and any String or object array local. A lambda resolves a captured receiver as a variable, and for a class-typed array that path never pushed the instance memory the array-entry path pushes, so the inlined `$BaseArray` `Size()` popped one value too many. Int, Float, Byte, Char and Bool arrays were unaffected
- **A call used as a statement inside a lambda body crashed the interpreter** ([#850](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/850)): the lambda body was analyzed with the enclosing statement's `in_return`/`in_assignment`/expression-depth state still set, so every standalone call in the body looked like a used value and no `POP` was emitted. The lambda returned with its operand stack unbalanced -- `0xC0000005` at `-opt s0` and `s3` under `--jit=off`, hidden by the JIT's per-call stack
- **A typed lambda with a function-typed parameter would not compile**: `\((Int) ~ Int, Int) ~ Int : (fn, a) => fn(a)` failed with "Undefined function/method call: '#{L0}#(..)'". The lambda's generated method is found through a function reference that names each parameter by its type, and a parsed `(Int) ~ Int` has no name, so the parameter was dropped from the lookup key; the encoded form is passed instead. Three defects on the same path came with it: the type parser took the parameters between the last `(` and the first `)` so several function-typed parameters parsed as one and the call failed with a size mismatch (both parsers now match parentheses); a nameless nested function type was encoded without its `m.` prefix, so a call through a variable of type `((Int) ~ Int, Int) ~ Int` was reported undefined; and the `DYN_MTHD_CALL` operand counted parameters where the JIT reads operand-stack words, and a function-typed argument is two, so such a call crashed under `--jit=1`
- **A program declaring a method whose parameter is a nested function type would not load**: the VM's signature and reflection parsers skipped an encoded function type by scanning to the first `~` and then the next `,`, which for `((Int) ~ Int, Int) ~ Int` stopped inside the inner type and hit `)`: ">>> load error: Invalid method signature! <<<", even if the method was never called. Both parsers follow the parameter list to its matching `)` and recurse into a function-typed return
- **An enum or consts value could not be put in a string** ([#835](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/835)): it could be printed with `e->PrintLine()`, but `"{$e}"` and `"x=" + e` failed with "Class/enum variable does not have a 'ToString' method" and `"{$C->R}"` with "Expression type does not have 'ToString' method". The string paths, format specifiers such as `"{$e:05}"` included, now treat any enum or consts type as Int and append the integer value; consts members keep their consts type everywhere else, so library APIs that take consts-typed parameters are unchanged
- **Chained casts on an enum or consts value failed to compile or crashed at run time** ([#835](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/835)): `e->As(Int)->PrintLine()` failed with "Cannot cast a Nil return value" and `e->As(Int)->ToString()` with "Invalid cast with classes: System.Int and System.String", because the parser copies a receiver's cast onto the chained call and the analyzer then validated it against the call's return value; a scalar receiver cast is skipped there. `x := e->As(Int)->Abs()` compiled and crashed, because the emitter chose the receiver shape from the variable's class type and never pushed the scalar receiver slot. `x := C->R->As(Int)->ToString()` parsed the glued item name `C#R` as a variable, which the analyzer took for an undeclared local, so the call read no receiver. `e->As(Float)` reinterpreted the Int bits as a double -- `4.94066e-324` -- where the enum item literal path already converted
- **`Size()` and `each` on an array of enum values saw Nil, and a field's `Size()` returned garbage** ([#839](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/839)): for a receiver typed as a program, inner or library enum array, the emitter loaded it like an object array and then pushed the scalar-enum instance load again -- twice for a library enum -- so `System.$BaseArray:Size` saw Nil. The scalar-enum loads apply only on the array-entry path now, which leaves every other receiver path's emitted code unchanged
- **An array of enum values captured by a closure read garbage under allocation pressure** ([#839](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/839)): `a[i] := Color->Blue` retyped the array entry as the scalar enum, so the frame, closure-capture and instance declarations marked the array slot `INT_PARM`. The collector never traces `INT_PARM`, so the live array was freed, and `c : Color := a[i]` failed with "Dimension size mismatch or uninitialized type". Inner-enum arrays are declared as array types too
- **A method call on a string literal lost its receiver when an argument contained a call** ([#854](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/854)): `"abcdef"->SubString(3, (3 + G->Zero()))` crashed with an access violation and `"abcdef"->Get(G->Zero())` dereferenced Nil, at `-opt s0` and `s3` under `--jit=off` and `--jit=1`; `"hello"->ReplaceAll("l", P->S())` silently printed the wrong string. When the receiver is emitted before the arguments -- a string literal, an interpolated string, or the previous call in a chain -- an instance method needs each argument swapped beneath it, and the emitter keyed that swap on a counter only a string literal sets and every method call clears, so an argument containing a call dropped the swap and the call popped an argument as its instance. Chained receivers with arguments (`"abc"->ToUpper()->SubString(1, 2)`) never swapped at all. The swap is decided per call from the resolved method: for instance methods, not for the static helpers behind primitive receivers. A conditional receiver such as `(t ? "abc" : "xy")->Size()` crashed for a separate reason, fixed below ([#863](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/863))
- **A `Bool[]` field serialized to a different number of bytes on different runs**: `Bool->New[n]` allocates a byte array, but every `Bool[]` local, parameter, field and closure capture was declared as an Int array. The collector marks both alike, but the serializer sized elements at 8 bytes and read past the byte block -- a holder of a `Bool[21]` and an `Int` serialized to 129, 137, 120, 119 or 88 bytes on different runs, and 39 bytes every run with the fix -- and the heap verifier aborted every collection that found a live `Bool[]` on the declared memory type. Both declaration sites emit a byte-array declaration
- **`-opt s1` and above dropped an assignment whose value had side effects**: dead-store elimination removed an assignment to a local overwritten before it is read, together with every instruction of the statement, so `v0 := s->M(p0); v0 := 1;` dropped the call to `M` entirely; a constructor, a static function call, an integer division by zero and an out-of-bounds array read vanished the same way, and the last two stopped raising at `-opt s3`. A statement is removed only when every instruction of its value is effect-free. Found by a 5000-program differential fuzz campaign
- **`-opt s1` and above could leave operands on the interpreter's operand stack** ([#843](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/843)): a method returned its own value correctly and the caller's arithmetic then consumed the leftovers -- wrong digests, or a divide-by-zero error when a leftover 0 became the divisor. Dead-store elimination finds the start of a dead assignment by walking back over instructions of the same statement, and that walk stopped mid-expression on instructions spliced in by setter/getter inlining or made by constant propagation, which carried no statement; they now carry the replaced instruction's. Dead-code elimination and jump cleanup also dropped a conditional jump to the label right after it without popping its condition. The JIT's per-call stack hid all of it
- **A constructor call used as a statement leaked an operand-stack slot per execution** ([#848](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/848)): `Foo->New(x);` emitted no `POP_INT` after the constructor call, and a loop overflowed the stack -- `0xC0000005` under `--jit=off` at `-opt s0` and `s3`
- **`-opt s3` hung or dereferenced Nil in a method holding a function-reference local** ([#844](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/844)): the method inliner and LICM took the next free local slot from the declaration count, but a function-reference declaration occupies two slots, so the inlined temporaries of a call such as `a0->Size()` in a loop condition landed on the caller's last local -- the loop counter
- **`-opt s3` inlining could put a live array in a slot the collector never traces**: inlining added one to the id base for the callee's and/or temporary as well as the caller's, so whenever the caller had no temporary every inlined local landed one id above its declaration -- an inlined `Int[]` or object sat in a slot declared Int, an Int in a slot declared a reference, and the last local fell past the declarations. The local space also counted the temporary's word as nothing, so the frame was undercounted and the interpreter, which zeroes only the counted words of a pooled frame, left declared reference slots holding a previous frame's words. Found by the heap verifier in `gl_context_test`
- **A method with many locals corrupted the heap when it ran interpreted** (`0xC0000374`): the compiler bounds a method's local space by `LOCAL_SIZE`, but the interpreter stores local id *n* at `mem[n + 1]`, and a method holding the and/or temporary -- and/or, ternary, select, `Try`/`Otherwise`, or creating a lambda -- starts its locals at id 1. Frames were allocated at exactly `LOCAL_SIZE`, so a 96-slot method wrote two words past the buffer. The frame buffer gets the two header words every other consumer already accounts for, and LICM no longer adds a cached slot that would take a method past the limit after the compiler's check
- **A capturing lambda stored in a local or an instance field crashed on its first call** ([#845](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/845), [#847](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/847)): `0xC0000005` under the JIT, with no GC involved. Both JIT backends chose the two-word function-reference store from the first word's working-stack shape and assumed the second word had the same one. A closure built in a compiled method arrives as a closure-memory load under an immediate method id, so the immediate case emitted `movq $-120, -144(%rbp)`: the closure slot's own frame offset stored as the closure pointer, which the lambda's first call then read its capture through. Each word is stored by its own shape
- **Plain integer and float code at `-opt s3` crashed under the JIT** ([#846](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/846)): AMD64's `and`, `or` and `xor` with a memory operand built the REX prefix with the register and base swapped, unlike every other memory encoder. With a destination register in R8-R15 the REX.R bit was lost, so the result was written to RAX-RDI, and REX.B was set on the RBP base, so the operand was read through R13. With a loop local pinned in R13, `v0 xor v0` read an address well outside the frame and crashed; without a pin the same bytes silently read through the caller's R13. ARM64 loads the slot into a scratch register first and was not affected
- **`--jit=1` read a field off the stack where `--jit=off` reported a Nil dereference**: at `-opt s3` the inliner turns `s->Get()` into a field load through `s` and constant propagation forwards `s : Sh := Nil` as a literal, and the JIT's load, store and copy paths treated any non-register object operand as a frame slot and loaded `[RBP+0]`, the saved frame pointer -- so the Nil check passed and the field was read off the stack, and the program exited 0. A shared helper materializes register, memory and immediate object operands on both backends
- **A stack trace mangled a function-typed parameter**: the formatter found the end of such a parameter by counting every `m.(` in the whole signature and skipping that many `~`, then re-split the nested list at the last `(` and the first `)`, so a method with one function-typed parameter unwound as `P1->Crash(a:(a:Int, , , b:Int, c:Int, , , d:Int)~Int)` and a one-dimensional array in the middle of a list printed as `Float[,]`. Parameters, types and function types are read by recursive descent now, so traces read `d:(Int)~Int, e:((Int)~Int, Int)~Int, g:(Int)~(Int)~Int`. The debugger's frame names, in the CLI and in DAP `stackTrace`, use the same formatter
- **A young object reachable only through a closure stored in an older object was freed** ([#838](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/838)): a closure's capture block always lives in the old generation, and the minor collection descended into a dirty holder's closure only when the closure itself was young -- which it never is -- while the capture store's own write barrier dirties an untyped block the dirty scan cannot read. The collection now descends into the closure's declarations regardless of generation. A fixture storing closures that capture new objects into promoted holders reported 253 of 256 rounds bad on v2026.9.4, in all four `-opt s0`/`s3` x `--jit=off`/`--jit=1` modes
- **Strings in an array literal passed straight into a method were freed** ([#838](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/838)): the trap that copies a string array literal writes freshly allocated young Strings into an array the allocator placed in the old generation, with no write barrier. While the array lives only on the operand stack or as a callee parameter, a minor collection neither repairs its elements nor, before the callee is entered, marks them at all, so they dangled once the nursery was recycled. The fixture reported 64 bad elements on v2026.9.4 in all four modes
- **Objects were allocated at twice their field size** ([#840](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/840)): the allocator, the young-object-start test and the AMD64 JIT's inline allocator all reserved `size * 2 + header`. The doubling dates from the original 4-byte-int import and went stale when field slots became 8 bytes, so every object carried an unused slack half. All three now use `size + header`, in step with each other, since the young allocation writes that size into the block and the start test compares against it. Before the change, a private build canaried the slack half of every object and checked every young object at each collection, every old object before each sweep and everything at exit, with the full regression suite reporting no violation under `--jit=off` and `--jit=1`
- **Two objects could be lost at the very end of the nursery**: with `--nursery` at or below about 300k a layout fixture failed deterministically, from two independent faults that both need a collection to land right after a particular allocation. A zero-field object had no field word, so its address was one past its own block; as the last allocation before a collection that address equalled the nursery's end, which every half-open young test rejects, so it was never marked, promoted or forwarded and the reset nursery reused its memory. And a closure's capture block, written through the barrier before its holder exists and held only by a closure slot and the operand stack, had the captured object promoted through its own local while the block's copy was never forwarded. Zero-field objects are padded to one field word everywhere the size is computed, and a dirty capture block is scanned conservatively -- marking only validated young object starts, relocating only genuine forwarding addresses
- **A stale or interior word from a conservative stack scan could be read as an object**: the class lookup returned a nursery word's size-or-class slot as a class pointer whenever its type slot happened to read as Nil, and the young-object-start test then dereferenced it. The collector builds a sorted set of the program's real class pointers at startup and both sites check membership before use, and a young candidate must now be at least four words into the nursery, since callers read below it. Keeping an interior address on the operand stack across minor collections gave `0xC0000005` on v2026.9.4 in all four modes
- **Running out of memory during a collection left dangling references or unwound from inside stop-the-world**: a failed promotion allocation skipped the copy, so every reference to that survivor dangled once the nursery reset, and the old-generation insert, the promoted-object list and the frame list in root fixup could each throw out of stop-the-world with both collector locks held -- reported as ">>> virtual machine: out of memory". Both are fatal now: the collector prints "garbage collector: out of memory promoting a live object" and exits 1. The report is written with `fwprintf`, because the wide-stream formatting itself threw under a real memory cap
- **A frame's `self` could be overwritten with 1 during a collection** ([#820](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/820)): all four frame fixup paths, three interpreter and one JIT, relocated `self` with a raw read of the object's mark word. That word holds a forwarding address only after promotion has copied the object; before that it holds the mark bits, so a non-forwarded `self` with a non-zero word was overwritten. All four call one helper that relocates only through an address the forwarding test accepts -- aligned, inside the nursery, and a member of the old generation. This is hardening; the crash it resembles was fixed in v2026.9.3 ([#821](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/821))
- **A multithreaded program could die with SIGSEGV and no output as threads exited** ([#861](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/861)): when a thread's entry method returns, both exit paths release the top frame and store nullptr but leave the call-stack position at 0; the thread then unregisters as a mutator, so a collection on another thread stops waiting for it, while its frame monitor stays registered until the interpreter is destroyed several statements later. A collection landing in that window reached the root scan with a null current frame and dereferenced it on a mark thread. Root fixup and the heap verifier already skipped a null frame; the mark phase does now. It takes a small nursery, which makes collections near-continuous, and few cores, which keep the exiting thread descheduled: 7 SIGSEGVs in 2200 runs of the new fixture pinned to two CPUs on Linux x64 before, 0 in 2700 after. Three smaller changes harden the same handshake -- mutators join and leave the mutator set under the stop-the-world lock, instead of a store/load pair that release/acquire ordering does not make sequentially consistent; the interpreter prologue publishes the first frame before the call-stack position; and the frame slot is nulled before the monitor is registered
- **A method call on a conditional receiver crashed with no output** ([#863](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/863)): `(t ? 3 : 4)->Max(1)`, `(t ? 'a' : 'b')->ToString()` and `(t ? "abc" : "xy")->Size()->Max(1)` died with SIGSEGV at `-opt s0` and `s3` under `--jit=off` and `--jit=1`. The emitter treated every call hanging off a conditional receiver as nested, so a static `$Int`, `$Char` or `$BaseArray` helper lost the instance-memory word it pops. Only instance calls on a conditional receiver are treated as nested now. `cond_expr_receiver.obs` makes 33 checks
- **A conditional over two arrays was typed as a scalar and returned garbage** ([#867](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/867)): `(t ? p : q)->Size()` over two primitive arrays resolved to the scalar helper and returned a meaningless value with no error, and `x := t ? p : q; x[0]` was rejected. A conditional's dimension now comes from the value each branch yields, so an indexed element `p[1]` and a call such as `p->Size()` stay scalar, and branches of different dimension, such as `t ? intArray : 5`, are rejected with `'?' invalid type mismatch`; Nil stays assignable to either kind. `cond_expr_array_type.obs` covers six element types and keeps an array alive only through a conditional-typed local across a minor collection
- **A variable captured by a lambda could not be interpolated into a string** ([#865](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/865)): `"n={$n}"` on a captured `n` failed with "Undefined variable" and warned that `n` was unreferenced, while `"n=" + n` compiled. A lambda's closure copy of a captured variable is created on its first reference and registered where a direct symbol lookup never looks, so the interpolation path missed a copy that already existed, and missed outright when it was the reference that should create it. Interpolation now resolves the name as a capture first, through the same lookup-then-create path as any other variable reference
- **`each` over a `Range` captured by a lambda would not compile, and fixing that exposed a frame-slot clobber** ([#866](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/866)): the loop did not recognize a captured range and reported "Undefined function/method call: Size(..)", for the same lookup reason as the interpolation fault above. Once recognized, the loop used the variable's own id as the frame slot holding the range, but a closure copy's id indexes closure memory, so the loop overwrote an unrelated local of the lambda -- seen as a Nil dereference. A captured range gets its own frame local; a local range variable keeps its slot, and all 290 existing regression tests compile to identical bytecode. `closure_capture_resolution.obs` covers both sites, every interpolated type, captured parameters, capture through two lambdas, `IntRange`, `CharRange` and `FloatRange`, and a minor collection between building each closure and calling it
- **Sorting a primitive array could end the program** ([#887](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/887)): `Int->Sort`, `Float->Sort`, `Char->Sort` and `Byte->Sort` partitioned on the middle element and recursed into both sides, so recursion depth followed the input. The VM's call stack is 256 frames, and a 600-element permutation whose middle element is the smallest of every range it lands in drove it 599 deep: ">>> call stack bounds have been exceeded! <<<" and exit 1, on every platform, at `-opt s0` and `s3`, interpreted and JIT-compiled. The same shape, without even a bounds guard, sorted user data in `ml_tree`'s split values and `ml_neighbors`' KD-tree build. All of them now bound their depth; `sort_primitive_arrays.obs` and `ml_sort_depth.obs` carry adversarial inputs that failed before in all four configurations
- **A runtime error or `Runtime->Exit` crashed the VM while another thread was running** ([#877](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/877)): the program printed its message and then died with SIGSEGV, exit 139 instead of 1 or the requested status, on Linux x64, Linux arm64 and Windows x64 in both JIT modes, whenever one other thread was busy. `exit()` runs the process's teardown on the calling thread while the others keep executing: static destructors freed the collector's old-generation set under a thread allocating into it, and on Windows the static C++ runtime freed locale state under a thread formatting a number. While another thread is registered, the VM now flushes its streams and ends the process without teardown; single-threaded programs exit exactly as before. `exit_with_live_threads.obs` calls `Runtime->Exit(0)` from a thread while two others work, since a negative test would accept the crash
- **A thread join could wait on a handle that was never a thread** ([#874](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/874)): `Join` copied the `Thread` object's address into a local, parked the thread so collections could proceed, and only then read the handle out of it. A minor collection in that window promoted the object and reset the nursery, the local then named whatever reused that memory, and `pthread_join` was handed a small integer: ">>> Unable to join thread! <<<", `ESRCH`, once in a few hundred runs of `gc_mt_small_nursery_stress` on macOS arm64 CI. The handle is now read before parking, in the interpreter and the JIT. A forced-window probe that failed 24 of 24 runs before the fix failed 0 of 24 after
- **A closure's captured values could be freed while still reachable** ([#881](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/881)): a typed function-reference root traced a closure's captures only when its own mark set the capture block's mark bit. A conservative scan of a stale stack word could mark the same block first without tracing it, and the JIT-roots scan runs on its own thread beside the interpreter-frame walk, so when it won the race a capture reachable only through the block was swept. Exactly one typed walker now traces each block per collection, whoever marked it. The heap verifier found it on linux-arm64, whose JIT scans the words below the frame; forcing the conservative scan first reproduced it on every run before the fix and on none after

### Performance
- **`Data.CSV` medians sort a `Float[]` in place** ([#892](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/892)): `CsvRow->Median` and `CsvColumn->Median` boxed every value into a `FloatRef` and sorted a `CompareVector`; they now copy into a `Float[]` and sort it with `ArraySort`. 50 medians of a 20,000-value column went from 1.48-1.90 s to 0.13-0.29 s on Windows x64, with the same result every run. `csv_median.obs` is the first test to cover either method

### Infrastructure
- **A heap verifier for the generational collector** ([#832](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/832)): `OBJECK_GC_VERIFY` checks the collector's invariants inside stop-the-world -- remembered-set membership before a collection, object headers, typed edges, the absence of typed nursery references and typed frame slots after it, unforwarded young objects during fixup, and with `OBJECK_GC_VERIFY=checkmark` a typed trace around the minor mark phase. Cheap checks run every collection and the full old-generation walk runs for the first 8 and then every Nth, backing off when verification exceeds about 20% of wall time. Only typed references are trusted: never operand stacks, JIT temp windows or pending-thread roots. `OBJECK_GC_VERIFY_INJECT` plants a corrupt promoted field, a skipped write barrier or a mark bit ORed into a young object's field, so the verifier can be shown to fail, and reports name the invariant, object, class, field, value and collection. With the variable unset each hook is one predictable branch. It found the inliner slot defect and the `Bool[]` declaration above
- **Every regression test must print the same thing in every VM mode** ([#829](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/829)): `run_differential.py` compiles each runtime test at `-opt s0` and `s3` and runs `s0/off`, `s3/off`, `s3/default` and `s3/jit1`, requiring identical stdout and the same zero/non-zero exit status. A first run on v2026.9.4 found 7 divergent tests, none of them a VM bug: timings, spin counts and GC and entry counts moved to stderr, and the two that differ by design carry markers. `check_diff_markers.py` requires a reason after each coverage-reducing marker and caps non-deterministic-output opt-outs at 5% of the suite
- **A differential program fuzzer** ([#837](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/837)): `tools/fuzz/gen.py` generates deterministic Objeck programs from a recorded choice stream over swarm-sampled feature layers, `run_fuzz.py` compares `s0/off` against `s3/off`, `s3/default`, `s3/jit1` and `s0/jit1`, and `reduce.py` shrinks a finding's choice sequence so every candidate stays a valid program. A `faults/` directory holds a deliberately broken `obc` and `obr` used to prove findings are caught. It found the `-opt s3` function-reference slot miscompile ([#844](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/844)), the interpreter-only dead-store divergence ([#843](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/843)), the zero-argument lambda JIT crash ([#845](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/845)) and the AMD64 REX-prefix crash ([#846](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/846)). JIT coverage is measured from positive evidence -- the VM reports each method it compiles, and each compile with inlined callees on AMD64 -- because subtracting rejected methods from the total scored a VM that ignored `--jit=1` at 100%
- **An EMI mode for the fuzzer**: `tools/fuzz/emi.py` mutates regression tests that compile and exit 0 -- inserting dead code, deleting unexecuted blocks, applying runtime identities -- into variants that must print the original's `s0/off` output, and compiles and runs each at `-opt s0` and `s3` under `--jit=off` and `--jit=1`. Guards read a static set from `args` so `-opt s3` cannot fold them, and unexecuted blocks are found with a stderr probe build rather than coverage tooling. A variant whose configurations agree with each other but not with the original counts as a divergence, which is what catches a front-end miscompile that is wrong everywhere; it found the string-literal receiver defect ([#854](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/854))
- **A nursery size knob and GC exit statistics** ([#831](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/831)): `--nursery=<n>(k|m)`, or `OBJECK_NURSERY` with the flag winning, sets the young-generation limit between 64k and 128m; anything else is an error naming the range. The full region stays reserved and only the limit both bump paths compare against moves, and an object larger than the limit goes straight to the old generation instead of forcing a collection per allocation. `runtime.memory.peak`, `runtime.gc.nursery.capacity` and `runtime.gc.promoted.bytes` are new, pauses are timed from taking the sweep lock until the world resumes so the stop-the-world handshake and the minor dirty-list scan are included, and `OBJECK_GC_STATS=1` prints one line to stderr at exit with minor and major counts, pause percentiles, promoted objects and bytes, and peak RSS. Small nurseries are what exposed the nursery-end losses and the thread-exit race above
- **A nightly hardening workflow** ([#833](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/833)): on the five CI platforms, against the latest green master artifacts -- differential regression, the fuzzer, the regression suite under the heap verifier in minor stress (a 256k nursery) and major stress (a 64k threshold), and GC stress loops in both JIT modes. `tools/cicd/nightly_triage.py` wraps each step, fails on a missing tool or knob instead of skipping, and classifies results as infra, known or new; a killed step is a new failure, not infra. The first run reported 33 new signatures for what was two test-output bugs, one forced-threshold cost and a handful of real findings, so collection-timing counters moved to stderr, two opt-out markers were added with a lint that requires each one's reason to name the run whose log shows the failure, and the triage now opens an issue only for a failure new on more than one leg or one that is a crash or a verifier violation, listing the rest in a single tracking comment
- **A stress-probe workflow**: `stress-probe.yml` downloads the platform trees from the latest successful build on a branch and loops ten GC and JIT fixtures on all five CI platforms in four modes -- default, `--jit=1`, a 256k nursery, and the heap verifier with a 256k nursery. `tools/cicd/stress_probe.py` counts failures per fixture and mode, saves the output of the first three, fails on any failure or a missing VM knob, and writes a summary with the rule-of-three bound. Run counts are measured per cell. It found the thread-exit race ([#861](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/861))
- **Negative regression tests name the error they expect** ([#830](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/830)): the compile- and runtime-error markers count only at the start of a line in both runners and in the manifest generator, so a comment mentioning a marker no longer makes a test negative, and `# EXPECT_COMPILE_ERROR: <message>` requires that message in the compiler output. Each of the 19 compile-error tests states a substring of the error it expects; one of them had been passing on the wrong error. `OBJECK_VM_ARGS` passes extra `obr` options to every test in both runners, and `check_test_exit_codes.py` rejects marker lines the two runners cannot honour identically
- **A test that guards object layout against the allocation size**: `obj_size_layout.obs` builds the shapes most likely to disagree with a class's instance size -- a zero-field class, a Float last field, a function reference last field, a library-class parent, a closure capture held in a field, a serialization round trip with a trailing function reference, and a native crypto call storing into a holder -- keeps them alive through minor and major collections, and asserts that collections and promotions actually ran. It is what caught both nursery-end losses once objects lost their slack half
- **The macOS deploy builds the verifier, and stops when a toolchain build fails**: the new verifier source was added to the Visual Studio projects and the arch makefiles but not to the Xcode projects macOS builds `obr`, `obd` and the module library from, so all three failed to link -- and the deploy's "Build toolchain" step still ended green, because the script had no `set -e`; the missing `obr` surfaced only in the smoke test. The Xcode projects compile it, and every toolchain build and product copy in the macOS deploy exits on failure
- **The benchmark harness and the published numbers use the same inputs**: the harness ran `binarytrees` at depth 21 where every table in `docs/performance.md` documents 17, roughly a 16x difference in work, so any number it produced would have been silently incomparable to the page it was going into. `tools/cicd/check_benchmark_inputs.py` parses the harness's benchmark arrays and every table that names one of them and fails when a shared benchmark carries different inputs. It also checks the benchmark set, so a benchmark on disk that the harness never runs and the documentation does not exclude is caught, and it runs in CI with its unit tests. `perf-results/objeck_bench.py` is the cross-platform driver that produced this release's baselines: interleaved runs, run counts scaled so each benchmark gets at least 10 s per build, peak RSS on all three platforms with the source of each value recorded, and a `stress` mode that refuses to run without CPU affinity rather than report a clean result that could not have failed
- **The thread-exit race loop oversubscribes where it cannot pin** ([#861](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/861)): the race needs more runnable threads than CPUs. The VM flag tests pinned the fixture to two CPUs with `taskset`, which macOS and Windows do not have, so there the loop ran sequentially and could not have failed at any run count. `run_vm_flag_tests.py` now runs `ceil(cpus * 3 / 8)` concurrent copies of the eight-thread fixture instead. On macOS arm64 that reproduced the race 11 times in 10,000 runs before the fix and 0 in 10,000 after
- **A failed thread join says why** ([#874](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/874)): ">>> Unable to join thread! <<<" was printed with nothing else, once on macOS arm64 CI, and the return code that separates the causes was discarded. Both join paths, interpreter and JIT, print a second line with the `pthread_join` result and its `strerror` text -- on Windows, the `WaitForSingleObject` result and `GetLastError` -- plus the thread handle and the `Thread` object's address. The first line is unchanged, so existing log matching still works
- **A test that prints a failure and then returns is caught before it is written**: `tools/cicd/check_test_exit_codes.py` required a test that prints FAIL to contain an exit call somewhere. `odbc_sqlite_test` had one and was still silent: the branch that found no database printed `FAIL: Cannot open connection` and returned, and a bare return from Main is exit 0, which both runners score as a pass, so on every machine without the SQLite ODBC data source it ran no check and counted green ([#819](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/819) fixed that test). A FAIL print whose next statement is a bare `return;` is now an offense, and the report names both remedies: exit non-zero when the branch is a failure, or print a line starting with `SKIP:` and return when the machine lacks a driver, data source or service, which both runners record as a skip. Tests marked `# EXPECT_RUNTIME_ERROR` are exempt, since the runner fails those on exit 0
- **`core_odbc` no longer reads as ODBC coverage**: only `Connection`, `ParameterStatement` and `ResultSet` call into `libobjk_odbc`, so a test of `Date`, `Timestamp` and `ColumnInfo` never loads it, yet its header called it "Core ODBC Bindings Test". `odbc_sqlite_test` is the only test that calls into the binding, and its data source exists only on the two Linux legs
- **The heap verifier no longer reports a dead closure as a lost capture** ([#880](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/880)): its B3 check assumed every surviving closure capture block was live, and reported one that still named a nursery object as a promoted capture left unforwarded. On linux-arm64 about 2% of verified runs of `closure_block_body` tripped it, and the cores showed a garbage block kept alive only by a stale JIT stack word, whose capture had been correctly freed. The capture's old header now decides: a forwarding address is still a violation, anything else is a warning. Reintroducing the original fixup bug still produces B3 violations in every affected configuration
- **The rule behind 37 allocation sites is written down**: allocating can run a collection, which moves young objects and resets the nursery, so a GC pointer held in a C++ local goes stale across it. Nearly every trap allocates with collection disabled while such a pointer is live; `memory.h` now says so next to the allocators, and the one trap that did not, `LoadMultiArySize`, follows it. An audit of all 21 park and safepoint sites found them already safe

### Documentation
- An Integer Arithmetic section: wrapping `+ - *`, shift counts taken modulo 64, truncating `/` and `%` with `INT64_MIN / -1 = INT64_MIN`, and a zero divisor as the only trap
- What a lambda can capture: that captures are copied when the lambda is created, that objects are therefore shared by reference, that a lambda may call methods on captured objects and call captured functions, and that `self`, `@field` and receiver-less instance calls are not available in lambda bodies yet
- Performance baselines in `perf-results/baseline-2026.10.0/`: master against v2026.9.4 on macOS arm64, where no benchmark is more than 5% slower at p<0.01 and `bench_gc_large_heap`'s peak RSS falls 26.6% with [#840](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/840), and the [#841](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/841) nursery-size sweep on macOS arm64, Linux x64 and Windows x64. The default nursery stays 128m: 64m cut peak RSS on macOS but made `binarytrees` 10.5% slower on Linux x64 and 25.8% slower on Windows x64, where promotion costs more ([#871](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/871))
- `docs/PLAN_2026_10_0_HARDENING.md`, the hardening plan this release follows ([#842](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/842))

## [v2026.9.4] - 2026-09-15

A corrective release for the compiler: two optimizer rewrites computed some integer
divisions wrong at the default optimization level, with no error. Programs compiled with
v2026.9.3 or earlier at `-opt s2` or `-opt s3` (the default) should be recompiled.

### Bug Fixes
- **Integer division by a power of two rounded the wrong way at `-opt s2` and `-opt s3`**: strength reduction replaced `n / 2^k` on a local with an arithmetic right shift. A shift rounds toward negative infinity and integer division truncates toward zero, so a negative dividend that was not an exact multiple came out one too low: `-7 / 2` gave `-4`, `-1 / 1024` gave `-1`. Division is no longer rewritten; both JITs already compile a constant divisor to a biased shift or a multiply. Multiplication by a power of two is unaffected. The interpreter and both JITs agreed on the wrong value, so no mode comparison could see it; `opt_int_division.obs` checks hand-computed quotients at every level
- **`1 / n` evaluated to `n` at `-opt s3`**: a peephole pattern meant for `x / 1` matched `LOAD_INT_LIT 1; DIV_INT`, but the value pushed last is the left operand, so it removed the division from `1 / n` instead. The pattern is gone
- **The compiler crashed folding `INT64_MIN / -1`**: constant folding at `-opt s1` and above divided the two literals in C++, which faults on x64 (`0xC0000095`), so `obc` exited with no message and no output file. The fold is skipped and the division is left to run time, as for a zero divisor
- **Deserializing an object array with a Nil element corrupted the enclosing object**: the deserializer wrote each Nil element into the object's next field slot instead of into the array, so the array field itself read back as Nil and every later field shifted. `serial_nil_array_element.obs` round-trips an object with Nil elements between its fields

## [v2026.9.3] - 2026-09-14

### Bug Fixes
- **macOS: `obr` could not start on a user's Mac** ([#812](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/812)): every macOS release since v2026.6.3 linked Homebrew's GnuTLS, nghttp2, nghttp3 and ngtcp2, plus an ngtcp2 GnuTLS backend that Homebrew does not ship, which the build machine had built by hand. On any other Mac `obr` stopped at launch with "Library not loaded: @rpath/libngtcp2_crypto_gnutls.8.dylib", even after the release notes' `brew install`. HTTP/3's TLS moves from GnuTLS to AWS-LC, and `obr` links AWS-LC, ngtcp2, nghttp3 and nghttp2 statically on Linux and macOS, built by `tools/deps/build_quic_deps.sh` from pinned versions. A Linux archive no longer needs those libraries from its distribution. Release builds install the macOS package on a clean runner, with Homebrew moved aside, before anything is published
- **macOS: the OpenCV, ONNX and LAME bindings needed Homebrew** ([#817](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/817)): v2026.9.2's `libobjk_opencv` and `libobjk_onnx` linked Homebrew's `opencv@4` and `onnxruntime`, and `libobjk_lame` its `lame`, so on a Mac without those formulas they failed with "Library not loaded". The package now carries every library it uses. `tools/deps/build_macos_deps.sh` builds OpenCV 4.12.0, mbedTLS 3.6.4 and libiodbc 3.52.12 as static libraries and LAME 3.100 as a shared one (it is LGPL), and fetches Microsoft's ONNX Runtime 1.30.0, each pinned by version and SHA-256. `libonnxruntime.1.dylib` and `libmp3lame.0.dylib` ship in `lib/native`, and the five licenses in `doc/licenses`. The OpenCV and ONNX bindings build with CMake (`core/lib/opencv/macos/CMakeLists.txt`), and the vendored mbedTLS and libiodbc archives leave the repository. The package runs on macOS 13.3 or later, and the ONNX binding on macOS 14; the app's `LSMinimumSystemVersion` had said 11.0
- **Linux and macOS: the libraries inside `obr` shipped without their licenses** ([#818](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/818)): since [#812](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/812), `obr` carries AWS-LC, ngtcp2, nghttp3 and nghttp2, and no archive included their license texts. `tools/deps/build_quic_deps.sh` installs each library's license files from its pinned tag, and both deploys ship them in `doc/licenses`. Rebuilding the QUIC libraries no longer deletes the macOS bundled libraries that share their directory, and on macOS they target 13.3 when `MACOSX_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET` is unset; a build by hand had produced archives for the build machine's own macOS
- **ODBC: `Connection->GetLastError()` was empty for a connection that did not open** ([#819](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/819)): a failed connect freed the handle that held the driver manager's diagnostic, so the reason -- a missing data source, a driver that would not load, a rejected login -- never reached the program. The binding keeps it, per thread, and returns it, e.g. `[IM002] ... Data source name not found`
- **ARM64: a collection could corrupt an object while compiled code ran on another thread** ([#821](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/821), [#816](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/816)): the collector marked every word its conservative scans handed it that pointed into the nursery, without checking that the word was the start of an object. A stale or interior pointer, which ARM64's compiled code leaves in the stack slots those scans read, had the mark bit set in whatever word lay below it. For a map's tree node, that turned an empty `@right` into 1, and a later lookup crashed on it: silently or as "Invalid object cast" on Windows arm64, SIGSEGV on macOS and Linux arm64. It took compiled code, a second allocating thread and frequent collections; a map-insert loop built that way failed about 1 run in 6 on Linux arm64. The collector now marks a nursery address only when it is a real object's start
- **Linux: `install_deps.sh` offered only SDL2** ([#809](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/809)): a Linux archive needs its distribution's runtime libraries -- mbedTLS, readline, OpenCV, LAME, ODBC and SDL2 among them -- and the script shipped inside it installed SDL2 alone, so the other bindings failed to load on a fresh machine. It now maps every library the binaries link to its package (`ldd`, soname, package) and installs the whole runtime; `--check` reports what is missing

### Infrastructure
- **A regression test that did not run no longer passes** ([#819](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/819)): `odbc_sqlite_test` printed "FAIL: Cannot open connection" and exited 0 wherever its SQLite data source was missing -- macOS, both Windows legs and most developer machines -- and both runners counted that as a pass. A missing data source is now a skip that names the reason, and any other connect failure fails. `http_persistence_test` starts on a random port and waits for a real connect, instead of a fixed port and a fixed sleep
- **The macOS deploy holds every Mach-O file to macOS 13.3** ([#817](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/817)): a library built for a later macOS does not load on an earlier one. The deploy reads the `minos` of every file in the tree and of every member of the static archives it links, and fails on one above 13.3, ONNX Runtime's 14.0 excepted; an archive built for a later macOS links with only a warning, and the binary still reports 13.3. Every build is given the deployment target and the dependency prefix explicitly. `create_macos_pkg.sh` creates its `OUTPUT_DIR`
- **macOS bindings carry `@rpath` install names, and the deploy checks them** ([#811](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/811)): v2026.9.2's bindings shipped with whatever install name their builds set, two of them the CI runner's build directory and `libobjk_ml`'s an Xcode template's `/usr/local/lib/libxcode.dylib`; only `libobjk_sdl` had been corrected. All eight are rewritten to `@rpath/<name>` and re-signed, and the deploy fails if one is not. Loading was never affected, since the VM opens a binding by absolute path
- **The macOS deploy checks where every library in the tree resolves** ([#815](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/815)): v2026.9.2's `obr` found an ngtcp2 backend that Homebrew does not ship through its only `LC_RPATH`, `/opt/homebrew/lib`, and the deploy's checks since -- `obr`'s absolute links ([#812](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/812)) and the bindings' install names ([#811](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/811)) -- do not read rpaths. `tools/cicd/check_macos_tree_links.sh` follows every `LC_RPATH`, `@rpath`, `@loader_path`, `@executable_path` and absolute link of every Mach-O file in the finished tree, the app launcher included, and the deploy fails on one that resolves outside the tree or macOS. Now that those libraries ship in the package, nothing in the tree is allowed an outside link, in the deploy or in the install tests
- **Release Build dry runs cannot publish** ([#808](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/808)): dispatching Release Build from master published a release, and its docs job pushed `api.zip` to master from any ref. Publishing and the `api.zip` push now happen only for a release tag, so a dispatched run is a safe dry run
- **One verification script per machine** ([#814](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/814)): `tools/cicd/verify_platform.sh` and `verify_platform.cmd` build a clean checkout the way the release does, run the regression suite with the default JIT and with every method compiled plus the VM flag, debugger and DAP tests, and write a PASS/FAIL result file; a step that skips counts as a failure. `tools/cicd/check_docs_deps.py` fails CI when install instructions or the shipped binaries disagree with `core/release/runtime_deps.json`, and a failed master or release build opens a tracking issue
- **`http_error_body` no longer flakes** ([#810](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/810)): the test's own server wrote the error body and closed the socket at once, so the client sometimes read a reset instead of the body. It closes gracefully, listens on a random port and waits until the server is ready

## [v2026.9.2] - 2026-09-13

A corrective release for v2026.9.1's native libraries: macOS ships OpenCV and ONNX
again, Linux x64's ONNX loads without `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`, and Linux ARM64 no longer
carries an x86-64 ONNX Runtime. Underneath all three, the deploys verified what they
built and ignored the answer. They now stop instead of shipping incomplete -- on a
missing native library, and on a missing file an example opens by name -- and the
deploy scripts say what they are doing while they run.

### Bug Fixes
- **macOS: `libobjk_opencv` and `libobjk_onnx` missing from v2026.9.1** ([#804](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/804)): Homebrew's default `opencv` formula is now OpenCV 5.0, which moved `contourArea`, `boundingRect`, `getRotationMatrix2D`, `approxPolyDP`, `estimateAffinePartial2D` and `LMEDS` out of `cv::`. CI had been pinned to `opencv@4` and `release-build.yml` had not, so the release built neither binding and published without them. The release workflow now installs `opencv@4` as well
- **Linux x64: `libobjk_onnx.so` loaded only with `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`** ([#804](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/804)): it was linked with no RUNPATH, so the `libonnxruntime.so.1` shipped beside it in `lib/native` was never found unless the environment pointed there -- which CI's does and an untarred release's does not. `core/lib/onnx/eq/build.sh` links with `-Wl,-rpath,'$ORIGIN'`
- **Linux ARM64: an x86-64 ONNX Runtime in the tarball** ([#804](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/804)): only `cuda/lib/x64` is vendored, so the ARM64 link failed and `deploy_posix.sh` still copied the 16 MB x86-64 `libonnxruntime` into the aarch64 tree. ONNX is built only for an architecture with a vendored runtime, and is declared optional on ARM64
- **`gl_crystal.obj` never shipped** ([#803](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/803), [#805](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/805)): `gl_model.obs` loads it by default and every deploy script copied it, but `.gitignore`'s `*.obj` (meant for MSVC object files) kept the Wavefront model out of git, and no deploy checked a copy. Committed as a hand-written quartz crystal, with `!programs/examples/*.obj` so the next model is not hidden the same way
- **`Image->Normalize()` could write past its output array** ([#806](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/806)): `opencv_normalize` sized the float array as `float_img.rows * float_img.cols * 3`, computed in 32-bit `int` before widening to `size_t`. Above about 716 megapixels the product wraps: a negative size (allocation failure) up to about 1.43 gigapixels, and a too-small array that the fill loop overruns -- an out-of-bounds write on the VM heap -- beyond that. The size is computed in `size_t` (CodeQL alert #362, `cpp/integer-multiplication-cast-to-long`)

### Infrastructure
- **Deploys act on native-library verification** ([#804](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/804)): `verify_native_libs.sh` ran on the POSIX deploys without `|| exit 1`, so v2026.9.1 logged "native-library verification failure(s)" on three platforms and the jobs stayed green. The result now fails the deploy; each platform declares its required libraries, and `--optional` names one a platform cannot build yet (reported as a warning and a GitHub annotation). CodeQL installs `libopencv-dev` and `libmp3lame-dev`, so the OpenCV, ONNX and LAME bindings are built and analyzed
- **Deploys fail on a missing example asset** ([#805](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/805)): `core/release/verify_example_assets.sh` (POSIX) and a matching check in `deploy_windows.cmd` fail when `README.md`, `data/gender.csv`, `data/weather.json`, `opengl/cube_gl.obs`, `opengl/gl_crystal.obj` or `opengl/gl_model.obs` did not land. The MSYS2 UCRT and Clang deploys now copy the OpenGL examples, the examples README and `data/`. `.gitignore` re-includes `programs/deploy/data/`, which `data/` hid at any depth
- **Deploy script presentation** ([#801](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/801)): `ui.sh`, `ui.cmd` and `ui.ps1` give `deploy_posix.sh` and `deploy_windows.cmd` a banner, live stage progress and quiet tool output, with the tail of the log shown for a failed stage and `-v`/`VERBOSE=1` to stream everything. The live line reads correctly in VS Code and Windows Terminal -- one solid bar coloured by finished stages and a stage count, where a block-and-shade bar looked full at 6% and a percentage sat still through the multi-minute solution rebuild -- and falls back to ASCII in a plain console; its activity text names the project being built instead of MSVC's chatter
- **Generated-template guard** ([#802](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/802)): the `search_index.json` validation had been added only to `core/release/code_doc64.cmd`, which `update_version.ps1` regenerates from `code_doc64.in` on every Windows deploy, so it never ran. It is ported into the template, and `tools/cicd/check_version_templates.py` fails CI when a generated file and its template disagree

### Documentation
- The definitive v2026.9.1 benchmark run, with `fasta` measured for the first time ([#800](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/800))
- `docs/ML_TABULAR_GAPS.md`: what a tabular classification study needs from `System.ML` ([#798](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/798))

## [v2026.9.1] - 2026-09-12

Compiled code now calls compiled code directly -- a bound call 26.5 ns to 5.5 ns,
a `virtual` call 125 ns to 6.0 ns, `Fib(32)` 0.208 s to 0.043 s -- and three ways
compiled code could corrupt memory are closed. Every string hash was wrong; JIT
arithmetic now matches the interpreter, and the JIT is 2-8x faster on the loops
programs actually write; the debugger sees threads and tells the truth about what
its commands do; `Game.OpenGL` gains a sky, rotations, normal maps and emissive
materials; and the release process reports what it actually did. Found by running
the v2026.9.0 release and then surveying every binary and library for the bugs a
compiler, VM and debugger platform cannot carry.

### At a Glance

The largest entry in this changelog by some distance — **100 bullets across 26
issues and pull requests**, against 42 for the next biggest (v2026.8.2) — and
weighted heavily toward correctness rather than features.

| Area | Count | The short version |
|---|---:|---|
| Bug fixes | 44 | Every string hash was wrong. Three ways compiled code could corrupt memory. Nine debugger commands reported success while doing the wrong thing. |
| Virtual machine | 25 | The F7 calling convention, jump tables for a dense `select`, checked VM flags, runtime properties and locales, POSIX exit codes. |
| Infrastructure | 11 | Libraries rebuilt by their own compiler, the every-method-compiled CI pass on all five legs, release gating that fails instead of skipping. |
| New features | 6 | Threads the debugger can see, `Game.OpenGL` sky/quaternions/normal maps/emissive materials. |
| Compiler | 6 | Short-circuit conditions, name resolution that stops copying the library maps, the func-ref inliner fix. |
| Testing | 3 | A guard on the headline performance number, and fixtures for the fixes above. |

**Performance.** A bound call went from **26.5 ns to 5.5 ns**, a `virtual` call from
**125 ns to 6.0 ns**, and `Fib(32)` from **0.208 s to 0.043 s** on AMD64 (17.1 → 7.4 ns
and 0.135 → 0.050 s on an M4 Max). Measured end to end on the benchmark suite
(`docs/performance.md`, 2026-09-12): **spectralnorm 16.4x**, **binarytrees 3.9x**,
**nbody 2.3x**, and micro-benchmarks up to **53x**. `fannkuchredux` and `mandelbrot`
did not move.

**The three that matter most if you are upgrading.** Every `Hash->SHA256`,
HMAC or signature Objeck computed over a string was wrong, because a `Byte[]`
carried its element count plus one in the word native libraries read — no digest
ever matched another implementation. A constant character stored into a `Char[]`
overwrote its neighbours on ARM64 and Linux x64, including inside `String->Pop()`
and `String->Delete()`. And a compiled frame holding a declared-but-unreferenced
local was scanned by the collector under the wrong types.

### New Features
- **Breakpoints fire inside threads, and threads are real** ([#719](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/719)) — a breakpoint in any thread's `Run` never fired: spawned interpreters were built with a null debugger pointer (the August fix for a crash on a thread's first instruction), so every spawned thread was invisible to `obd` and DAP answered every `threads` request with a fabricated single thread while claiming `allThreadsStopped`. Spawned interpreters now attach to the one debugger; the model is all-stop, which is what the stop state already assumed — a thread reaching the hook while another owns the stop waits its turn, and three threads on one breakpoint stop three times with their own frames. Each thread gets a stable small id and its last source position: `obd` gains a `threads` command, DAP reports the real list, and the stopped event names the thread that stopped. Conditional breakpoints evaluate against the stopping thread's frame (the fixture proves `step = 200` is satisfiable only in worker 2). Three CLI tests in both harnesses, six DAP checks in a new suite
- **`Game.OpenGL`: `Skybox`** — a procedural sky evaluated per pixel from the view direction (horizon colour, zenith colour, a sun), so an outdoor scene gets a sky by saying what colour it is; no assets, no cubemap upload path, no native change
- **`Game.OpenGL`: `Quaternion`** — a rotation as a value: slerp for orientation tracks (Euler lerp does not take the short way round and stalls when axes align) and composition for turret-on-hull accumulation; a headless test caught the first version being wrong
- **`Game.OpenGL`: tangent-space normal maps** — `Material->SetNormalMap` and three shader factories (`LitTexturedNormalMapped`, `…Shadowed`, `…PointShadowed`); the tangent frame is derived in the fragment shader from screen-space derivatives, so no vertex attribute, no native upload change, and OBJ meshes without tangents work unchanged
- **`Game.OpenGL`: `Material->SetEmissive`** — light a surface gives off itself, added after lighting rather than multiplied into it; the one way to make a surface visible where no light arrives (the lantern's relics, and a shade standing between the player and their own lamp)
- **The lantern demo is a game** ([#716](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/716) and the commits before it) — monsters that ignore the lamp, relics to find, a hide with relief, fuel that tells the truth about reach, a completable arc; the last relic pulses so a session does not end sweeping swept corners

### Bug Fixes
- **JIT on ARM64 and Linux x64: a constant character stored into a `Char[]` overwrote the elements after it** ([#781](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/781)) — the char-array store's constant case wrote 8 bytes: ARM64 stored the whole register (its `move_imm_mem32` went through the 64-bit store) and AMD64 outside Windows a `movq` (its `move_imm_mem32` was `move_imm_mem`). So `a[i] := 'x'` also zeroed the next element on Linux and macOS (4-byte characters) and the next three on Windows ARM64 (2-byte characters), and wrote past the end of the array when `i` was the last index; Windows x64 stores 16 bits there and was never affected. A forward fill rewrites each clobbered element on its next store, so code that builds a string left to right came out right; a constant stored below an element that was already set destroyed it. Both helpers store 32 bits now and the ARM64 constant case stores 16 on Windows, as the register and memory cases always did. The Windows x64 native entry's four-byte `ip` and `jit_offset` went through the same AMD64 helper and wrote over their neighbours, harmlessly (the next store resets `jit_called`; the rest is padding); they are four-byte stores now too. `jit_const_char_store.obs` fails before the fix on linux-x64 and linux-arm64 (96 characters overwritten)
- **`Console->WriteBuffer(Char[])` encoded its output twice, and corrupted every write after it** ([#793](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/793)) — `obr` puts stdout in a wide CRT mode (`_O_U8TEXT` by default, or whatever `--objeck-stdio` selected) and the rest of the VM writes wide characters through `wcout`, letting the CRT encode them. `StdOutCharAryLen` did the conversion itself and wrote the resulting *bytes*, so the data was UTF-8 encoded twice — once by the trap and once by the CRT, which reads those bytes back a pair at a time as one `wchar_t`. `'A','B'` arrived as U+4241 (䉁). The invalid sequence then left the stream unusable, so a **single call corrupted the rest of the program's output**, including ordinary `PrintLine` calls that followed. The trap also ignored its `num` argument: `UnicodeToBytes` takes a NUL-terminated pointer, so it wrote to the first NUL rather than the count requested. It now hands `wcout` the wide characters and lets the stream's mode decide the encoding, as every other output path does. Verified on Windows *and* Linux, because the changed branch is shared and POSIX stdout is a plain byte stream where the old code was correct
- **`fasta` never compiled, and had been silently skipped by the benchmark harness since at least June** — four float literals carried a `d` suffix (`0.3029549426680d`), a Java-ism from the original shootout source; Objeck has only ever had a `u` suffix. The harness discarded the compiler's stderr, so the failure surfaced only as `SKIP: fasta (compile failed)`. Fixing it exposed the `WriteBuffer` bug above, which is why `fasta` is still absent from the v2026.9.1 performance tables
- **Windows x64 JIT: compiling a method that held ten integer values at once ended the process** ([#773](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/773)) — Windows adds `RSI` and `RDI` to the AMD64 allocator as auxiliary registers, handed out only when the eight pool registers are all live. Once one of them held a value that was stored to a local and then read back through the local-register cache, it sat in two of the compiler's lists at once, and the compiler deleted every list when it finished the method: one register holder was freed twice, Windows' heap reported corruption, and `obr` exited with `0xC0000374`, before the program had printed anything. The machine code was never wrong. The holders now belong to a single list and are deleted once; the two in-use lists, which nothing else read, are gone, and a failed compile no longer leaks its holders. Linux x64 has no auxiliary registers and ARM64 keeps its own lists, which is why only windows-x64 failed, and there only with every method compiled: the one test with such a method called it three times, below the auto-JIT threshold. `jit_entry_shapes.obs` gains `TenLive`, a ten-term probe called often enough to compile in both regression passes, which ends the old VM in both
- **`obr` exited at startup under a locale the C++ library could not construct** — on macOS, setting `LC_CTYPE` without `LANG` makes the C library report the per-category composite `C/C.UTF-8/C/C/C/C`, which libc++ cannot turn into a `std::locale`; the constructor threw out of the entry point and the VM printed `>>> virtual machine: collate_byname<char>::collate_byname failed to construct for C/C.UTF-8/C/C/C/C <<<` instead of running the program. That is exactly the environment Python 3 puts in every child when `LANG` is unset (PEP 538), so every Python harness — `run_vm_flag_tests.py` among them, seven failures from any shell with no `LANG` — hit it, and the workaround was `LC_ALL=en_US.UTF-8`. The locale `wcout` converts through is now built by a helper that never throws: the name `setlocale` returned, then the `LC_CTYPE` category alone (the one that decides how wide characters are written, and the one the composite was hiding), then `C.UTF-8` and `en_US.UTF-8`, then the classic locale with a UTF-8 converter, which needs no locale data at all. Wide output stays UTF-8 in every fallback, and the C library's own locale is left as the environment set it. glibc names its composites in a form libstdc++ accepts, so Linux takes the first candidate as before. The flag tests run `obr` under `LC_CTYPE=C.UTF-8` alone and under a locale the system does not have, and check the bytes of a wide string (`vm_locale_wide.obs`)
- **A C++ exception under JIT-compiled code aborted the process instead of ending the program with the VM's error line** ([#778](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/778)) — neither backend registers unwind information for the code it emits, so an exception thrown in a call the JIT's bridge made (the interpreter it nests for a callee with no native code, a trap, or the bridge's own opcode switch) could not pass through the compiled frame: libc++ terminated `obr` (SIGABRT, `terminating due to uncaught exception`) before Execute's catch printed anything, and libstdc++ and Windows end the same way. Interpreted, the same program stopped with `>>> virtual machine: internal error: ... <<<`. The bridge's two entries catch the exception now and report it as Execute would: the same line, the stack from the compiled method down, exit status 1. `jit_bridge_exception.obs` aborts the old VM in both regression passes, and the flag tests' section 8 names the status and the message, which the regression runner (any non-zero exit with output) cannot tell from the abort
- **`String->ToFloat` on text that is not a number killed the program on its tenth call** ([#778](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/778)) — the JIT bridge's S2F ran `std::stod` bare, so once the library method was auto-compiled (its tenth call, by default) the first unparseable string threw `std::invalid_argument` into compiled code and aborted the process, where the interpreter's `Str2Float` gives 0.0: a program parsing text answered 0.0 nine times and died on the tenth. The bridge's S2I caught that but let `std::out_of_range` through the same way for an integer that does not fit, where the interpreter gives 0. Both answer as the interpreter does now; `vm_jit_equiv.obs` parses bad text for twelve rounds across the switch to compiled code, and the old VM aborts on round ten
- **A method taking a func-ref parameter went wrong once the compiler inlined it** ([#763](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/763)) -- the inliner shifts a pasted callee's local slots past the caller's, but it shifted only the `Int` and `Float` variable instructions; the func-ref loads and stores kept the callee's ids, so the reference was stored over the caller's own locals and the call went through whatever was there. The interpreter looped forever, the JIT returned garbage; any one-return helper of the shape `Apply(x, f) { return f(x); }` called from a loop outside `Main` hit it. The func-ref instructions shift with the rest now. `inline_funcref_param.obs` fails on the old compiler in every VM mode, and `vm_jit_equiv.obs` inlines such a helper into a compiled loop
- **ARM64 JIT: compiling a method holding a patched func-ref call ended the process** — `PatchCallSites` rewrites a func-ref site into `DYN_MTHD_CALL_JIT` when a method whose ids equal its operands compiles; the ARM64 pre-scan accepted that opcode and the instruction switch had no case for it, so the compile reached the default ("Unknown instruction", exit 1). It is handled as the same call, as on AMD64
- **`Runtime->SetLocale` with a name the system lacks ended the program** — the VM switched the C library's locale and then built a `std::locale` from whatever `setlocale` returned, unguarded, in every build but Windows x64 (which only called `setlocale`). For a name the system does not have `setlocale` returns NULL, and on macOS it accepts a per-category composite (`C/C.UTF-8/C/C/C/C`) that libc++ still cannot build; the constructor threw on either. Interpreted, the program stopped with `>>> virtual machine: internal error: locale constructed with null <<<` while POSIX `obr` still exited 0; with the JIT on, the exception unwound through compiled code and the process aborted. Such a name is now refused rather than replaced with a fallback the program did not ask for: the C library's locale and the console's are left as they were, and the `locale` property is not set. A name the system has is accepted as before, except on Windows ARM64, where even a valid name stopped the program: the VM imbued `std::wcout` with the named locale, so wide output reached the Unicode-mode stdout `obr` sets up as narrow bytes, and the C runtime ended the process with 0xC0000409 and no output. Windows now sets only the C library's locale, as the x64 build always has. `vm_set_locale_refused.obs` aborts the old ARM64 VM on macOS in both regression passes and caught the Windows ARM64 crash in CI, and the flag tests run it with `--jit=off`, where the old VM's exit status hid the failure
- **`obr` on Linux and macOS exited 0 after a program died inside the VM** — `Execute` returns -1 once it has printed `>>> virtual machine: internal error: ... <<<`, `>>> virtual machine: out of memory <<<` or `>>> load error: ... <<<`, but the POSIX entry point discarded that result and returned 0, so a program that ended in one of those lines reported success to the shell, a `Makefile` or a CI step, and only a reader of stderr knew; Windows returned the -1. First seen on macOS when `Runtime->SetProperty("locale", "xx_YY.bogus")` killed the program with `locale constructed with null` (that call is made to survive separately); `"1e999"->ToFloat()` reaches the same catch on every platform. The POSIX entry now exits 1 after such an error, the status every other failure of `obr` already uses there (a runtime trap, a bad flag); a program that runs to the end still exits 0, and `Runtime->Exit(n)` still exits `n`, since the trap calls `exit` itself. A command line that is all flags (`obr --jit=off`, no program) exited 0 with nothing printed on POSIX and -1 with nothing printed on Windows; both print the usage and exit 1 now. `vm_error_exit.obs` fails on the old `obr` in the regression suite (`# EXPECT_RUNTIME_ERROR`), and the flag tests check the message and the status
- **ARM64 JIT: a func-ref parameter followed by any other local overwrote it** — the frame reserved two words for a func-ref but placed its slot at the pair's upper word, and the store wrote the pair upward from there: the reference's second word landed in the next local's slot (a plain function reference's closure word is 0, so that local read as zero), and the collector, walking the pair from the slot, read it one word low. Hidden because every fixture's func-ref parameter was last in its list or inlined away; `jit_entry_shapes.obs` fails on the old layout with every method compiled
- **ARM64 JIT: every compiled method returned zeros in its caller's `D8`-`D15`** — the entry zeroing ran from the first spill slot through the locals, across the eight save slots the prologue had just written, so the epilogue restored zeros into the callee-saved float registers. No C++ caller keeps a value there across the call, which is why nothing noticed; the zeroing skips the save slots now, and the save is emitted only when the method takes one of those registers
- **JIT (both backends): a compiled method with a declared-but-unreferenced local had its frame scanned under the wrong types** — the JIT laid out a native slot only for locals that some instruction references, while the collector walks a compiled frame by declaration, one slot each. A local the code declares but never touches shifted every slot below it by one word under the collector's eyes: an object where an object array was declared had its first field taken for a length, and the walk ran off the heap. Found on Linux with every method compiled (`OBJECK_JIT_THRESHOLD=1`, which CI does not run there): `Web.HTTP.Server.HttpRequestHandler:ServeOne` brought the collector down in `mcp_debug_test`; a nine-line reproducer crashes the shipped Windows binary the same way. The frame is laid out from the declarations now, one slot per declaration after the and/or temp, on AMD64 and ARM64. `jit_frame_unreferenced_local.obs` fails on the old layout in both JIT modes
- **AMD64 JIT: a constant character stored into an array element through `R8`-`R15` released the register twice** — the char store's `R8`-`R15` path copied the element address to a second register, released the element holder there, stored through the released register, and released the holder again at the end: two entries in the free pool for one register, two later owners, and a miscompiled method that crashed. Reachable whenever the element address landed in an extended register; the calling-convention work's result pop made that the common case, which is how `core_arrays_simple` and `arm64_char_arrays` caught it. Both store encoders take an extended base, so the copy is gone with the double release; `vm_jit_equiv.obs` stores constant characters and bytes right after call results
- **A compiled caller of a `virtual` method ran slower than the interpreter** — the JIT's callback bridge looked the callee up by the call site's operands, which name the `virtual` declaration rather than the override; the declaration has no native code and never gets any, so every such call built a fresh interpreter (a heap allocation and a root registration under the global lock) to resolve and run the override: 125 ns per call against the interpreter's own 70 ns. The bridge now resolves the override through the receiver's class, as the interpreter does, and the call takes the compiled path: 20 ns. That lookup miss was also all that kept the compiled path from running the declaration's empty body; it refuses one explicitly now. An inherited override's resolution is cached on the receiver's class, where the next lookup asks, instead of on the class the walk ended at, where it missed on every call. Probes in `vm_jit_equiv.obs`; design and numbers in `docs/JIT_CALLING_CONVENTION_DESIGN.md`
- **Nested string concatenation produced the wrong string, and could crash** ([#750](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/750)) — the compiler lowers a concatenation to "allocate a `System.String`, store it in a temporary, then for each part evaluate the part, load the temporary and `Append`", and that temporary was named after the enclosing *method*, so every concatenation in a method shared one. A concatenation nested inside another, such as a call argument that is itself a concatenation, overwrote the slot the outer one was still accumulating into: `Id(a + b) + "Z"` gave `ABABZ`, and `"Z" + Id(a + b)` gave `AB`, losing the `"Z"` entirely. One shape segfaulted. Each concatenation now gets its own slot. This was equally wrong in the interpreter and under the JIT, so the interpreter/JIT equivalence fixture could not see it; `string_concat_nesting.obs` checks expected values and fails on the old compiler
- **An interpolated string on the left of a concatenation printed its value twice** ([#752](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/752)) — `"{$a}" + "{$b}"` gave `AAB`.
