The concatenation builder analyzed its leftmost operand once to learn whether the `+` was a string concatenation at all, then analyzed every operand in its list, the leftmost a second time; a literal's analysis appends its interpolated segments on every visit, so the left value was emitted twice. Right-hand operands were visited once, which is why only the left side was wrong. The leftmost operand is now analyzed once, and a literal's analysis is idempotent. Wrong identically in the interpreter and under the JIT; `string_interp_concat.obs` checks expected values and fails on the old compiler
- **JIT loops never reached a GC safepoint** (both backends) — the loop-header poll was emitted at the label and keyed on the label's index, but a jump's target is the instruction after the label, so it never matched; a collection triggered by another thread waited for the whole loop. The poll is at the target now, and `jit_gc_safepoint.obs` fails on the old code
- **A compiled loop kept a stale `self` after parking at its safepoint** (both backends, [#746](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/746)) — when a thread parked at a loop-header safepoint while another thread's collection promoted (moved) the young object the loop's method runs on, the collector relocated the frame's `self` but not the JIT's private copy of it, which only the callback path refreshed. Every field read and write in the loop then went through the old nursery address until the next callback: wrong values, and writes into whatever the recycled nursery now held, other threads' objects included. JIT-only, multi-thread-only, on every platform, present since the poll started firing. The poll's slow path now re-reads `self` from the frame, as the callback path does. `core_thread_gc_stress.obs` runs compiled again and fails on the old code (35–45 corruptions on every CI leg; 5–34 on a 4-core affinity locally, 0 after)
- **AMD64 JIT: constant division with the dividend in `RDX`** multiplied the magic constant by itself; a call, a loop and an `if`/`else` before the division produce that register assignment
- **ARM64 JIT: a method's slot 0 vanished when its `&`/`|` sat only in conditions** — the compiler reserves local slot 0 in such methods and the ARM64 collector skips it, but the JIT laid the frame out from the referenced ids alone, so the collector read every declared slot one off and followed integers as pointers; both backends now reserve the slot whenever the method claims it. `OBJECK_GC_TRACE=1` names each JIT frame and slot the collector scans
- **ARM64 JIT: a floating-point register leaked per fused float compare**, so a method with more of them than the pool holds silently ran in the interpreter
- **AMD64 JIT: char-array elements through `R8`–`R15`** — the 16-bit movers emitted no REX prefix, so the element was read from or written to the low register with the same number; dormant until the allocator gained `R8`–`R11`
- **AMD64 JIT: `>>` was a logical shift** — the interpreter shifts a signed `INT64` (arithmetic) and the ARM64 backend emits `asr`, so `-8 >> 1` was `-4` in three places and `9223372036854775804` in the fourth: every negative operand of `>>` in JIT'd x64 code has been wrong, in every release. All three emission sites (immediate, register and memory shift counts) use `SAR` now; the equivalence fixture's new shift probe diverges on the shipped `obr` and matches
- **AMD64 JIT: `?->` on a nil receiver in a JIT-compiled caller exited the process** instead of yielding `Nil`. The backend treated `TRY_START`/`TRY_END` (what `?->` desugars to) as no-ops, on the recorded assumption that method calls were never JIT-compiled — they have been for some time — so no recovery handler was ever registered in native code. A method containing a try region is now left to the interpreter, as ARM64 always did; `nil_safe_ops` passes with every method JIT-compiled (`OBJECK_JIT_THRESHOLD=1`), which is how it was found
- **Debugger commands that reported success while doing the wrong thing** ([#717](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/717)) — `unwatch` with no id removed every *second* watchpoint (erase-and-advance); `set x = "str"` stored 0 and reported it; `delete <id>` deleted the breakpoint at the *current* line; `p 0 % n` was refused and `p n / 0` divided the debugger process by zero; `watches` could not say what it watched (the same empty field reached the DAP stop payload); `info class=C` hid a class holding only statics; any leading whitespace broke every command; `frame N` dereferenced an unguarded class; `delete` leaked. String comparisons compared pointers, so `text <> "other"` was true for equal text. Sixteen tests, each proven to fail on the pre-fix binary. DAP: `evaluate` ignored `frameId` and answered from the top frame; three more requests lied in the same shape
- **The language server crashed at startup** ([#717](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/717)) — the GC's `IsAllocated` young-generation range test accepted an unaligned pointer (`…ffffffec`) and walked garbage; root-caused with a hand-built Win32 debug loop, 0/200 after the fix (`IsYoungCandidate`: 8-byte aligned, inside the region)
- **`Game.OpenGL`: the overlay drew everything upside down** — every label, HUD bar and crosshair in every program using an `Overlay`; the overlay's quad mapped screen-space y=0 to the texture's bottom row after the (correct) upload flip. Fixed in the overlay's own quad. Shipped in v2026.9.0; found from a screenshot, not a test
- **macOS ships an uninstaller** — the `.pkg` wrote to four places (`/usr/local/objeck-lang`, `/etc/paths.d/objeck`, a LaunchDaemon, an `export OBJECK_LIB_PATH` line in `/etc/zshenv` and `/etc/profile`) and shipped no way to undo any of them; the shell edit now carries a marker so removal cannot match a line the user wrote
- **Every hash of a string was wrong** — `Hash->SHA256("abc"->ToByteArray())` digested `"abc"` *plus a zero byte*, so no digest, HMAC or signature computed by Objeck ever matched another implementation. A `Byte[]` has two size words: `Size()` reads `[2]`, native libraries read `[0]`. The interpreter's `Byte->New` writes `[0] = count`, but the JIT's `NEW_BYTE_ARY` and twelve trap producers (`Char[]->ToBytes`, the zlib/gzip/brotli traps, the HTTP/2 and HTTP/3 response bodies, string literals, the serializer's buffer) wrote `[0] = count + 1` for the NUL pad, and a comment called that the standard layout. `[0]` is the element count everywhere now; the pad stays in the allocation only. `byte_array_header_test.obs` hashes arrays from each producer and compares with known digests
- **JIT: `IMUL` wrote its product to the wrong register** — `mul_reg_reg` encoded `0F AF` with the ADD family's operand layout, so the result landed in the *source* register; its one caller compensated by passing the arguments backwards. Found while adding the missing 64-bit guard to `mul_imm_reg` (`add`/`sub` had it since v2026.8.4): `n * 4294967311` multiplied by 15. Both fixed; `vm_jit_equiv.obs` compares the interpreter and the JIT on the shapes involved
- **JIT: a native-call temporary clobbered live values** — `call_xfunc`/`call_xfunc2` (the path behind `Pow`, `Log`, `Sin`, …) spilled nothing, so `7 + x->Pow(5.0) + Bump(x)` evaluated differently compiled than interpreted. Live working-stack registers are spilled around the call
- **JIT: relinking retargeted `select` defaults** — `PatchCallSites` matched on operands alone, so a `JMP_TABLE` whose (base, range) happened to equal a (class, method) pair had its default arm rewritten to instruction 1. Only call opcodes are patched
- **A debugger halt could never land** — `halt` was a plain `bool` written by other threads and spun on by the dispatch loop; the `-O3 -flto` build was free to keep it in a register. It is `std::atomic<bool>`, as the structurally identical safepoint flag already was
- **DAP `setVariable` on an expanded object's field wrote a top-frame local** — the handle decode had no upper bound, so a child handle (100000+) became a frame index that `DbgFrameAt` clamped to the top frame: editing `obj.count` overwrote a local named `count` and reported success. The CLI `set` command and the DAP request now share one path (`ResolveAssignable` → `AssignSlot`), fields of an expanded object and elements of an expanded `Int[]`/`Float[]` are assignable through their handles, the value is an expression evaluated in the stopped frame (`count + 8`, `0x2a`) from either surface, and refusals carry one message. `dap_setvar_test.obs` has a local and a field of the same name so a cross-write is observable
- **`EvaluateForDapRaw` cast every expression to a `Reference`** — a hover or watch on an arithmetic expression read a declaration out of an object that had none
- **Debugger: hex literals never scanned** — the scanner's identifier rule claimed any digit followed by a letter, so `0x2a` was the identifier "0x2a" and `print 0x2a` answered "unknown variable"; behind it, the number rule's hex state machine cleared itself on the leading `0`, and the literal was 32-bit from `wcstol` through the token to `IntegerLiteral` (`long` on Windows), so `0x7fffffffff` evaluated to -1. All fixed; `hex_literal` runs in both CLI harnesses
- **A runtime property could be set only once per process** — `Runtime->SetProperty` stored with `std::map::insert`, which leaves an existing key alone, so every set after a property's first was dropped without a word. The library relies on overwriting: `String->SetIntFormat`, `SetFloatFormat` and `SetFloatPrecision` store the formats `Int->ToString` and `Float->ToString` read, in the interpreter and under the JIT, so a number format could be changed once and never changed back (hex and then dec printed 255 as `ff` twice), and `Runtime->GetProperty("locale")` kept the first name given to `Runtime->SetLocale`. Both `SetProperty` variants, Windows and POSIX, assign now. `vm_set_property_overwrite.obs` fails on the old VM in both regression passes and with `--jit=off`
- **On Linux and macOS, a first `Runtime->SetProperty` kept the runtime's own properties from ever loading** — the runtime fills its property map (`install_dir`, `lib_dir`, `user_dir`, `tmp_dir`, and `config.prop` from the working directory) the first time the map is used while it is empty. `GetProperty` did that everywhere and `SetProperty` did it on Windows, but the POSIX `SetProperty` stored its key into the empty map without it, and a map holding one key is never empty again. A program that set a number format or a locale before reading any property got `""` from `Runtime->GetTempDir`, `GetUserDir`, `GetInstallDir` and `GetLibraryDir` for the rest of the run. The POSIX `SetProperty` fills the map first now, as Windows always did. `vm_set_property_first.obs` fails on the old macOS VM in both regression passes and with `--jit=off`
- **POSIX runtime divergences from Windows** — `Pipe->WriteByte` returned `send()`'s `-1` as `true`; `Runtime->Command()` dropped stderr (Windows appended `2>&1`); `File->Owner()` dereferenced a NULL `getpwuid`/`getgrgid` in containers; `CommandOutput()` leaked one file descriptor per call from `mkstemp`
- **JSON serialized strings without escaping** — a value holding a quote, backslash or newline produced text that was not JSON; two of the three AI clients did not know to pre-encode. `JsonElement->ToString()` escapes string values and object keys; the pre-encodes in `ollama.obs` and the example programs are gone (they would now be escaped twice). `json_escape_test.obs` round-trips control characters
- **`Ollama` swallowed error replies** — of ten inlined response parsers, one bound its `else-if` to the wrong `if`, so an `{"error": …}` body (the shape Ollama sends) was silently a `Nil` result and a non-JSON body dereferenced `Nil`; two paths decoded the reply twice. One `ParseResponse`/`ReportError` on `EndPoint` serves all ten, `ParseGenerateResponse(body)` is testable without a server (`ollama_parse_test.obs`)
- **`--lib-path` documented the wrong thing** — it is the Objeck `lib` root: `obr` loads `<dir>/native/libobjk_*` and `<dir>/cacert.pem` from it (`obc` reads the `.obl` files). The usage text said ".obl libraries"; and the flag test proved nothing twice — its fixture loaded no native library, and Windows resolves the `..\lib\native` fallback against `obr.exe`'s own directory. The test now runs a copy of `obr` with no `lib` beside it and requires the control to fail

### Testing
- **TLS verification is tested for what it refuses** — `tls_verify_test.obs` starts a loopback HTTPS server with a certificate for `DNS:localhost` only and proves, through `TCPSecureSocket` and `HttpsClient`, that a self-signed certificate is refused without a trust anchor and that a trusted certificate which does not name the host (`127.0.0.1`) is refused; `https_persistence_test` had only ever proved acceptance. Also recorded: `WebServer` is a process-wide singleton (its server lives in statics), so a second `ServeSecure` in the same process replaces the first rather than running beside it
- **A compiled call must stay out of the bridge** — `jit_call_overhead.obs` guards the release's headline number. Nothing in the suite would have noticed if the F7 native entry were lost and every call fell back to the C++ bridge: `vm_jit_equiv.obs` proves the answer is right, never that it was cheap. The measure is a ratio, `(RealCall - NoCall) / NoCall`, which cancels both the iteration count and the machine's speed so it cannot flake on unknown CI hardware; best of three rounds. Validated against the pre-F7 baseline (`2cf5500722`), where the same `.obe` reports **59.9x** and exits 1, versus **6.2x** on master; an interpreted run reads ~1.1x, so a platform with no JIT passes rather than failing for the wrong reason
- **`vm_write_char_buffer.obs`** covers the `WriteBuffer` fix from inside the program, where stdout correctness cannot be asserted directly: a partial write must return the count requested (4 of 8, not 8) and a non-ASCII write the character count (3, not the 6 UTF-8 bytes the old path counted). Both read identically for plain ASCII written in full, which is why it uses a partial write and characters above U+007F

### Compiler
- **A long addition chain no longer takes exponential time to compile** — `AnalyzeCalculation` recursed into each calculated operand and then analyzed it again through `AnalyzeExpression`, so every level of a nested expression was analyzed twice: twenty terms of `a + b + ...` compiled in three seconds, twenty-four in over a minute, and a thirty-term sum never returned (nine gigabytes and counting). Each operand is analyzed once now; a 37-term chain compiles in 0.05 s, with bytecode byte-identical to the old compiler's on the JIT fixture and the concatenation tests. `compiler_long_add_chain.obs` sums forty terms
- **Conditions branch directly** — `&`, `|` and `<>` in an `if`, `while`, `do`/`while`, `for` or `?:` condition become short-circuit jumps instead of materializing a value through a temporary local (a store, a reload and a `cmov` per connective in JIT code); evaluation order is unchanged and now pinned by `core_bool_short_circuit.obs`
- **A `native` method is no longer inlined into a caller that is not itself native** — the `-opt s3` inliner pasted small calls into their caller and the copy ran in whatever engine the caller did; `Main` was exempt, but a thread's `Run` (called once per thread, so never auto-compiled) was not, so a worker thread's native loop ran in the interpreter, ~50x slower than the same code on the main thread. Pinned by `jit_native_inline.obs`
- **Name resolution stopped copying the library maps** — `Linker::GetAll{Aliases,Classes,Enums}Map` returned the whole `unordered_map` by value and `Search{Alias,Class,Enum}Libraries` copied it to perform one lookup, from 75 call sites; a 4,087-line application spent 20% of its compile hashing wide strings for those copies. `Class::AddMethod`'s duplicate check walked every method and copied every parsed name — an `unordered_set` now, O(1), insertion order preserved
- **`CheckIdentifier` is a map lookup** — a 366-line switch restated every keyword the map already held (a token listed in only one silently scanned as an identifier — `true`, `false`, `Nil` and `threads` were each dead on arrival in the debugger's copy for that reason), and `operator[]` inserted every non-keyword identifier in the source into the keyword table. Both scanners consult an `unordered_map` and never grow it
- **Linker copies** — `SearchClassLibraries(name)` copied the whole class map per call in the innermost resolution loop; `GetInstructions()`, `GetMethods()` and `GetClasses()` returned their containers by value. All by reference now

### Infrastructure
- **Release shore-up** — the notes above now cover everything merged since the tag (they had omitted #716–#719 and the OpenGL work), and the README shipped inside the archive lists it; the LSP hover/completion index is regenerated from the current `lib_src` (it was five days and one library API behind); the VS Code extension manifest is stamped by `update_version.ps1` instead of sitting two releases stale; `verify-signing-credentials.yml` now checks the credentials the pipeline actually uses -- the two Apple identities, imported with the release's own commands -- and runs weekly, so a rotated password or an expiring certificate shows as a red schedule instead of an unsigned release (it had been checking the retired Windows `CODESIGN_*` secrets, whose stored certificate turned out to be the Sectigo intermediate CA with no private key; Windows signs locally with the eToken); the 31-line library-build list that `update_version.sh` and CI's Windows step each carried is one `build_libs.sh`; `code_doc64_debug.cmd` lists `sdl_gl`; `code_doc64` resolves `obc`/`obr` from its own directory (under Git Bash, `NoDefaultCurrentDirectoryInExePath` made the bare names fail) and refuses to package an `api.zip` that holds no pages -- it had produced a three-file archive and reported success; `docs/third_party_dependencies.md` records where every platform's TLS/HTTP/media libraries come from, and `docs/release_integrity.md` designs the signed manifest that would let `obu` detect a substituted `SHA256SUMS`
- **The standard libraries are rebuilt by the compiler that ships with them** ([#792](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/792)) — the committed `.obl` set dated from 2026-09-04, and four compiler *correctness* fixes landed after it: the func-ref inliner (#763), both string-concatenation accumulator fixes (#750, #752), and the native-inlining rule. `build_libs.sh` compiles every library with `-opt s3`, which is exactly the inliner #763 fixed, so the libraries were built by a compiler that still pasted a func-ref parameter's slots unshifted. CI does not cover this: only the `build-docs` job runs `update_version.sh`, while the `build` job that produces the shipped artifacts uses the committed set, and the regression suite runs against those same libraries so it could not have caught it either. 30 of the 32 libraries changed on rebuild, and they got *smaller* (`xml.obl` 8779 → 8137, `sdl_gl.obl` 51827 → 50203)
- **A Windows deploy stops dirtying eight tracked files** ([#787](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/787)) — `update_version.ps1` stamped through `Set-Content`, which joins lines with CRLF, against a repository declared `eol=lf`; every `deploy_windows.cmd` run left `version.h`, the five `.rc` files, `readme.json` and the VS Code `package.json` modified with an empty content diff, and the next `git pull` refused to merge
- **The weekly secret scan failed every Monday** ([#718](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/718)) — `gitleaks-action` scans the pushed range on push/PR but the whole history on a schedule, so the weekly run reported 326 findings from 2014-era test data and was never green; a job that is always red is a job nobody reads. Scheduled runs use the same baseline the push scan reasons about, verified by a positive control that omits an already-public 2014 commit from the baseline and fails
- **POSIX deploys verify their native libraries** — `verify_native_libs.sh`, the counterpart of the Windows `verify_native_libs.ps1`, runs after the native copies in `deploy_posix.sh`, `deploy_macos_arm64.sh` and both MSYS2 deploys: a library whose build failed was dropped from the release with a green exit (the obu incident, again). The MSYS2 deploys also invoked `build_msys2-*.sh` scripts for lame and opencv that did not exist
- **`update_version.sh [amd64|arm64]`** — `update_version_arm.sh` was a copy with four characters changed and is a shim now; both stop on the first failed compile (`set -e`) instead of shipping a stale `.obl` green
- **`core/vm/arch/jit/README.md`** stated three things the code does not do (method inlining, `JMP_TABLE` compilation, ARM64 loop detection as a blacklist)
- **A manual declaration nothing reads is not a declaration** ([#692](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/692)) — the `docs` token in `RELEASE_MANUAL_STEPS` was inert: `check_release_config.sh` reported the configuration OK, then the v2026.9.0 publish failed in "Deploy API Documentation" anyway, because that job had a hard-fail on the missing secret and no branch reading the variable beside it. Sourceforge and playground each had both halves; docs had only the second. The gate now greps the workflows for a branch reading each token and reports `INERT` when there is none
- **Signing is not finished until the manifest matches** ([#693](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/693)) — signing rewrites the MSIs, so the `SHA256SUMS` published beside them describes bytes that no longer exist and `sha256sum -c` fails on both Windows installers. `sign_release.cmd` re-uploaded the signed MSIs and never touched the manifest; the repair lived in `post_release.sh`'s gate 4 and in whoever remembered. New `tools/cicd/update_sha256sums.ps1` rebuilds it from the published assets, writes LF, and re-downloads its own upload to verify
- **The playground deploy that ships a fix now runs the fix** ([#694](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/694)) — `update.sh` pulls the repo from inside itself, so a run that pulls a new `update.sh` kept executing the old one and exited 0. The first v2026.9.0 deploy printed "Update complete", moved `/api/health` to the new version, and left the engine on 2026.8.4. It now re-execs when the pull changes it. The health check also polls for 20s instead of sleeping 3s and asking once, which had it reporting failure after every healthy restart
- **An open issue must be fixed or explained before a tag** ([#695](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/695)) — releases were cut without consulting the issue list, so an issue could sit open across several releases having never been considered, indistinguishable from one deliberately deferred. `tools/cicd/check_open_issues.sh` requires each open issue to be fixed or carry a `Deferred from v<VERSION>:` note, which it prints; a note naming an older version does not carry forward

### Virtual Machine
- **A call between compiled methods costs 16 ns instead of 26** (`docs/JIT_CALLING_CONVENTION_DESIGN.md`, phase 1) — the bridge every compiled call crosses took two critical sections and cleared a 768-byte frame buffer per call. Frames come from a per-thread pool with no lock now, zeroed on acquire for exactly the local space the method declares (which is what a local that is declared but never assigned relies on to read as 0 or Nil), and a call whose callee is bound at compile time enters the bridge with the method itself rather than an opcode to switch on and look up. Recursion (`Fib(32)`): 0.22 s to 0.13 s compiled, 0.39 s to 0.25 s interpreted, since the interpreter's calls cross the same pool; a compiled loop calling a field-touching method 0.53 s to 0.34 s. The register-argument convention that removes the rest is phases 2 and 3 of the design
- **A compiled method's entry and exit are a third shorter** (AMD64; the design's phase 2) — a method that never uses a float register no longer saves and restores `XMM10`-`XMM15` on Windows (the blocks are patched into a jump over themselves once the body is emitted), the frame is zeroed with straight stores instead of a microcoded `LOOP`, and the operand-stack arithmetic around parameters, returns and call results is computed once per sequence rather than per value. A compiled call goes from 16.3 ns to 14.4 ns; what remains is the C++ bridge, which the design's phase 3 replaces
- **A compiled method's entry and exit are shorter on ARM64 too** (the design's phase 2, its section 12) — the arguments are read below one `top` pointer and the results popped with one scaled load, the frame is zeroed with `stp xzr, xzr` pairs instead of a five-instruction loop (nineteen trips for a one-local method), `D8`-`D15` are saved and restored only by a method that takes one, and the prologue's frame-size immediate is computed exactly rather than ORed onto a template that held 96. A compiled call goes from 17.1 ns to 13.4 ns on an M4 Max, `Fib(32)` from 0.135 s to 0.103 s; `jit_entry_shapes.obs` covers the shapes the entry and exit take
- **The ARM64 JIT allocates from twelve general registers** (F4 on ARM64) — `X12`-`X15` join `X0`-`X7`, handed out after them, so a method whose expressions carry a ninth to twelfth temporary compiles instead of falling back whole; the backend does not spill, and the bytecode pushes every term of a chain before its first operation, so a ten-term sum needed ten registers. `jit_entry_shapes.obs`'s eleven-term sums compile now and fell back before (a ten-term version fit Windows x64's ten registers exactly and exposed a miscompile there, [#773](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/773)); the six loop kernels of `jit_probe.obs` and the call probe are unchanged within noise
- **A compiled call on ARM64 goes straight into the callee** (the calling-convention design's phase 3 and native entry, its section 13) — every compiled method gains a second prologue a compiled caller calls with self, its stack pointer and the end of its outgoing area in `X0`-`X2`, the frame record built in the callee's own frame and pushed on the call stack; `RTRN` returns the value in `D0`; `virtual` and func-ref sites keep inline caches. The C++ bridge is the slow path. A compiled call goes from 13.8 ns to 7.4 ns on an M4 Max, `Fib(32)` from 0.107 s to 0.050 s, a `virtual` call loop from 0.0345 s to 0.016 s
- **ARM64 JIT: a float pending across a libc call no longer sends the method to the interpreter** — the libc helpers behind `Sin`, `Cos`, `Pow`, `Log` and the rest spilled pending integer temporaries around the call but gave up on a pending caller-saved float; each is parked in a free callee-saved `D8`-`D15` register for the call now, two `fmov`s and no memory. `Arith:Mix`, the equivalence fixture's one standing fallback on ARM64, compiles, and `OBJECK_JIT_REPORT=1` on the fixture is silent there for the first time
- **A compiled method calls a compiled method directly** (AMD64; the design's phase 3, first step) — a call between compiled methods went through a C++ bridge: an eleven-argument entry, a frame from the pool, a release. The caller now reads the callee's entry address from one word on the method, builds the callee's frame record on its own stack, pushes it on the call stack and calls the entry itself; a callee without native code yet, or a full call stack, takes the bridge as before, and a callee's error status is reported by `JitNativeCallError` naming both methods. A compiled call goes from 14.4 ns to 7 ns and `Fib(32)` from 0.115 s to 0.071 s; on master the call was 26.5 ns and `Fib(32)` 0.208 s. `virtual` and func-ref calls still take the bridge. Two tests cover the exits: a directly called callee that dereferences Nil, and recursion past the call stack's limit
- **A `virtual` call from compiled code costs what a bound one does** (AMD64; the design's phase 3, second step) — each `virtual` call site keeps a small inline cache of receiver class, resolved target and entry; a hit compares the receiver's class word with the last record and calls the entry directly, a miss resolves the override through the receiver's class and fills a record, and a site that sees more classes than it holds stays on the bridge. A monomorphic virtual call goes from 18 ns to 7.5 ns. `vm_jit_equiv.obs` exercises a six-class site beside a monomorphic one
- **A func-ref call from compiled code takes the same cache** (AMD64; the design's phase 3, third step) — a call through a function reference or closure is keyed by the func-ref's packed word, so it too calls its target's entry directly after the first call; about 15 ns per call, the bridge's share, are gone. The patched `DYN_MTHD_CALL_JIT` opcode, which the interpreter writes at a site when a callee with matching operands compiles, is now emitted as the same call; a method holding one failed to compile before. `vm_jit_equiv.obs` exercises one site alternating between two references beside one that keeps a single reference
- **Compiled methods take their arguments without the operand stack** (AMD64; the design's section 11, the register-argument entry) -- every compiled method now has a second entry that a compiled caller enters with `self` and the caller's frame pointer in registers and the arguments in the caller's outgoing area, its frame record built in its own frame; the result comes back in `XMM0` instead of through the operand stack, and the caller's marshalling, the callee's count traffic and the sections-8-to-10 record building on the caller's stack are gone. The bridge entry stays for the interpreter and for a callee not compiled yet. A bound call goes from 7.0 ns to 5.5 ns and a virtual one from 8.0 ns to 6.0 ns; where the cost is latency rather than instruction count the gain is larger: `Fib(32)` from 0.072 s to 0.043 s (0.208 s on master before the design), and a func-ref call to less than half. `vm_jit_equiv.obs` gains probes for every value shape across such a call (`Float` arguments and results as literals, locals and registers; a call with no arguments; eight arguments; a `Nil` result; receivers held only by the callee while it allocates; a func-ref result, which keeps the bridge), byte-identical across the three modes
- **The Linux x64 JIT allocates the same eight registers as Windows** -- the POSIX backend had four pool registers and three auxiliary ones where Windows had `R8`-`R11` in the pool since batch 3, so an expression with five live values, or a method with a dozen locals, quietly ran in the interpreter on Linux (`OBJECK_JIT_REPORT=1` on the equivalence fixture named two of its own probes). `R8`-`R11` join the Linux pool, the prologue no longer pushes them (they are caller-saved, and every path that calls out spills or pushes them), and the write barrier saves the four of them. `RealCall` 0.140 s to 0.128 s on Linux
- **AMD64 JIT: a quarter less machine code for the same programs** -- every memory operand was encoded with a four-byte displacement (the `ModRM` helper fixed mod=10), so a frame slot or a field took seven bytes where four do; every add, subtract and compare of an immediate against memory took a four-byte immediate; a boolean result took four instructions and a second register (`mov 0`, `mov 1`, compare, `cmov`); the prologue zeroed the frame one word per eleven-byte store. Displacements and immediates that fit in a byte now take one (`mod=01`, `0x83`), a boolean is `setcc` and `movzx`, and the frame is zeroed sixteen bytes at a time from a cleared `XMM0`. The equivalence fixture's 84 methods went from 292,958 to 222,755 bytes of code, byte-identical output in every mode; the call kernels gained a few percent
- **The hottest integer locals of a loop live in registers** (AMD64: up to three in `R13`–`R15`) — loaded at the loop header, read and written as registers inside, stored back on every exit; `OBJECK_JIT_REPORT=1` names each pinned loop and `OBJECK_JIT_PIN_MAX` / `OBJECK_JIT_PIN_SKIP` turn it off for bisecting. Design in `docs/JIT_LOOP_LOCALS_DESIGN.md`

The measured findings of [`docs/JIT_CODEGEN_ASSESSMENT_2026_09.md`](docs/JIT_CODEGEN_ASSESSMENT_2026_09.md) ([#728](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/728)), landed in [#731](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/731), [#732](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/732) and [#733](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/733) with the interpreter/JIT equivalence fixture, the flag tests and the regression suite (normal, and with every method JIT-compiled) green, and all five CI legs green on the combined tree:

- **`a->Size()` is inlined by the JIT** (AMD64 and ARM64) — `LOAD_ARY_SIZE` went through the interpreter callback: push the array onto the VM operand stack, call C++, read the result back, ~35 instructions and a call per evaluation of `i < a->Size()`. It is a nil check and one load now. An array-summing loop went from 6.0 to 0.75 ns per element (8×); a float dot product 2.7×
- **Division by a constant is a multiply** (AMD64 and ARM64) — every constant divisor other than 0 and ±1 (and, on AMD64, powers of two, which already used shifts) now goes through its Granlund–Montgomery magic number: a 3-cycle `imul`/`smulh` and a few shifts in place of a 10–20-cycle `idiv`/`sdiv`. The recipe was checked against exact division for 480,000 cases including `INT64_MIN`, `INT64_MAX`, negative divisors and powers of two before it was emitted, and the equivalence fixture holds divisors of every shape. The assessment's integer loop (two constant divisions per iteration) runs 2.5× faster
- **Constant divisors skip the divide-by-zero check**, and AMD64 saves `RAX`/`RDX` around a division only when they hold something — they were saved and restored unconditionally
- **The AMD64 allocator has eight general registers** — `R8`–`R11` join `RAX`–`RDX` (on Linux and macOS they were already reachable through the auxiliary pool). Four registers meant any expression with five live values spilled, or the method fell back to the interpreter: the equivalence fixture's own twelve-term division probe did exactly that, silently. Ten spill slots instead of six in the frame, and the write barrier's slow path saves the whole pool
- **A dense `select` is compiled as a jump table** (AMD64 and ARM64) — `JMP_TABLE` was in neither backend's whitelist, so any method holding a `select` ran in the interpreter whole: parsers, tokenizers, state machines and dispatch loops, the code written with `select`. It is now a bounds check and an indirect jump through a table of 32-bit offsets placed inline after the jump (no data section, PC-relative). A tokenizer-shaped state machine over a 1,160-character buffer, 2,000 rounds: 0.099 s interpreted, 0.002 s compiled. The equivalence fixture gains eight `select` shapes (dense, gaps to the default, a negative base, `Char` labels, no `other`, nested, a `break` out of a pinned loop, a method-call selector). Design: `docs/JIT_SELECT_TABLES_DESIGN.md`
- **`Main`, a thread's `Run`, and any once-called method with a loop are now compiled** — the auto-JIT counted calls, and these are called once, so a hot loop written at top level ran in the interpreter for the life of the program (~50x slower than the same loop in a helper; the documented workaround was a `native` helper). A method with a loop is compiled on its first entry or call; straight-line methods keep the ten-call threshold; a raised `OBJECK_JIT_THRESHOLD` (as the LSP server sets) turns both rules off. Found on the way: the loader's classless `$Initialization$` routine's call to `Main` was never patched to the JIT opcode, so a compiled `Main` was still interpreted for its one call — dispatch now follows the callee's native code. A 2e8-iteration loop: 3.65 s in `Main` and 3.52 s in a `Run` before, 0.08 s and 0.13 s after (`jit_entry_compiled.obs`). Design: `docs/JIT_ENTRY_COMPILE_DESIGN.md`
- **The hottest `Float` locals of a loop live in `XMM6`–`XMM9` on Windows** — the float half of loop-local pinning: up to four `Float` locals per loop stay in callee-saved XMM registers for the loop's extent, written back on every exit, alongside the three integer pins in `R13`–`R15`. Linux and macOS keep every XMM caller-saved, so nothing changes there (ARM64's `D8`–`D15` are the follow-up). The assessment's `FloatDot` kernel: 0.048 s → 0.021 s. Six new fixture probes cover accumulators, a float through a nested loop, mixed int/float pins, more floats than registers, a `break`, and calls inside the loop
- **Array element addressing is one instruction** (AMD64 `lea` with a scaled index; ARM64 `add` with a shifted register) — the index and the bound were both shifted by the element size before the compare, which changed nothing about the comparison, and the address was then built with two adds
- **Windows: JIT code saves `XMM10`–`XMM15`** — the allocator hands out those registers and the Windows x64 ABI makes them callee-saved; the JIT'd function is entered by a plain C++ call whose MSVC-compiled caller may keep values in them across the call, and nothing saved them. 96 bytes in the prologue, restored in the teardown; Linux and macOS have no callee-saved vector registers
- **`OBJECK_JIT_REPORT=1`** names every method the JIT hands back to the interpreter and why (an unsupported opcode, or a compile that failed part-way, with the instruction index). One unsupported instruction returns the whole method to the interpreter and nothing said so — the equivalence fixture's own division probe turned out to be interpreted because a twelve-term expression exhausted the AMD64 allocator's four registers
- The interpreter/JIT equivalence fixture gains probes for `Size()` in a loop condition and body, a multi-dimensional `Size()`, and constant divisors of every shape (small, negative, above 32 bits, composite)
- **`--jit=off` / `--jit=<calls>` and `--lib-path=<dir>`** — the JIT tunables existed only as environment variables documented in a comment inside `jit_common.h`, so "does it still happen with the JIT off?" could not be asked without reading source. The environment forms remain as the legacy path. Both platform mains carried their own copy of the option parsing and usage text, and they had drifted: a `g` suffix on `--gc-threshold` multiplied by 2⁴⁰ (a terabyte, not a gigabyte); `--objeck-stdio` accepted any value while acting only on `binary`, so `--objeck-stdio=1` silently did nothing; POSIX did not parse the flag and passed it through to the program as an argument; and the Windows flag path discarded `SetStdIo`'s result, so `binary` switched the console and then told `Execute` it had not. One parser and one usage string now serve every platform, every flag states its valid values, and a value outside them is refused with the expectation named. `run_vm_flag_tests.py` gates it in CI, including the first automated check that the interpreter and the JIT print byte-identical output

### Documentation
- Sourceforge mirrors the GitHub release through a webhook and is not a manual upload; objeck.org has no versioned `/api/v<VERSION>/` directories, so `latest` is the only served path. Both were documented the other way round, in the pre-flight gate's to-do text and in the publish workflow's failure message
- **The shipped examples folder is curated and indexed** ([#751](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/pull/751) and the follow-up) — `welcome.obs` is an executable index that needs no libraries or display and prints, for every example, what it shows and the exact `-lib` list that compiles it (all verified by compiling them); a scratch hello-world, a JIT bug reproduction and a 4,092-line duplicate of the 2D game no longer ship; `tools/cicd/check_examples_index.py` fails CI if an example ships unindexed or the index names a missing file. Two leftovers fixed after: the OpenGL shooting gallery moves to the `opengl` folder with the other OpenGL examples as `gl_fps.obs`, and the second file numbered 19 is now `loops_27.obs`

### Known Issues
- [#722](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/722) — the ARM64 JIT miscompiles `String->Equals` in a virtual request-handler callback; the two regression tests that would show it run with `JIT_DISABLE`, so shipped behaviour is unchanged from v2026.9.0. Scheduled with the `System.Terminal` release
- [#723](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/723) — `SHA256SUMS` is unsigned, so `obu` verifies hashes but cannot detect a substituted manifest; design in `docs/release_integrity.md`, awaiting a maintainer-generated key
- [#729](https://github.com/objeck/objeck-lang/issues/729) — the API-doc parser rejects two `GLWindow` members (`Own`, `GetDrawableGeneration`), so they are absent from `api.zip`, as they have been in every release since they landed

## [v2026.9.0] - 2026-09-03

A socket close that stops losing the response on Windows loopback, a language server that no longer serializes every request behind one lock, and a release pipeline that reports what it actually did.

### Bug Fixes
- **A server that wrote a response and closed could lose all of it** — on Windows loopback the reader got a connection reset and zero bytes, even though every byte had been accepted and delivered. `TCPSocket` and `TCPSecureSocket` gain `CloseGracefully()`, which reads until the peer hangs up and then closes, so the client owns the teardown. Measured over 180 transfers of a 16KB response: `Close()` lost 21, `CloseGracefully()` lost none
- **The language server serialized every request behind one lock** — concurrent analysis was correct only because of it, with `TreeFactory` and `TypeFactory` as process-wide singletons underneath. They are now bound per thread through a scope guard, so each analysis gets its own and the coarse lock gives way to per-program locking

### Infrastructure
- **Four publish steps reported success while doing nothing** — Sourceforge, the Marketplace, the playground and the API docs each skipped on an absent credential and passed, so v2026.8.4 published with all four green while the playground served a three-month-old engine. A missing credential now fails and names the secret, or is declared manual in one place that also prints as a to-do, and a pre-flight gate checks the pipeline can do what it advertises before the tag is pushed
- **Documentation**: `docs/windows_loopback_sockets.md` listed bounded drains at 100ms and 400ms as having no effect, then recommended a drain with a 1000ms bound. Both are true — the bound is the variable, not the technique — but the document did not say so and read as contradicting itself

## [v2026.8.4] - 2026-08-30

The release is dominated by one thing: `Game.OpenGL`, a 3D graphics framework built from nothing to a shipping library over roughly forty commits. Beside it sit a library that had never been usable, a class of JIT bugs that produced wrong arithmetic with no diagnostic, three ways the collector could destroy a live object, and a Windows ARM64 distribution that shipped without the runtimes it needs.

### New Features
- **`Game.OpenGL`**: OpenGL 3.3 core over SDL2, on Windows, Linux and macOS &mdash; 26 classes. `GLWindow` (windowing, frame pacing, key/mouse/wheel input, held-key state, gamepad), built-in shaders with uniforms that no longer leak between programs, `Mesh` primitives and OBJ loading, textures with anisotropic filtering, `Transform`/`Vector3`/cameras (first-person, `LookAtPoint`, orthographic), materials, up to eight directional/point/spot lights with Blinn-Phong specular, shadow maps including omnidirectional cube shadows from point lights, render-to-texture, instanced drawing plus the object-layer `PropBatch`, frustum culling on by default, `Scene->Raycast` for hitscan and picking, transparency with correct ordering, multisampling, stencil/points/line-width/depth-func, particles, billboards, a transform hierarchy with keyframed animation, sound, and a pixel-space text overlay with a default font. The draw loop stops allocating: per-frame allocations in both original loops went to zero, and the minimal window demo went from 105 lines to 25. Verified by 453 checks that read pixels back rather than merely exiting cleanly
- **Two 3D demos ship in the distribution**: `3d_gl_24.obs` and `fps_gl.obs`, a first-person shooting gallery with hitscan shooting, sliding collision and controller support. `run_gl` makes setup one command on Linux and macOS
- **`Web.Server` reimplemented in pure Objeck**: the bundle shipped in every release and could not be used by anyone. Its 13 native entry points appeared in exactly one file &mdash; the binding itself &mdash; with no `.cpp`, no build target and no `libobjk_web_server` in any deploy tree; nothing set `OBJECK_LIB_WEB_SERVER`, and `Request` and `Response` declared no constructor, so an instance could not be obtained at all. The missing native library was never a possible fix: `DllProxy` takes the library name from a runtime property and the design is a per-host bridge for Nginx, IIS and Apache, whose request structures differ entirely, so a single generic library cannot exist. It is now an adapter over `Web.HTTP.Server`, which already provided a complete, tested `Request`/`Response` &mdash; same bundle, same class names, same method signatures, no native library, no new build target, no new CI leg. Coverage 0 of 13 methods to 13 of 13
- **HTTP keep-alive**: removes the Windows loopback exposure rather than hiding it, by changing which side closes rather than any socket call. No connection reuse is required for the fix
- **`obu update` and `obu rollback` on Windows**: the in-place swap needed no copy-self-and-re-exec dance &mdash; Windows forbids deleting a running image but permits renaming it. `obu check` also gained offline test coverage on every platform; it had none anywhere, because it built its own request URL instead of going through the function that honors the offline switch
- **`TTF_CloseFont` is bound**, so a font can be released at all
- **macOS ships as a notarizable `.zip`**, and the playground deploys from the release pipeline

### Bug Fixes
- **JIT truncated 64-bit immediates to 32 bits**: on AMD64 for `and`, `or`, `xor`, `add` and `sub`; on Windows ARM64 for every one of them, at the call sites rather than the emitters, because `long` is 32 bits under LLP64. Neither crashed nor diagnosed &mdash; arithmetic above 2^31 was simply wrong. Windows ARM64 had shipped untested since 2026-02-10, which is why its variant survived
- **JIT stored float compare clobbered a callee-saved register** on AMD64
- **JIT `Int[]` copy dropped the write barrier**
- **A trap-returned array held elements a minor GC could destroy**: arrays are old-generation from creation and objects bump-allocate in the nursery, so a minor collection promoted and moved the elements while repairing references only in objects the write barrier had dirtied &mdash; which a freshly filled trap array never was. Measured at 395 of 395 entries destroyed on a single collection, with the reading thread allocating nothing itself
- **A value returned by a native library could be collected** out of a reused argument buffer
- **One failed name lookup shut down Winsock for the whole process**: `WSAStartup` runs once, so the `WSACleanup` on `IPSocket::Open`'s `getaddrinfo` failure path dropped the refcount to zero and closed every open socket on every thread. Dormant only because `getaddrinfo` on a literal address does not fail
- **A short `send()` dropped the remainder of the buffer** on both platforms
- **A real HTTP 500 lost its body, and a dead socket reported one**; discarded queued response data was not closed, closed sockets read as open, and persistence framing disagreed between HTTP and HTTPS
- **A program that started a server and returned normally from `Main` segfaulted during teardown** (Windows): a thread blocked in `accept()` never observes `Halt`, so the drain timed out and `~Loader` freed the program image while that thread was still live; `WSACleanup` on the way out of `win_main` then unblocked every parked socket call process-wide and sent it executing against freed memory. All buffered output was lost with it, so a program that ran to completion looked as though it had done nothing. Linux was always clean for exactly one reason &mdash; it has no equivalent call. The image is no longer freed while a thread may still run against it, and `WaitForThreadsToDrain` now measures real elapsed time instead of counting `sleep_for(1ms)` iterations as 1ms each, which on Windows' ~15.6ms timer granularity turned a nominal 2000ms budget into 20&ndash;30 seconds of hang on every exit with a live thread
- **The debugger had the same defect, and there it was not Windows-only**: `obd` hosts the debuggee's VM in-process, and `ClearProgram` ran after every single run &mdash; freeing the bytecode image, the `StackProgram`, and the whole GC heap, since `MemoryManager::Clear` releases the young region, every old-generation object and the locks the VM takes on the way in. It called neither `HaltAllExcept` nor `WaitForThreadsToDrain`; the strings did not appear anywhere under `core/debugger`. Because `obd` outlives the run and returns to its prompt, an ordinary client connecting to the port the parked thread sits on wakes it with no `WSACleanup` involved &mdash; 6 access violations in 6 runs on Windows and 2 in 2 on Linux, against 8 in 8 for the DAP session-exit shape, which was Windows-only. The drain would also have lied had it been called: it reports "drained" when at most one interpreter is registered, and `obd`, unlike `obr`, never registered its own
- **`obd` could not debug any multithreaded program**: compile one with `-debug` &mdash; which is how a program you intend to debug is compiled &mdash; and it segfaulted on the first instruction the spawned thread executed, before that thread produced a line of output. `StackInterpreter::debugger` was set by exactly one constructor, and spawned VM threads use the default one, which left the member indeterminate while `Execute`'s hot loop called through it on every instruction. It read as a `-debug` problem rather than a threading one because the hook touches nothing until a line number exists, and without symbols every line number is -1. `_DEBUGGER` is defined only for `obd`, so `obr` was never affected and the VM regression suite could not have caught it. Breakpoints and stepping still do not reach inside spawned threads, but they run
- **A failed library load on Windows said nothing about why**
- **Windows ARM64 shipped without its runtimes**: no C++ redistributable, because `VCToolsRedistDir` is empty on the ARM64 runner, and OpenCV without its image codecs. Nothing checked either. Also fixed: the 7-Zip probe stored a quoted path and then re-quoted it, and the vcpkg root probe missed `~/vcpkg` while vcpkg's zlib shadowed the vendored one
- **mbedTLS and nghttp2 came from stale committed blobs**, not vcpkg
- **SDL2 failed to load on Windows without a hand-set `PATH`**: the DLLs shipped in `lib/sdl`, but Windows resolves a dynamically-loaded library's imports against the executable's directory, never the directory holding the library. Every SDL program was affected, and the regression runner hid it by prepending the directory before running the tests. macOS now bundles SDL2 in the distribution instead of asking users to install it
- **ONNX aborted the process when destroying its `Env` at exit** on macOS
- **macOS notarization had been failing on every release, silently**
- **The doc parser was reading prose as code**: the word "bundle" in a comment or string literal re-filed every class after it under a bundle named for the next word. Five files hard-code the library list and had drifted apart &mdash; one short two libraries while still naming a deleted third, so the counts matched and nothing looked wrong; a CI check now set-diffs all five against the sources
- **Game.OpenGL defects found by review rather than by use**: props parented into a hierarchy were culled and sorted in local rather than world space, the instanced shader never wrote `world_position`, textures rendered upside down, the projection went stale on resize, an accidentally-public API surface was narrowed, and `run_gl` never passed `gl_model` its model file

### Performance
- **Native call names are no longer allocated on every call**: a string literal allocates on each evaluation, and the literal naming the native function was 92% of a call's cost &mdash; 1655ns to 130ns. Guarded by `tools/cicd/hoist_native_names.py --check` so it cannot drift back
- **Resolved native entry points are cached, on every platform**: every `EXT_LIB_FUNC_CALL` converted the function's name from wide to narrow and then asked the loader for its address &mdash; `GetProcAddress` walking a DLL's export table, `dlsym` walking the module's symbol hash &mdash; on every call, for a name that never changes. Measured at 110ns per call, 69 in the lookup and 39 in the conversion feeding it, or about 5.5% of a native call. The cache is thread-local rather than shared behind a lock, since an uncontended mutex would hand back a useful fraction of what it saves; invalidation is a generation counter, so a library unloaded and another loaded at the same address cannot be called through a stale pointer
- **Game.OpenGL holds constant strings instead of rebuilding them per call**, and reuses native-call argument buffers on the per-object paths

### Testing
- **125 of 168 assertion-bearing tests printed FAIL and exited 0**, so the runner scored them as passes; the suite scores by exit code, and two tests were failing silently inside a green run
- **The ONNX and LAME bindings had never executed on any platform**; both now have runtime tests
- **A regression test for the VM teardown crash** that fails against the unfixed VM rather than merely passing against the fixed one, and a standalone reproduction for the Windows send-then-close data loss
- **Debugger teardown coverage in both shapes**, CLI and DAP, reusing the VM reproducer rather than duplicating it. It reports 2&ndash;4 failures on 3 runs out of 3 against a build with the fix reverted, and deliberately never asks `obd` to read a second command: the command loop repeats the previous one on an empty read, so a `readline()` that returns rather than blocks on a pipe would re-run the program forever instead of quitting

### Infrastructure
- The Windows ARM64 CI leg could not run a single thing it built; `dumpbin` is now located outside the ARM64 `PATH`, and the runner is asked what the OpenCV DLLs actually import
- A crashing macOS regression test reached the log as one frameless line; the real backtrace is now collected into the failure artifact
- Release monitoring and post-publish are two tested scripts rather than prose, and the release skill no longer contradicts itself on signing &mdash; the certificate is on a hardware token, so CI signing is impossible rather than merely unconfigured
- `release-drafter` stopped advertising a macOS Intel build that has never existed
- The native-interface guide corrected two claims that were not true, and now states the generational rule library authors actually need: everything a native library allocates is old-generation, and why

## [v2026.8.3] - 2026-08-19

A full static-analysis pass. Coverity Scan now runs on Windows as well as Linux, and this release works through both reports: crashes reachable from library input, two hangs, a class of stream-format leaks, and the unguarded entry points behind every “terminated with no message”. Alongside it, the Windows installer signing pipeline was corrected — it had been reporting signatures it never produced.

### Bug Fixes
- **Compiler crash on library input**: `TypeParser::ParseType` and `ParseParameters` switch on the first character of a type string with no default case, so anything outside `l/b/i/f/c/n/m/o` left `type` null and fell straight into `SetGenerics`/`SetDimension`. An **empty** string reaches this too &mdash; `operator[](0)` on an empty `wstring` yields a null character. Both are called from `linker.cpp`/`linker.h` on type strings read out of `.obl` files, so a corrupt or crafted library crashed the compiler. `ParseParameters` already guarded the `SetDimension` dereference, which is how the `SetGenerics` one above it was missed. `ParseGenerics` is still called when `type` is null so the index advances exactly as before &mdash; only the assignment is skipped
- **REPL hang on an unmatched brace**: `Document::List` and `Document::Save` both did `ident_count--` on a `size_t` when a line starts with `}`. With the count already `0` that wraps to `SIZE_MAX` and the indent loop below runs about 1.8e19 times. A buffer whose first non-empty line is `}` was enough to hang the REPL, on listing and on save
- **Lambda silently dropped on a signature collision**: `AddMethod` returns false on a collision and `BuildLambdaFunction` ignored the result, so a colliding lambda was never registered while the code continued to `EncodeSignature` and `AssociateMethod` on it &mdash; with no diagnostic at all. `ParseAnonymousClass` now checks the same return for consistency, so a future change to its `HasDefaultNew()` guard cannot lose the method silently
- **Indeterminate `func_ref_unwrap`**: four of `MethodCall`'s five constructors null it; the `New` constructor did not. `IntermediateEmitter` tests it with `if(func_ref_unwrap)` and emits through it, so non-null garbage meant emitting a bogus call
- **Debug session lost to one malformed request**: only `json::parse` was guarded, so a message that parsed but carried unexpected types reached the handlers, threw from inside nlohmann, unwound out of `Run()` to a `main()` with no handler, and terminated the process. Dispatch is now wrapped: parse errors are dropped as before (there is no `seq` to answer), other JSON errors get a DAP error response, and `main()` keeps a backstop for the clean exit code
- **DAP flags read without a lock**: seven flags written under `mtx` by the DAP thread were read unlocked by the VM thread at every instruction. Beyond tearing, nothing stopped this `-O3 -flto` build hoisting those loads out of the dispatch loop, so a disconnect or step request could go unseen indefinitely. All seven are `std::atomic<bool>` now &mdash; including `break_on_exception`, which Coverity never flagged because its writes never hold `mtx` at all, leaving the least protected flag the one that went unreported
- **Undecodable console line re-ran the previous command**: `ReadLine()` called `BytesToUnicode` unchecked, and it appends rather than assigns while leaving prior contents in place on failure &mdash; so an undecodable line silently handed back and re-executed the last command. It now clears up front and drops undecodable input
- **Connect that never completed reported success**: `getsockopt(SO_ERROR)` was unchecked on both the POSIX and Windows connect-with-timeout paths, so on failure `err` stayed `0`. Both paths could also hand back a socket still in non-blocking mode, giving a caller expecting a blocking read a spurious `EAGAIN`/`WSAEWOULDBLOCK` instead of data; the POSIX case was worse, because an unchecked `F_GETFL` made `flags` `-1` and that value was then restored onto the socket. `SO_REUSEADDR` and the two `SO_RCVTIMEO` defaults are now checked as well, deliberately non-fatal with the consequence recorded. Windows has no `F_GETFL` equivalent to get wrong &mdash; `ioctlsocket` takes an explicit mode &mdash; so it carried the same six defects minus that root cause
- **Editor hang on a child that had not written**: the same unchecked-`F_GETFL` shape in `child_run.h` left `read_fd` blocking, stalling the poll loop inside `read()`. The failure path now also reaps the already-forked child rather than leaving a zombie writing into a closed pipe
- **ONNX leaked `std::fixed` onto every later float**: both generation reports applied `std::fixed` with `std::setprecision(1)` directly to `std::wcout` and never restored it. This is user-visible, not cosmetic &mdash; the VM's `StdOutFloatPer` sets the stream's precision but never its floatfield, so nothing on the Objeck side could clear it, and every float printed after one SLM or vision call rendered in fixed notation at one decimal for the rest of the run. Both sites now format through a local `wostringstream`, so `wcout`'s state is never touched and there is no save/restore to get wrong
- **`StdErrFloat` saved and restored the wrong streams**: it read the saved format state from `std::wcout`, modified `std::wcerr`, and restored onto `std::cout` &mdash; three different objects. `wcerr` therefore kept the fixed/scientific/precision setting permanently while narrow `std::cout` had its flags overwritten
- **Exceptions escaping `main`**: every entry point had a large unprotected prologue &mdash; `obi` (all 89 lines) and the VM's POSIX main (all 126) had no handler at all, and the three that did started their `try` well after the opening brace. That prologue is where the throws are: codecvt/locale construction in the REPL, and the long `usage += ...` `wstring` building in every tool, which is a `bad_alloc` path. Each `main` is now a thin wrapper around a static `objeck_main`. **This introduced and then fixed a POSIX segfault**: the VM's POSIX branch deliberately discards `Execute`'s result and fell off the end of the function, which is an implicit `return 0` in `main` but undefined behaviour in an ordinary function &mdash; at `-O3` no return instruction was emitted and control ran into garbage. Windows structurally could not catch it, since it takes the returning branch above
- **Negative `WriteBytes` return converted to `size_t`**: `SockDtlsOutByteAry` and `SockDtlsOutCharAry` pushed `IPDtlsSocket::WriteBytes`' return straight into `PushInt(size_t, ...)`, so the `-1` failure code reached the op stack through an implicit signed-to-unsigned conversion. It read back as `-1` on the Objeck side because the conversion sign-extends, but nothing in the code said that was intended
- **Unvalidated `/proc` arithmetic**: `GetProcessResidentBytes` computed `pages * sysconf(_SC_PAGESIZE)` from an `fscanf` of `/proc/self/statm` &mdash; a negative page count casts to a huge `size_t`, the multiply can wrap, and `sysconf` returns `-1` on error, which casts to `SIZE_MAX`. All three are checked, and the failure path leaves `rss` at `0` exactly as it already did
- **Malformed terminal escape sequences**: bytes outside the ECMA-48 parameter ranges could be stored as negative characters before being fed to `atoi`; they are now rejected the same way a short read already was

### Performance
- **Move instead of copy in the REPL and compiler entry points**: clears eight `COPY_INSTEAD_OF_MOVE` findings. Stated honestly &mdash; these run once at startup on short strings, so the runtime saving is nil; the value is a clean baseline so a real defect in these files stands out in the next report. Each move was checked for a later use rather than applied mechanically: `compiler/posix_main.cpp`'s `usage` **does** appear 22 lines later, in the mutually exclusive `else` branch, and a mechanical application of the checker's advice would have needed exactly that check to be safe

### Concurrency
- **`free_memory_cache_size` is atomic**: mutated only under `free_memory_cache_lock` but read without it by the sweep and zeroed unlocked in `Initialize`. Now `std::atomic<size_t>`, relaxed throughout &mdash; the lock still orders the mutations, and the sweep's use is a heuristic threshold that tolerates a value one cycle stale
- **Sweep/mark lock hierarchy recorded**: the reported `ORDER_REVERSAL` between `allocated_lock` and `pda_monitor_lock` cannot fire today &mdash; `CheckPdaRoots` is joined before the sweep takes its lock, the JIT thread it spawns is joined inside it, and `marked_sweep_lock` admits one collector at a time. But the safety rested on four unstated invariants, and overlapping sweep with marking &mdash; the natural next step for this collector &mdash; makes the ABBA live and hangs the VM. The hierarchy is now documented at both lock sites
- **`half_to_float_u16` rewritten with a signed exponent**: the subnormal path relied on a `uint32_t` underflow wrapping back to exactly the right exponent field. Bit-identical after the change, verified by an exhaustive sweep of all 65,536 half patterns against an independent `ldexp` reference, but the correctness no longer rests on a wrap the reader has to reconstruct

### Release and Infrastructure
- **Windows installers are signed, and the notes say so only when true**: every Windows MSI from v2026.4.0 through v2026.8.2 shipped **unsigned** while the generated notes asserted “All Windows MSI installers are digitally signed for security”. This was not a missing secret &mdash; `signtool` was configured, ran, and failed on every artifact, after which the build exited 0 by design and the publish workflow tolerated it. A false security claim survived a fully green pipeline for months. The signing certificate lives on a SafeNet eToken, so its private key is non-exportable and a GitHub-hosted runner has no USB port: CI signing is not broken, it is impossible. CI no longer attempts it, the generated notes no longer assert it, and `check_release_signatures.ps1` reports the real `Get-AuthenticodeSignature` status per installer &mdash; alerting, printing the exact `sign_release.cmd` invocation, and exiting non-zero when any installer is unsigned. It also warns when a signature is valid but **not timestamped**, since such a signature dies with the certificate rather than outliving it
- **Signing selects the certificate by thumbprint, not `/a`**: `/a` picks the “best” valid certificate, and the signing machine's store holds three valid code-signing certs with private keys. It was safe while the token was the only candidate; picking a self-signed cert would produce installers that carry a signature and fail every trust check &mdash; worse than shipping them unsigned
- **Coverity Scan on Windows**: the first Windows scan captured the whole solution and found twenty real defects, most of them in cross-platform sources that MSVC compiles differently than GCC, so the Linux scan had never reached them. The scan token is read from the environment rather than the repository
- **`linux-arm64` is smoke tested**: the POSIX smoke test excluded it on the assumption it was cross-compiled. It is not &mdash; the matrix builds it on `ubuntu-24.04-arm`, which runs the binaries it produces &mdash; so the shipped `linux-arm64` toolchain was the one platform never exercised before publication. Windows ARM64 is the only genuinely cross-compiled target and stays excluded

## [v2026.8.2] - 2026-08-16

Supersedes v2026.8.0 and v2026.8.1, which were withdrawn; this entry covers everything since v2026.6.4.

### New Features
- **Subscript the result of a method call**: `GetItems()[0]->GetName()` now works. It had never been implemented, and the grammar swallowed the attempt rather than rejecting it &mdash; `[` after a call was read as the start of a fresh static-array literal, so the call was evaluated and discarded. `GetArr()[0]->Size()` silently returned `1`, the size of the literal `[0]`, and `GetArr()[0]->PrintLine()` printed `[0]`; with an object-typed array the same root cause surfaced instead as "Undefined function/method call". The silent half is the dangerous one, since a wrong value is far harder to notice than a build failure. Implemented across the parser (all three call-parsing paths), the context analyzer (element-type resolution, index analysis, rejection of non-integer and multi-dimensional subscripts) and the emitter. **Behaviour change:** code that previously compiled and returned the wrong value now returns the correct one.
- **`AddHeader` now actually sends headers on HTTP/2 and HTTP/3**: `Http2Client->AddHeader` and `Http3Client->AddHeader` stored headers on the Objeck side and then dropped them &mdash; the trap signature had no headers argument, so they never reached the VM, and both native contexts carried a `request_headers` map the backends already consumed and nothing ever wrote. Headers now cross as a `String[]` of alternating key/value through new `HTTP2_REQUEST_HDRS` / `HTTP3_REQUEST_HDRS` traps and a 5-argument `Request` overload. The existing 4-argument trap is deliberately **untouched**: the `TRAP` operand encodes the argument count, only the JIT reads it, and CI does not rebuild `lang.obl` &mdash; so widening it would have desynced the operand stack silently, and only once the JIT threshold tripped. Verified by a server echoing the value back, not by a status code. HTTP/1.1 was never affected: it serializes headers in Objeck.
- **HTTP/3 on Windows**: `Http3Client` now works on Windows 11 / Server 2022 and later, backed by **WinHTTP over MsQuic**. It had shipped documented-and-dead: the trap handlers compile unconditionally, so the API was present and every request simply failed. The POSIX ngtcp2/GnuTLS path is untouched &mdash; none of it exists in the Windows toolchain &mdash; so this adds no vendored QUIC library, no second TLS backend and no new DLL to deploy (`winhttp.lib` ships with Windows). WinHTTP treats HTTP/3 as a *preference* and falls back transparently, so every request asserts `WINHTTP_OPTION_HTTP_PROTOCOL_USED` and **fails rather than silently downgrading** &mdash; a client that quietly spoke HTTP/2 would be the same false promise this fixes. Verified live against four HTTP/3 hosts (GET, POST with body, custom headers, session reuse) and negatively against two HTTP/1.1-only hosts, which are correctly refused.
- **`runtime.feature.http3` / `runtime.feature.http2`**: report which protocol engines were actually compiled in. Because the traps compile unconditionally, "the API exists" proved nothing, and callers could not tell *not supported* from *the network failed* &mdash; which is how the Windows gap went unnoticed. `Runtime->GetProperty("runtime.feature.http3")` returns `"1"` only when an engine is really present.
- **`obu` updater, phase 2**: `obu update` and `obu rollback` on Linux/macOS. It downloads the platform archive and its `SHA256SUMS`, verifies the digest with an inline SHA-256 **before touching disk**, then does an all-or-nothing staged swap (`.obu-work` &rarr; `.previous` &rarr; root) with an `obc -v` post-install check and automatic rollback. No shell is used for the download, extract or swap &mdash; curl/tar/obc run via argument vectors &mdash; and a per-run lock plus interrupted-swap recovery guard against corruption. Covered by an offline CI test (`test_update.sh`). Windows in-place swap and signature verification are later phases.
- **Full-screen editor in `obi`** (`/e`): raw-mode terminal editing over the REPL buffer with damage-diffed rendering, CJK/tab-correct widths and a protected read-only shell. **F5 compiles and runs the buffer as a subprocess**, streaming its merged output into a pane without blocking the UI and **F8 jumping the cursor through compile errors**; undo/redo with coalesced typing runs, Shift-selection, an internal clipboard, and an opt-in **vi profile** (F2: hjkl, i/a/o, dd/yy/gg, visual mode). `/et` opens a terminal capability test. Header-only implementation; no build-system changes on any platform.
- **`obu` updater, phase 1**: `obu check` compares the installed version against the latest GitHub release (or a pinned `--channel <tag>`), exit 0/1/2 with `--quiet` for scripting, using the system curl with no new dependencies. Every future release now ships a **`SHA256SUMS`** asset generated over the exact released file set, and a weekly workflow snapshots per-asset download counts into a committed CSV. Design in `docs/UPDATER_DESIGN.md`.
- **Debugger: structured variable inspection**: every variable was previously reported with `variablesReference` 0, so an object, array or collection was a dead end in the Variables pane &mdash; a `Map` displayed as `Collection.Map@0x1f4a2c0` and nothing more. Objects now expand into their instance fields, arrays into indexed elements, and `Vector`, `Map` and `Hash` into their contents (`Map` in key order, `Hash` as key &rarr; value), each child expanding recursively. Strings and boxed scalars (`IntRef`, `FloatRef`, &hellip;) render as their value and are deliberately terminal, since drilling into them would only expose backing storage. Expansion handles hold raw object pointers, so they are discarded on every resume and on each new stop &mdash; the collector moves objects, and a handle must never outlive the stop that produced it. Depth, child-count and cycle guards keep a self-referencing structure from expanding forever. Collection internals are located by field name where debug symbols exist and by a validated slot index otherwise, because library classes are compiled without symbols and carry no declaration names.
- **Debugger: watch and hover expressions expand**: `evaluate` returned `variablesReference` 0, so a watch on a collection dead-ended even though the Variables pane could expand it. Expressions resolving to an object or array now hand back an expandable reference, backed by a new typed evaluation entry point (`EvaluateForDapRaw`) &mdash; the existing evaluator returned only a formatted string, with no value to attach a handle to.
- **Debugger: wider DAP protocol coverage** (8 &rarr; 17 capabilities): `terminate` (the graceful stop clients prefer over `disconnect`), `breakpointLocations` (reports only lines that carry an instruction, so editors stop offering breakpoints that cannot bind &mdash; permissive until a program is loaded, since nothing can be validated before then), `exceptionInfo`, `setExpression`, `completions` (debug-console completion over the variables visible in the selected frame), `modules` and `loadedSources`. **Data breakpoints** break when a value changes rather than when a line is reached; the watch machinery already existed for the CLI `watch` command but reported only to the console and could not be created over DAP, so the stop now names the watch and reports its old &rarr; new transition. Variable paging is supported through `indexedVariables` plus `start`/`count`, and the client's own capabilities are now read from the initialize request instead of ignored.
- **Unsigned integer literals**: a `u`/`U` suffix reads a literal across the whole unsigned range and keeps its bit pattern, so `0xFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFu` and `18446744073709551615u` are now writable. The suffix affects only how the number is scanned &mdash; the value is an ordinary `Int`, so no new type, opcodes or conversion rules come with it. Hex and binary literals are read the same way, since they are bit-pattern notation.
- **Unsigned shift `>>>` and unsigned `Int` operations**: every integer sits in a signed 64-bit slot, so `>>` copies the sign bit into the high end &mdash; there was no way to shift a value that is being used as a bit pattern rather than as a number. `a >>> n` shifts a zero in instead, at the same precedence as `>>` and `*` and left-associative. Alongside it, `Int` gains `ShiftRightUnsigned`, `CompareUnsigned`, `DivideUnsigned`, `ModUnsigned` and `ToUnsignedString`, which read both operands as unsigned 64-bit quantities &mdash; so `-1` is `18446744073709551615` and `Int->DivideUnsigned(-1, 3)` is `6148914691236517205`. Like `?->` and `??`, `>>>` desugars in the parser onto an ordinary library call, so no opcode is added and the bytecode reader, both JIT backends and the VM are unchanged. Because `Vector<Vector<Vector<X>>>` closes three type arguments with a single `>>>` token, the parser now tracks how many `>` a multi-character close still owes rather than a single "expand" flag; two- and three-deep generics are covered by the regression suite.
- **Language server reports `serverInfo`**: the initialize response carried no server name or version, so clients had nothing to display and a stale deployed server was invisible. `codeActionProvider` now also declares its `codeActionKinds` (`quickfix`, `source.organizeImports`) so clients can filter them and route "Organize Imports" to the server, and `signatureHelpProvider` declares `retriggerCharacters` to keep the signature popup alive while arguments are edited.
- **Nil-safe operators `?->` and `??`**: `a?->b()` calls `b` only when `a` is non-`Nil`, yielding `Nil` instead of faulting, and `a ?? b` supplies `b` when `a` is `Nil` (evaluating `b` only when needed). A single `?->` guards the whole remainder of a chain, so `maybe?->Trim()->ToUpper()` is safe throughout. Both desugar in the parser onto the existing `Try()`/`Otherwise()` intrinsics — `a?->b()` is `a->Try()->b()` and `a ?? b` is `a->Otherwise(b)` — so no new opcode is emitted and the bytecode reader, both JIT backends, and the VM are unchanged. A chain ending in a value type needs `??` to supply the result, since an `Int` cannot itself be `Nil`: `maybe?->ToUpper()->Size() ?? -1`. Note that `Try()` guards against any runtime error in the chain, not only a `Nil` dereference. The spelling is `?->` rather than `?.` because Objeck's member accessor is `->`; it also avoids the float-literal ambiguity `?.` would introduce, since `.` followed by a digit starts a number.

### Security
- **HTTP request-line injection via a URL path**: `Url->New` does not sanitize, and both the path and the host were appended verbatim into the HTTP/1.1 request line, so a CR/LF split the request line itself. Proven against a local listener: `Url->New("http://host/evil
- **Header injection via caller-supplied content types (HTTP/1.1 and HTTPS)**: the same flaw as the HTTP/2 and HTTP/3 case below, on the text-serialized path. `net.obs` and `net_secure.obs` appended the caller's content type verbatim at three sites, reachable from fifteen public methods, and the injected line landed **before `Content-Length`** &mdash; the request-smuggling position. Validated and failing closed at each serialization site.
- **HTTP request splitting via `AddHeader` (HTTP/1.1 and HTTPS)**: `net.obs` and `net_secure.obs` serialize the request as text in Objeck and appended caller-supplied header values verbatim at seven sites, so a CR/LF ended the field and began a new one. Demonstrated against a local listener, which received `X-Injected: yes` as a header of its own. Unlike the HTTP/2 and HTTP/3 `AddHeader` &mdash; inert because headers never reached the VM &mdash; this one always worked, and so had always been injectable. Adds `Web.HTTP.HeaderCheck`, guarding all six `AddHeader` methods per RFC 9110 5.1/5.5 as tightened by RFC 9113 8.2.1; it also rejects names beginning with `:`, since a caller-supplied pseudo-header is malformed and is the classic smuggling primitive.
- **Header injection via caller-supplied content types (HTTP/2 and HTTP/3)**: `Post`/`Put`/`Patch` passed the content type into the header serializer unvalidated. On Windows that builds a CRLF-delimited block for `WinHttpAddRequestHeaders`, whose input *is* a CRLF-separated header block &mdash; so `"text/plain
- **HTTP/3 connection IDs could be uninitialised stack memory**: the five `gnutls_rnd` calls in the QUIC client ignored their return value, and the initial connection IDs were declared uninitialised. `gnutls_rnd` leaves the buffer untouched on failure, so a CSPRNG failure transmitted stack contents in the cleartext QUIC Initial header — a memory disclosure plus predictable connection IDs, and a forgeable stateless-reset token. All sites now check, the IDs are zero-initialised, connection setup fails closed, and the callback that cannot report failure returns `NGTCP2_ERR_CALLBACK_FAILURE`. Secrets now draw from `GNUTLS_RND_KEY`.

### Bug Fixes
